## 六、机器学习的能力与局限——AI不是魔法，一个实用的判断法则是"你一秒钟内能做出的判断，AI大概也能做"，但它搞不定股票预测这种高度随机的事情

### 在实际承诺做一个AI项目之前，吴恩达通常会自己或让工程师做"技术尽职调查"——仔细看数据、看A和B的输入输出，认真判断这件事AI到底能不能做；问题在于，有些CEO对AI期望过高，会让工程师去做今天AI根本做不到的事情；而媒体和学术文献又只报道成功故事，我们只看到一串成功案例，看不到失败案例，导致有些人以为AI什么都能做——这当然不是真的

> 在这一集和下一集中，我希望帮你建立关于AI能做什么和不能做什么的直觉。实际上，在我承诺做一个具体的AI项目之前，我通常会自己或让工程师对这个项目做技术尽职调查，确保它是可行的。这意味着仔细看数据、看输入A和输出B，认真思考这件事AI到底能不能做。不幸的是，我见过一些CEO对AI抱有过高的期望，让工程师去做今天AI根本做不到的事。一个挑战是，媒体和学术文献往往只报道AI的正面结果或成功故事。我们看到一串成功案例，却看不到失败案例。人们有时候就觉得AI什么都能做。但不幸的是，这根本不是真的。所以在这集和下集视频中，我想给你看一些例子——今天AI技术能做什么，以及它不能做什么。我希望这能帮你磨练直觉，判断哪些项目对你的公司来说更有希望、哪些可能不太靠谱。

> In this video and the next video, I hope to help you develop intuition about what AI can and cannot do. In practice, before I commit to a specific AI project, I usually have either myself or engineers do technical diligence on a project to make sure that it is feasible. This means looking at the data, looking at the input and output A and B, and just thinking through if this is something AI can really do. What I've seen unfortunately is that some CEOs can have an overinflated expectation of AI and can ask engineers to do things that today's AI just cannot do. One of the challenges is that the media, as well as academic literature, tends to only report on positive results or success stories using AI. And we see a string of success stories and no failure stories. People sometimes think AI can do everything. And unfortunately, that's just not true. So what I want to do in this and the next video is to show you a few examples of what today's AI technology can do, but also what it cannot do. And I hope that this will help you hone your intuition about what might be more or less promising projects to select for your company.

### 一个不太完美但很实用的经验法则：几乎所有你花不到一秒钟思考就能做的事情，现在或不久的将来，大概率可以用监督学习的A到B映射来自动化——比如判断其他车辆的位置是一秒内能做的事，看手机有没有划痕是一秒内能做的事，听懂别人说了什么也就几秒钟的思考量；这个法则不完美，但能帮你快速筛选出AI系统可能胜任的任务

> 之前你看到了AI应用的那个列表——从垃圾邮件过滤到语音识别到机器翻译等等。有一个不太完美但很实用的经验法则，可以用来判断监督学习大概能做什么、不能做什么：几乎所有你花不到一秒钟思考就能做的事，现在或不久的将来，大概都可以用监督学习——也就是输入到输出的映射——来自动化。所以，判断其他车辆的位置，是你不到一秒就能做的事。判断手机有没有划痕，看一眼就能判断，用不了一秒。理解——至少是转录——别人说了什么，也用不了几秒钟思考。虽然这是个不完美的经验法则，但它也许能帮你快速想出一些AI系统能做哪些任务的例子。

> Previously, you saw this list of AI applications, from spam protein to speech recognition to machine translation, and so on. One imperfect rule of thumb you can use to decide what supervised learning may or may not be able to do is that pretty much anything you could do with a second of thought, you can probably now or soon automate using supervised learning, using this input-output mapping. So for example, in order to determine the position of other cars, that's something that you can do with less than a second. In order to tell if a phone is scratched, you can look at it and you can kind of tell in less than a second. In order to understand, at least transcribe what was said, you know, doesn't take that many seconds of thought. And while this is an imperfect rule of thumb, it maybe gives you a way to quickly think of some examples of tasks that AI systems can do.

### 反之，预测股市就是今天AI做不到的事——仅凭一只股票的历史价格（A）去预测未来价格（B），这是人类一秒钟做不到、甚至花更长时间也做不到的事，机器学习也很难做到；如果你硬要用机器学习，简单算法可能拟合出一条直线——但根据你选的时间窗口不同，红色线和蓝色线给出的结果会天差地别；根本问题是：股票的历史价格对未来价格的预测能力非常低，AI无法准确预测如此随机的东西

> 相比之下，今天AI做不到的事情，一个例子就是预测股市——比如仅凭一只股票的历史价格去预测未来的价格。这是一件人类大概不可能一秒钟做到、甚至超过一秒钟也做不到的事，而且大概也不可能让机器学习准确地做到。我们来仔细看看。假设我们要处理的任务是：给定一只股票近期的价格——这是输入A——预测未来某个时间点、比如一个月后的价格——这是输出B。如果你对这个问题应用机器学习会发生什么？一个简单的算法可能会尝试拟合一条直线穿过数据，但取决于你选择哪个时间段去拟合，你可能会得到这样的红色线，或者如果你选择这个窄窗口拟合，可能会得到这条蓝色线。所以根据实现细节的不同，这个算法可能会给出天差地别的输出值。这个应用的主要问题是：股票过去的价格历史对未来股价的预测能力非常低——这就是为什么用机器学习做这件事的尝试一直没有成功。未来股价的随机性太强了，AI很难准确预测。顺便说一句，为了完整性我需要补充：仅凭同一只股票的历史价格来预测股价似乎是不可能做到的。但确实有些股票交易者有时候会找到其他输入——比如，如果他们合法获取了某些网络流量或客流量数据，帮他们估算出公司销售额，那么这些数据结合历史价格数据，也许能让算法有一些预测能力。然而，这些额外的输入通常很复杂或成本很高，而且仍然无法克服股市本身固有的随机性。

> Whereas in contrast, something that AI today cannot do would be actually predicting the stock market. Say, predict the future price of some company stock given only the historical price of that stock. It's a thing that a person probably can't do in one second or even longer than a second and it's probably not possible to get machine learning to do this accurately either. Let's look in greater detail at this. Say the task we want to tackle is, given the recent price of a stock, that's the input A, predict the price at a future point in time, say a month into the future, that's the output B. What would happen if you were to apply machine learning to this? Well, a simpler algorithm might try to fit a straight line to the data, but depending on what period of time you fit the data to, you might get a red line like this, or if you fit it to this narrow window, you might get this blue line. So depending on the details of the implementation, this particular algorithm might give wildly varying output values. The main problem with this application is that the past history of a stock price is just not very predictive of the future stock price, which is why attempts to use machine learning this way haven't been successful. Future stock prices are so random that it's just hard for AI to predict it accurately. By the way, for completeness, I should say that predicting stock price based only on the historical price of the same stock seems to be impossible, but there are stock traders that sometimes find other inputs. For example, if they manage to legally obtain some web traffic or foot traffic data that helps them estimate what the company's sales were, then that, in combination with the historical price data, might make it possible for the algorithm to have some predictive power. However, these other inputs are typically complex or costly to acquire and still can't overcome the intrinsically somewhat random nature of the stock market.

### 帮你在筛选项目时快速判断可行性的另外两条经验法则：第一，学习"简单概念"更可能成功——虽然没有正式定义，但如果一件事你只需要不到一秒或几秒钟就能得出结论，那就可以认为是个简单概念，比如自动驾驶里识别前方车辆是个相对简单的概念，而想出一个聪明信号去预测某公司销售额就不是；第二，有大量数据（包括输入A和输出B都有的数据）的项目更可能成功——比如判断手机有没有划痕，如果你有几千张手机照片同时标注了A（图片）和B（有没有划痕），那做出一个高准确率的检测系统就大概率可行

> 为了进一步帮你磨练快速筛选可行/不可行项目的直觉，这里还有另外两条经验法则，关于什么让一个机器学习问题更容易或更可能可行。第一：学习简单概念更可能可行。但什么叫"简单概念"？这没有正式定义，但如果一件事你只需要花不到一秒钟、或者很少几秒钟的脑力思考就能得出结论，那这就暗示它可能是个简单概念。所以，如果你从自动驾驶车辆的窗外往外看，去识别其他车——这看起来是个相对简单的概念。相比之下，想出一个聪明信号去预测某公司的销售额——这看起来就不那么像一个简单概念。第二：如果你有大量可用数据，机器学习问题就更可能可行。这里说的数据既包括输入A也包括输出B——也就是你在A到B、输入到输出映射中需要AI系统具备的两样东西。所以，举个例子，判断手机有没有划痕这个任务中，输入A是一组手机图片，输出B是标注手机是否被划伤的标签。如果你有几千张手机图片，同时有A和B，那你做出一个高准确率划痕检测机器学习系统的概率就会高很多。

> To hold your intuition about how to quickly filter feasible or not feasible projects, here are a couple of other rules of thumb about what makes a machine learning problem easier or more likely to be feasible. One, learning a simple concept is more likely to be feasible, but what does it mean for something to be a simple concept? There's no formal definition of it, but if it is something that takes you less than a second of mental thought, or maybe just a very small number of seconds of mental thought to come up with a conclusion, then that would be suggestive of it being a simple concept. So, if you're looking outside the window of a self-driving car to spot the other cars, well, that would seem like a relatively simple concept. Whereas in contrast, trying to come up with clever signals to predict a given company's sales, well, that seems like less of a simple concept. Second, a machine learning problem is more likely to be feasible if you have lots of data available. Here, data means both the input A and the output B that you want the AI system to have in your A to B or input output mapping. So for example, in trying to determine whether a phone is scratched or not, the input A would be a set of images of phones, and the output B could be a label identifying the phone as being scratched or not scratched. Then if you have thousands of pictures of phones with both A and B, the odds of you building a machine learning system to detect scratches accurately would be much higher.

### AI是新的电力，正在变革每个行业——但它不是魔法，不是什么事都能做；希望这集开始帮你建立关于AI能做什么、不能做什么的直觉，提高你选出可行且有价值的项目的概率；下一集我会继续给你看更多具体例子，进一步强化你的判断力

> AI是新的电力，正在变革每个行业——但它不是魔法，我们做不了天底下所有事。希望这集视频开始帮你磨练关于AI能做什么、不能做什么的直觉，提高你为团队选出可行且有价值的项目的概率。为了帮你继续发展这种直觉，我想给你看更多AI能做什么和不能做什么的例子。咱们进入下个视频。

> AI is the new electricity and is transforming every industry, but it's also not magic and we can't do everything under the sun. I hope that this video started to help you hone your intuitions about whether AI can or cannot do and increase the odds of your selecting feasible and valuable projects for maybe your teams to try working on. In order to help you continue developing your intuition, I would like to show you more examples of what AI can and cannot do. Let's go on to the next video.


---

## 七、更多机器学习示例——AI能检测其他车辆但看不懂人的手势，能从X光片诊断肺炎但没法像人一样读教科书学知识，而且它对训练数据之外的新类型数据适应能力远远不如人类

### 想要培养判断AI能做什么、不能做什么的能力，需要看足够多的成功和失败案例；如果你平均一年做一个AI项目，看三个案例要花三年工作经验——太慢了；这两集的目标就是在短时间内给你看多个具体案例，帮你快速建立直觉选出有价值的项目

> 要想擅长识别AI能做什么和不能做什么，确实需要看到一些AI成功和失败的具体案例。如果你平均一年做一个新的AI项目，那看三个例子就要花你三年的工作经验——这时间太长了。我在上一集和这集的目标，就是快速给你展示几个AI成功和失败的例子——也就是它能做什么和不能做什么——让你在更短的时间内看到多个具体案例，帮你磨练直觉、选出有价值的项目。来看看更多例子。

> One of the challenges of becoming good at recognizing what AI can and cannot do is that it does take seeing a few examples of concrete successes and failures of AI. And if you work on an average of say one new AI project per year, then to see three examples will take you three years of work experience and that's just a long time. What I hope to do both in the previous video and in this video is to quickly show you a few examples of AI successes and failures or what it can and cannot do so that in a much shorter time you can see multiple concrete examples to help hone your intuition and select valuable projects. So let's take a look at a few more examples.

### 自动驾驶中AI能做到的：输入一张车前方的照片（A）——可能来自摄像头、也可能结合雷达或激光雷达等传感器——输出其他车辆的位置（B）；今天的自动驾驶行业已经知道怎么收集足够的数据，也有相当不错的算法来做好这件事；但AI做不到的是：输入一段视频（A），理解一个人在对你的车做什么手势（B）——比如建筑工人举手示意你停车、搭便车的人在挥手拦车、骑自行车的人举左手表示要左转；问题在于人类的手势方式实在太多了，根本不可能收集到几千上万个人在各种不同方式下做手势的数据，而且理解手势意图本身就是一个复杂概念，连人类有时候都搞不清楚对方挥手到底想要什么

> 假设你在造一辆自动驾驶车。AI能做得很好的一件事是：拍一张你车前方的照片——可能只用摄像头，也可能结合雷达或激光雷达等其他传感器——然后判断其他车辆的位置、也就是其他车在哪里。这就是一个AI系统，输入A是你车前方的图片（或同时有图片以及雷达和其他传感器的读数），输出B是其他车辆的位置。今天自动驾驶行业已经搞明白了怎么收集足够的数据，也有相当好的算法来做好这件事。这就是今天AI能做到的。而今天AI做不到——或者至少用今天AI会非常困难——的事是：输入一张图片，输出人的意图或者这个人对你的车在做什么手势。比如这里有个建筑工人伸出手要你的车停下，这里有个搭便车的人在挥手拦车，这里有个骑自行车的人举左手表示要左转。如果你想构建一个系统来学习A到B的映射——输入A是一段人对你的车做手势的短视频，输出B是这个人的意图或想要什么——这在今天是极其困难的。部分问题在于：人向你做手势的方式多到数不清。想想看，一个人可能会用的所有手势——叫你慢下来、叫你走、叫你停——人们向你做手势的方式实在是太多太多了。所以你很难收集到足够的数据——几千甚至上万个人用各种不同方式对你做手势的数据——来捕捉人类手势的丰富性。所以从一段视频中学习这个人想要什么，其实是个相当复杂的概念。事实上，连人类自己有时候都很难判断一个向你的车挥手的人到底想要什么。其次，因为这是安全关键型应用，你会希望AI系统在判断建筑工人是想让你停还是想让你走上能极其准确——这也让AI系统更难做到。所以今天，如果你收集个一万张其他车辆的照片，很多团队都能构建一个至少具备基本其他车辆检测能力的AI系统。相比之下，即便你收集了一万个人的照片或视频——要找到一万个向你的车挥手的人就已经很难了——即便有这个数据集，我觉得今天也很难构建一个AI系统，能以安全驾驶所需的高精度来从手势中识别人的意图。这就是为什么今天很多自动驾驶团队有检测其他车辆的组件，也确实依靠这项技术来安全驾驶，但很少有自动驾驶团队会去尝试构建一个AI系统来识别大量多样化的人类手势，并仅靠这个来在人群中安全驾驶。

> Let's say you're building a self-driving car. Here's something that AI can do pretty well, which is to take a picture of what's in front of your car and maybe just using a camera, maybe using other sensors as well such as radar or LiDAR. And then to figure out what is the position or where are the other cars. So this would be an AI where the input A is a picture of what's in front of your car or maybe both a picture as well as radar and other sensor readings. And the output B is where are the other cars. And today the self-driving car industry has figured out how to collect enough data and has pretty good algorithms for doing this reasonably well. So that's what AI today can do. Here's an example of something that today's AI cannot do or at least would be very difficult using today's AI, which is to input a picture and output the intention or whatever the human is trying to gesture at your car. So here's a construction worker holding out a hand to ask your car to stop. Here's a hitchhiker trying to wave a car over. Here's a bicyclist raising the left hand to indicate that they want to turn left. And so if you were to try to build a system to learn an A to B mapping where the input A is a short video of a human gesturing at your car and the output B is what's the intention or what does this person want. That today is very difficult to do. Part of the problem is that the number of ways people gesture at you is very, very large. Imagine all the hand gestures someone could conceivably use, ask you to slow down or go or stop. The number of ways that people could gesture at you is just very, very large. And so it's difficult to collect enough data from enough thousands or tens of thousands of different people gesturing at you and all of these different ways to capture the richness of human gestures. So learning from a video to what this person wants is actually a somewhat complicated concept. In fact, even people have a hard time figuring out sometimes what someone waving at your car wants. And then second, because this is a safety critical application, you would want an AI system that is extremely accurate in terms of figuring out does the construction worker want you to stop or does he or she want you to go. And that makes it harder for an AI system as well. And so today, if you collect just say 10,000 pictures of other cars, many teams will build a built-in AI system that at least has a basic capability at detecting other cars. In contrast, even if you collect pictures or videos of 10,000 people, it's quite hard to track down 10,000 people waving at your car. Even with that data set, I think it's quite hard today to build an AI system to recognize human intention from the gestures at the very high level of accuracy needed in order to drive safely around these people. So that's why today, many self-driving car teams have some components for detecting other cars and they do rely on that technology to drive safely. But very few self-driving car teams are trying to counter an AI system to recognize a huge diversity of human gestures and counting just on that to drive safely around people.

### 医学诊断中AI能做到的：输入胸部X光片（A），输出有没有肺炎的诊断（B）——这件事AI能做到；但AI做不到的是：仅凭一本医学教科书里10张图片和几段文字来诊断肺炎——一个年轻的医学生可以看几十张图片再读几段文字就开始建立概念，但AI系统今天完全做不到这一点；因为你根本不知道给定一本教科书，该怎么定义A和B、怎么把这个任务变成一个AI问题

> 再看一个例子。假设你想构建一个AI系统来看X光图像并诊断肺炎。这些都是胸部X光片。输入A可以是X光图像，输出B可以是诊断——这个病人有没有肺炎？这件事AI能做到。AI做不到的事是：仅凭一本医学教科书里10张图片和几段关于肺炎的说明文字来诊断肺炎。人类可以看一小批图片——也许几十张——再读几段医学教科书的文字，就能开始有个大概的理解。但我真的不知道：给定一本医学教科书，什么是A什么是B？怎么把这个问题变成一个AI问题？如果你手上只有10张图片和几段解释肺炎在胸部X光片上是什么样子的文字，我真的不知道怎么写出一个软件来解决这个问题。而一个年轻的医学博士呢，读读医学教科书、看看几十张图片就能学得相当不错——相比之下，AI系统今天就是做不到这一点。

> Let's look at one more example. Say you want to build an AI system to look at x-ray images and diagnose pneumonia. So all of these are chest x-rays. So the input A could be the x-ray image and the output B can be the diagnosis. Does this patient have pneumonia or not? So that's something that AI can do. Something that AI cannot do would be to diagnose pneumonia from 10 images of a medical textbook chapter explaining pneumonia. A human can look at a small set of images, maybe just a few dozen images and read a few paragraphs from a medical textbook and start to get a sense. But I actually don't know, given a medical textbook, what is A and what is B or how to really pose this as an AI problem to let know how to write a piece of software to solve if all you have is just 10 images and a few paragraphs of text that explain what pneumonia and chest x-ray looks like. Whereas a young medical doctor might learn quite well reading a medical textbook and just looking at maybe dozens of images in contrast, an AI system isn't really able to do that today.

### 总结机器学习的强项和弱项：它擅长学习简单概念（你一秒钟内能判断的事）加上有大量数据的情况；它不擅长从少量数据中学习复杂概念；此外，AI还有一个被低估的弱点——当遇到训练数据之外的新类型数据时表现很差；比如用高质量医学中心的X光片训练出来的肺炎诊断AI，换到另一家医院——那里技师可能让病人斜着拍片，或者图像上有奇怪的划痕和异物——AI的表现就会大打折扣；相比之下，人类医生从左边那些高质量图像中学到的东西，更容易适应右边的图像——因为人能判断出"哦，病人只是躺歪了而已"，但AI系统的泛化能力远不如人类医生

> 总结一下机器学习的强项和弱项。机器学习往往在以下情况下效果很好：你学习的是一个简单概念——比如你一秒钟以内就能做出判断的事——并且有大量数据可用。机器学习往往在以下情况下效果不好：你试图从少量数据中学习一个复杂概念。AI另一个被低估的弱点是：当遇到跟训练数据中见过的数据类型不一样的新数据时，AI的表现往往很差。让我用一个例子来解释。假设你构建了一个监督学习系统，用A到B来学习从这些图片中诊断肺炎——这些是高质量的胸部X光片。但现在假设你把这个AI系统拿去另一家医院或另一个医学中心使用——那里拍X光的技师不知怎么搞的，总让病人斜着躺着拍，或者图像上有这些缺陷（不知道你能不能看到图片上那些小划痕、这些落在病人身上的小东西）。如果AI系统是从左边那些数据中学出来的——可能来自一个高质量医学中心——然后你把这个AI系统拿到另一个产生右边那种图像的医学中心去用，它的表现也会很差。一个好的AI团队能够改善或减少这些问题，但这并不容易做到。这也是AI比人类弱很多的地方之一——如果一个人从左侧的图像中学到了东西，他们更有可能适应右侧的图像，他们能判断出"病人只是躺歪了"。但AI系统在泛化或者说"搞清楚怎么处理新类型数据"方面，可能远不如人类医生。

> To summarize, here are some of the strengths and weaknesses of machine learning. Machine learning tends to work well when you're trying to learn a simple concept such as something that you could do with less than a second of mental thought and when there's lots of data available. Machine learning tends to work poorly when you're trying to learn a complex concept from small amounts of data. A second underappreciated weakness of AI is that it tends to do poorly when it's also performed on new types of data that's different than the data it has seen in your dataset. Let me explain with an example. Say you built a supervised learning system that uses A to B to learn to diagnose pneumonia from images like these. These are pretty high quality chest x-ray images. But now let's say you take this AI system and apply it at a different hospital or a different medical center where maybe the x-ray technician somehow strangely had the patients always lie at an angle or sometimes there are these defects. Not sure if you can see the little scratches in the image. These little other objects lying on top of the patients. If the AI system has learned from data like that on your left, maybe taken from a high quality medical center and you take this AI system and apply it to a different medical center that generates images like those on the right, then this performance will be quite poor as well. A good AI team would be able to ameliorate or to reduce some of these problems but doing this is not that easy. And this is one of the things that AI is actually much weaker than humans. If a human has learned from images on the left, they're much more likely to be able to adapt to images like those on the right as they figure out that the patient is just lying at an angle. But an AI system can be much less robust than human doctors in generalizing or figuring out what to do with new types of data like this.

### 如果你觉得AI能做什么和不能做什么的边界仍然模糊，完全正常——连吴恩达自己今天也不能一眼就判断一个项目是否可行，他通常也需要几周的技术尽职调查才能形成比较确定的判断；但希望这些例子至少能帮你开始在脑子里想象你公司里有哪些事可能可行、值得进一步探索；接下来两个视频是可选内容，会给你一个关于神经网络和深度学习的非技术性解释

> 我希望这些例子在帮你磨练关于AI能做什么和不能做什么的直觉。如果你还是觉得"能做什么和不能做什么"之间的边界有点模糊，别担心——这完全正常，也完全没问题。事实上，即便今天，我也没办法看到一个项目就立刻判断它是否可行。我通常也需要好几周——小几周——的技术尽职调查，才能对一件事是否可行形成比较有信心的判断。但我希望这些例子至少能帮你在脑中开始想象：你的公司里有哪些事情可能可行、值得进一步探索。接下来两个视频是可选的，会给你一个关于什么是神经网络和深度学习的非技术性描述。欢迎你去看。然后下周，我们会深入讲解构建一个AI项目的过程是什么样的。期待下周见到你。

> I hope these examples are helping you hone your intuitions about what AI can and cannot do. In case the boundary between what it can and cannot do still seems fuzzy to you, don't worry, this is completely normal and completely okay. In fact, even today, I still can't look at the project and immediately tell if something is feasible or not. And I often still need weeks of, small numbers of weeks of technical diligence before forming strong conviction about whether something is feasible or not. But I hope that these examples can at least help you start imagining some things in your company that might be feasible and might be worth exploring more. The next two videos after this are optional and are a non-technical description of what are neural networks and what is deep learning. Please feel free to watch those. And then next week, we'll go much more deeply into the process of what building an AI project would look like. Look forward to seeing you next week.

## 八、深度学习解释（第1部分）——神经网络就像搭乐高积木，每个神经元计算一个简单函数，堆叠起来就能算出极其复杂的A到B映射，而且中间那些层全是你不需要操心的——AI自己搞定

### 用T恤需求预测来直观理解——假设你运营一个卖T恤的网站，价格越高需求越低，你可以拟合一条曲线：价格作为输入A，预估需求作为输出B；这个蓝色曲线就是最简单的神经网络，中间那个小圆圈就叫"神经元"（人工神经元），它做的事就是计算这条曲线

> 在AI中，"深度学习"和"神经网络"这两个词几乎可以互换使用。虽然它们做机器学习非常厉害，但围绕它们也有一些炒作和神秘感。这个视频会帮你揭开深度学习的神秘面纱，让你理解深度学习和神经网络到底是什么。咱们用一个需求预测的例子。假设你运营一个卖T恤的网站，你想知道——根据你怎么定价——你预计能卖出多少件T恤。你可能会创建这样一个数据集：T恤价格越高，需求越低。你可能会拟合一条直线穿过这些数据，显示出价格上升、需求下降。但需求不可能降到零以下，所以你可能会说需求在零这里趋于平坦——超过某个点之后，你预计基本没人会买T恤了。结果，这条蓝色线条可能就是最简单的神经网络。你把价格作为输入A，你想让它输出预估需求B。用神经网络的方式来画：价格输入到这个小小的圆形物体，然后这个小圆形物体输出预估需求。在AI的术语中，这个小圆形物体叫"神经元"——有时候叫"人工神经元"——它做的事情就是计算我左边画的这条蓝色曲线。这大概是最简单的神经网络了——只有一个单个人工神经元，输入价格，输出预估需求。如果你把这个橙色圆圈、这个人工神经元想象成一块小乐高积木——神经网络不过就是拿很多这样的乐高积木堆叠起来，堆出一个强大的、由这些神经元构成的大型网络。

> The terms deep learning and neural network are used almost interchangeably in AI. And even though they're great for machine learning, there's also been a bit of hype and a bit of mystique about them. This video will demystify deep learning so that you have a sense of what deep learning and neural networks really are. Let's use an example from demand prediction. Let's say you run a website that sells t-shirts and you want to know based on how you price the t-shirts, how many units you expect to sell, how many t-shirts you expect to sell. You might then create a data set like this where the higher the price of the t-shirt, the lower the demand. So you might fill a straight line to this data showing that as the price goes up, the demand goes down. Now demand can never go below zero, so maybe you say that the demand will flatten out at zero and beyond a certain point, you expect pretty much no one to buy any t-shirts. It turns out this blue line is maybe the simplest possible neural network. You have as input the price, A, and you want it to output the estimated demand, B. So the way you would draw this as a neural network is that the price would be input to this little round thing there and this little round thing outputs the estimated demand. In the terminology of AI, this little round thing here is called a neuron or sometimes it's called an artificial neuron and all it does is compute this blue curve that I've drawn here on the left. This is maybe the simplest possible neural network with a single artificial neuron that just inputs the price and outputs the estimated demand. If you think of this orange circle, this artificial neuron as a little Lego brick, all that the neural network is is if you take a lot of these Lego bricks and stack them on top of each other until you get a big power, a big network of these neurons.

### 再看一个更复杂的例子——除了价格，你还知道运费、市场营销预算和T恤材质（厚棉还是薄料）；你清楚顾客很在意性价比，所以一个蓝色神经元负责估算"可负担性"（主要由价格和运费决定）；另一个神经元负责估算"知名度"（主要取决于营销预算）；第三个神经元负责估算"感知质量"——这个比较微妙，既受营销影响（广告说服人这是高质量产品），也受价格影响（贵的东西有时候让人觉得质量更好）；然后这三个神经元的输出再汇入一个黄色神经元，综合算出最终的需求预测量

> 来看一个更复杂的例子。假设除了T恤的价格，你还知道顾客需要付的运费，你每周在营销上可能花多花少，你还可能用厚实昂贵的棉料或更便宜的轻质材料做T恤。这些就是你认为会影响T恤需求的因素。我们来看看一个更复杂的神经网络会是什么样子。你知道顾客非常在意"负担得起"的程度。所以我们有一个神经元——我画蓝色的这个——它的工作就是估算T恤的可负担性。可负担性主要取决于T恤的价格和运费。第二，影响T恤需求的还有"知名度"——消费者在多大程度上知道你在卖这款T恤？影响知名度的主要因素是营销。所以我在这里画第二个蓝色人工神经元——输入营销预算（你花了多少钱做营销），输出消费者对你T恤的知名度。最后，产品的"感知质量"也会影响需求。感知质量受营销影响——如果营销试图说服人们这是一件高质量T恤；有时候东西的价格也影响感知质量——贵的东西让人觉得质量更好。所以我在这里画第三个蓝色人工神经元——输入价格、营销和材质——来尝试估算T恤的感知质量。最后，既然前面这三个蓝色神经元已经分别算出了可负担性、消费者知名度和感知质量，你就可以在右边再加一个黄色神经元——接收这三个因素作为输入，输出预估需求。这就是一个神经网络——它的任务是学习从这四个输入（即输入A）到输出B（需求）的映射。也就是说它学习这个输入到输出、A到B的映射。这是一个相当小的神经网络——只有四个人工神经元。实际中，今天用的神经网络要大得多，动辄几千、几万甚至远远更多的神经元。

> Let's look at a more complex example. Suppose that instead of knowing only the price of the t-shirts, you also have the shipping cost that the customers will have to pay to get the t-shirts. Maybe you spend more or less on marketing in a given week and you can also make the t-shirt out of a thick, heavy, expensive cotton or a much cheaper, more lightweight material. These are some of the factors that you think will affect the demand for your t-shirts. Let's see what a more complex neural network might look like. You know that your customers care a lot about affordability. So let's say you have one neuron and let me draw this one in blue whose job it is to estimate the affordability of the t-shirts because affordability is mainly a function of the price of the shirts and of the shipping costs. A second thing that will affect the demand for your t-shirts is awareness. How much are consumers aware that you're selling this t-shirt? So the main thing that affects awareness is going to be your marketing. So let me draw here a second artificial neuron that inputs your marketing budget, how much you spent on marketing and outputs how aware are consumers of your t-shirt. Finally, the perceived quality of your product will also affect demand and perceived quality would be affected by marketing. If the marketing tries to convince people this is a high quality t-shirt and sometimes the price of something also affects perceived quality. So I'm going to draw here a third artificial neuron that inputs price, marketing and material and tries to estimate the perceived quality of your t-shirts. Finally, now that the earlier neurons, these three blue neurons have figured out how affordable, how much consumer awareness and what's the perceived quality, you can then have one more neural neuron over here that takes this input, these three factors and outputs the estimated demand. So this is a neural network and its job is to learn to map from these four inputs, that's the input A, to the output B to demand. So it learns this input output or A to B mapping. This is a fairly small neural network with just four artificial neurons. In practice, neural networks used today are much larger with easily thousands, tens of thousands or even much larger than that numbers of neurons.

### 但神经网络最神奇的地方在于：你根本不需要自己去找出"可负担性、知名度、感知质量"这些中间因素——你只需要把输入A（价格、运费、营销、材质）和输出B（实际需求数据）喂给它，神经网络自己就会自动学会中间那些蓝色神经元应该算什么；只要数据够多、网络够大，它就能自动找到从A到B最准确的映射函数

> 这个描述中还有一个细节我需要澄清一下：在我描述神经网络的方式中，听起来好像你得自己先搞清楚关键因素是"可负担性"、"知名度"和"感知质量"。但用神经网络最棒的一点恰恰是：要训练一个神经网络——也就是说，用神经网络来构建一个机器学习系统——你唯一需要做的就是给它输入A和输出B，它自己会搞定中间所有这些东西。所以，构建一个神经网络时，你要做的就是：喂给它大量包含输入A的数据，然后有一个像这样的神经网络——几个蓝色神经元汇聚到一个黄色输出神经元——你还需要给它包含需求B的数据。软件自己会去搞清楚这些蓝色神经元应该计算什么，从而完全自动地学习出从输入A到输出B的最准确可能的映射函数。而且事实证明，如果你给这个网络足够多的数据、训练一个足够大的神经网络，它就能非常出色地从输入A映射到输出B。所以这就是神经网络——它是一组人工神经元，每个神经元计算一个相对简单的函数，当你像搭乐高积木一样把它们堆叠到足够多，它们就能计算出极其复杂的函数，给你从输入A到输出B的非常精确的映射。

> Now there's just one final detail of this description that I want to clean up, which is that in the way I've described a neural network, it was as if you had to figure out that the key factors are affordability, awareness and perceived quality. One of the wonderful things about using neural networks is that to train a neural network, in other words, to build a machine learning system using a neural network, all you have to do is give it the input A and the output B and it figures out all of the things in the middle by itself. So to build a neural network, what you would do is feed it lots of data with the input A and have a neural network that just looks like this with a few blue neurons feeding to a yellow output neuron. And then you have to give it data with the demand B as well. And it's the software's job to figure out what these blue neurons should be computing so that it can completely automatically learn the most accurate possible function mapping from the input A to the output B. And it turns out that if you give this enough data and train a neural network that is big enough, this can do an incredibly good job mapping from inputs A to outputs B. So that's a neural network. It's a group of artificial neurons, each of which computes a relatively simple function, that when you stack enough of them together like LEGO bricks, they can compute incredibly complicated functions that give you very accurate mappings from the input A to the output B.

### 这集用一个简单的需求预测例子展示了神经网络的基本原理；下集会看一个更复杂的例子——人脸识别，来理解神经网络怎么"看懂"图片

> 这集视频中，你看到了一个神经网络应用于需求预测的例子。咱们进入下个视频，来看一个更复杂的例子——神经网络怎么应用于人脸识别。

> Now in this video, you saw an example of neural networks applied to demand prediction. Let's go on to the next video to see a more complex example of neural networks applied to face recognition.


---

## 九、深度学习解释（第2部分）——人脸识别中，神经元自动从像素中逐层提取特征：先检测边缘，再检测眼鼻嘴等器官，再到脸型，最后输出身份，整个过程你只需要提供图片和答案，中间的每一步AI自己搞定

### 上集讲了神经网络做需求预测，但神经网络怎么"看"一张图片并认出里面是谁？计算机看图片的方式跟人类完全不同——我们看到的是人眼，计算机看到的是一个像素亮度值的网格：1000×1000的黑白图片就是100万个数字，彩色图片则是300万个数字（每个像素的红绿蓝三个值）；神经网络的任务就是输入这几百万个数字，然后告诉你图片里的人是谁

> 上集视频中，你看到了神经网络怎么应用于需求预测。但神经网络怎么能看一张图片就知道里面是什么——或者听一段音频就理解里面说了什么？咱们来看一个更复杂的例子：把神经网络应用于人脸识别。假设你想构建一个系统，通过照片来识别人。一段软件怎么能看这张照片就知道照片里的人是谁？咱们放大到一个小方块来更好地理解计算机是怎么"看"图片的。你我看到的是人的眼睛，而计算机看到的是这个——一个像素亮度值的网格。它告诉计算机，图像中每个像素有多亮。如果是黑白或灰度图像，每个像素对应一个数字，告诉你这个像素有多亮。如果是彩色图像，每个像素实际上有三个数字——分别对应这个像素的红色、绿色和蓝色分量有多亮。所以神经网络的任务就是：输入一大串像这样的数字，然后告诉你照片中这个人的名字。

> In the last video, you saw how a neural network can be applied to demand prediction. But how can a neural network look at a picture and figure out what's in the picture or listen to an audio clip and understand what is said in an audio clip? Let's take a look at a more complex example of applying a neural network to face recognition. Say you want to build a system to recognize people from pictures. How can a piece of software look at this picture and figure out the identity of the person in it? Let's zoom in to a little square like that to better understand how a computer sees pictures. Where you and I see a human eye, a computer instead sees that. It sees this grid of pixel brightness values that tells it for each of the pixels in the image. How bright is that pixel? If it were a black and white or a gray scale image, then each pixel would correspond to a single number telling you how bright is that pixel. If it is a color image, then each pixel would actually have three numbers corresponding to how bright are the red, green and blue elements of that pixel. So the neural network's job is to take as input a lot of numbers like these and tell you the name of the person in the picture.

### 上集T恤的例子中输入只有4个数字（价格、运费、营销、材质），而人脸识别中输入的是几百万个像素值——1000×1000的黑白图就是100万个数字，彩色图就是300万个数字；跟之前一样，神经网络里有大量人工神经元在计算各种值，但你还是不需要操心它们该算什么——神经网络会自动学会：最前面的神经元先学会检测图像中的边缘，稍后的神经元学会检测物体的局部（比如眼睛、鼻子、脸颊的形状、嘴巴的形状），再往后的神经元学会检测不同的人脸形状，最后把所有信息汇总输出这个人的身份

> 上集视频中，你看到了神经网络怎么接收四个数字作为输入——对应T恤的价格、运费、营销量和材质——然后输出需求。在这个人脸识别的例子中，神经网络需要输入多得多的数字——对应这张图片所有像素的亮度值。如果这张图片的分辨率是1000×1000像素，那就是100万个像素。所以如果是黑白或灰度图像，这个神经网络就要接收100万个数字作为输入——对应这100万个像素每一个的亮度。如果是彩色图像，它就要接收300万个数字作为输入——对应这100万个像素每一个的红、绿、蓝三个值。跟之前一样，你会有很多很多个人工神经元在计算各种值。而且你不需要操心这些神经元该算什么——神经网络自己会算出来。但通常的情况是：当你给它一张图片，神经网络前段的神经元学会检测图像中的边缘；然后稍后一点的神经元学会检测物体的局部——所以它可能学会检测眼睛、鼻子、脸颊的形状、嘴巴的形状；再然后，更靠后的神经元学会检测不同的脸型；最终，把所有这一切汇总在一起，输出图像中这个人的身份。

> In the last video, you saw how a neural network can take as input four numbers corresponding to the price shipping cost, amount of marketing and the cloth material of a t-shirt and output demand. In this example, the neural network just has to input a lot more numbers corresponding to all of the pixel brightness values of this picture. If the resolution of this picture is 1000 pixels by 1000 pixels, then that's a million pixels. So if it were a black and white or a gray scale image, this neural network would take as input a million numbers corresponding to the brightness of all one million pixels in this image. Or it was a color image, it would take as input three million numbers corresponding to the red, green and blue values of each of these one million pixels in this image. Similar to before, you will have many, many of these artificial neurons computing various values. And it's not your job to figure out what these neurons should compute. The neural network will figure it out by itself. But typically, when you give it an image, the neurons in the earlier parts of the neural network will learn to detect edges in pictures. And then a little bit later, I'll learn to detect parts of objects. So it may learn to detect eyes and noses and the shape of cheeks and the shape of mouths. And then the later neurons further to the right will learn to detect different shapes of faces. And it will finally put all this together to output the identity of the person in the image.

### 神经网络的魔力就在于：你完全不需要操心中间发生了什么——你唯一需要做的就是给它大量这样的图片数据（A）以及对应的正确身份（B），学习算法自己会搞定中间每个神经元该计算什么；第一周的全部视频到此结束，你现在知道了机器学习和数据科学是怎么工作的，下周你将学习如何构建自己的机器学习或数据科学项目

> 再说一次，神经网络的魔力之一就是：你其实不用太操心它在中间做了什么。你唯一需要做的就是给它大量数据——像这样的图片（A）以及正确的身份（B）——学习算法自己会搞定中间每个神经元该计算什么。恭喜你完成了本周的所有视频！你现在知道了机器学习和数据科学是怎么工作的。期待在下周的视频中见到你——下周你将学习如何构建你自己的机器学习或数据科学项目。下周见！

> And again, part of the magic of neural networks is that you don't really need to worry about what it is doing in the middle. All you need to do is give it a lot of data of pictures like this, A, as well as of the correct identity B. And the learning algorithm will figure out by itself what each of these neurons in the middle should be computing. Congratulations on finishing all the videos for this week. You now know how machine learning and data science work. I look forward to seeing you in next week's videos as well, where you learn how to build your own machine learning or data science project. See you next week.

## 二、第2周课程介绍——上周学了AI是什么，这周教你如何把AI技术落地成实际项目：从项目工作流程、项目选择框架到数据和团队组织，学完你就能和朋友一起尝试有意思的AI项目了

### 上周你学习了AI和机器学习的基础技术，但这周我们要回答一个更实际的问题：怎么把这些技术用到项目里——不管是你一个人在自家车库里做的小项目，还是大公司里与战略对齐的大项目，这周都会教你怎么做

> 欢迎回来。上周你学了 AI 和机器学习技术的基础知识。但这些技术怎么用到项目里呢？不管你是想在自家车库里做一个项目，还是想在大公司里做一个和公司战略对齐的大项目，咱们一起来看看。首先，这周你会学到 AI 项目的工作流程是什么。AI 项目有不同的步骤，就像办生日派对有一系列可预测的步骤一样——你先确定客人名单，然后找场地，然后订蛋糕，然后发邀请——AI 项目也有一系列可预测的步骤。通过这个，你会学到 AI 项目的工作流程是什么样的，还会了解做 AI 项目到底是什么感觉。
> Welcome back. Last week you learned about the basics of AI and machine learning technology. But how do you use this technology in a project? Either if you want to do a project in your proverbial garage, or if you want to do a project in a bigger company, or maybe even have something that builds up to align with your corporate strategy. Let's take a look. First, in this week you learn what is the workflow of an AI project. AI projects have different steps. So just as a birthday party has a sequence of predictable steps, first you figure out the guest list, then you find the venue, then you order the birthday cake, then you send invites and so on. So too does an AI project have a sequence of predictable steps. And so through this you learn what is the workflow of an AI project, and you learn what it feels like to be working on an AI project.

### 第二，你会学习如何选择AI项目——看起来好像有很多事可以做，这周给你一个头脑风暴框架，帮你筛选出有潜力的项目，不管是你一个人做、跟几个朋友一起做，还是作为大公司的一部分来推进

> 第二，怎么选择 AI 项目？看起来好像有很多事可以做。这周你要学的第二件事就是一个框架，用来头脑风暴和筛选有潜力的项目——不管是你自己做，还是和几个朋友一起，还是作为大公司的一部分来推进。最后，你还会学到怎么组织数据、怎么组织团队——同样，这个团队可以只是你自己或者几个朋友，也可以是一个大得多的公司团队。但你学到的是怎么为执行 AI 项目把数据和人组织起来。
> Second, how do you select an AI project? It seems there are a lot of things you could do. The second thing you learn this week is a framework for brainstorming and selecting potentially promising projects to work on, either by yourself or with a few friends or as part of a bigger company effort. Finally, you also learn how to organize the data, as was the team, which again could be just you or a few friends or a much bigger corporate team, but you learn how to organize the data and team for executing on an AI project.

### 到这周结束，你会知道做AI项目是什么感觉、以及怎么构建你自己的AI项目——也许你就能开始跟朋友们一起探索一些有趣的项目想法了

> 到这周结束，你就知道做 AI 项目是什么感觉了，也知道怎么构建你自己的 AI 项目。也许你就能开始跟朋友们一起探索一些有潜力的想法来试一下。咱们进入下一个视频吧。
> By the end of the week, you know what it feels like and how to build your own AI project, and maybe you'll be able to start exploring with some of your friends' promising ideas to try. Let's go on to the next video.


---

## 三、机器学习项目工作流程——机器学习项目有三个核心步骤：收集数据、训练模型、部署模型，整个过程充满迭代，以语音识别和自动驾驶为例一步步拆解给你看

### 机器学习项目的工作流分三步：第一步是收集数据——以语音识别为例，你想让系统识别"Alexa"这个唤醒词，你就得去录一堆人说"Alexa"的音频，再录一堆人说其他词比如"hello"的音频，把这些音频收集起来；第二步是用机器学习算法来学习从输入（音频）到输出（说的是什么词）的A到B映射

> 机器学习算法可以学习从输入到输出、或者说从 A 到 B 的映射。那你怎么构建一个机器学习项目呢？这个视频你会学到机器学习项目的工作流程。咱们用语音识别来举例。你们家可能已经有 Amazon Echo、Google Home、Apple Siri 或者百度 DuerOS 设备了。几年前我在 Google 的语音识别系统上做过一些工作，也主导了百度的 DuerOS 项目。今天我自己厨房里就有一个 Amazon Echo。每次我煮鸡蛋的时候都会说："Alexa，设个三分钟计时"，然后它会在三分钟到了的时候提醒我，鸡蛋煮好了。那你怎么构建一个能识别"Alexa"或"Hey Google"或"Hey Siri"或"你好小度"的语音识别系统呢？咱们来梳理关键步骤。为了简单起见，我用 Amazon Echo 检测"Alexa"关键词作为例子。第一步是收集数据——你去找一些人说"Alexa"这个词，把音频录下来；同时也找一堆人说其他词，比如"hello"，也录下来。第二步是训练模型，用机器学习算法学习 A 到 B 的映射，其中输入 A 是一段音频，第一段音频系统应该输出"用户说了 Alexa"，第二段音频输出"用户说了 hello"。每次 AI 团队开始训练模型时，第一次尝试几乎总是不太好用，团队需要反复尝试很多次——在 AI 领域我们叫"迭代"——直到模型表现足够好为止。
> Machine learning algorithms can learn input to output or A to B mappings. So how do you build a machine learning project? In this video you'll learn what is the workflow of machine learning projects. Let's take a look. As a running example, I'm going to use speech recognition. So some of you may have an Amazon Echo or Google Home or Apple Siri device or a Baidu Duo OS device in your homes. Some years back I've done some work on Google's speech recognition system and it also led Baidu's Duo OS project. And today I actually have an Amazon Echo in my kitchen. So every time I'm boiling an egg, I will say Alexa set timer for three minutes and then it lets me know when the three minutes are up and my eggs are ready. So how do you build a speech recognition system that can recognize when you say, Alexa or hey Google or hey Siri or hello Baidu? Let's go through the key steps of a machine learning project. And just for simplicity, I'm going to use Amazon Echo or detecting the Alexa keyword as this running example. The first step is to collect data. So that means you go around and get some people to say the word Alexa for you and you record the audio of that. And you'll also get a bunch of people to say other words like hello or say lots of other words and record the audio of that as well. Having collected a lot of audio data, a lot of these audio clips of people saying either Alexa or saying other things, step two is to then train the model. And this means you would use a machine learning algorithm to learn an input to output or A to B mapping where the input A would be an audio clip. And in the case of the first audio clip above, hopefully it will tell you that the user said Alexa and in the case of audio clip two shown on the right, hopefully the system will learn to recognize that the user has said hello. Whenever an AI team starts to train the model, meaning to learn the A to B or input output mapping, what happens pretty much every time is the first attempt doesn't work well. And so invariably the team will need to try many times or in AI we call this iterate many times. You have to iterate many times until hopefully the model looks like it's good enough.

### 第三步是部署模型——把AI软件放进实际的智能音箱里，交付给测试用户或大规模用户群；很多AI产品上线后你会发现，在实际环境里模型表现可能不如预期，比如你拿美国口音训练的系统发到英国，英国人一说"Alexa"系统就懵了，这时你需要获取这些新数据回来维护和更新模型

> 第三步是部署模型，也就是把这个 AI 软件放进实际的智能音箱里，交付给一小批测试用户或者大规模用户群。很多 AI 产品上线后你会发现，当它开始接收新数据时，表现可能不如你最初期望的那么好。比如说我是英国人，就拿英国人开涮吧——假设你的语音识别系统是用美国口音训练的，然后你把这个智能音箱发到英国，英国人开始说"Alexa"，你可能发现它识别英国口音的语音不如你期望的好。当这种情况发生时，希望你能获取到这些英国口音表现不佳的数据，然后用来维护和更新模型。总结一下，机器学习项目的关键步骤是：收集数据、训练模型（A 到 B 的映射）、部署模型。贯穿这些步骤的是大量的迭代——微调、适配模型让它更好用，甚至在发货后获取数据回来持续改进产品，当然这取决于你能不能拿到数据回传。
> The third step is to then actually deploy the model. And what that means is you put this AI software into an actual smart speaker and ship it to either a small group of test users or to a large group of users. What happens in a lot of AI products is that when you ship it, you see that it starts getting new data and it may not work as well as you had initially hoped. So for example, I am from the UK, so I'm going to pick on the British, but let's say you had trained your speech recognition system on American accented speakers and you then ship this smart speaker to the UK and you start having British accented people say Alexa, then you may find that it doesn't recognize this speech as well as you had hoped. And when that happens, hopefully you can get data back of cases such as maybe British accented speakers was not working as well as you're hoping and it uses data to maintain and to update the model. So to summarize, the key steps of a machine learning project are to collect data, to train the model, the A to B mapping, and then to deploy the model. And throughout these steps, there is often a lot of iteration, meaning fine tuning or adapting the model to work better or getting data back even after you've shipped it to hopefully make the product better, which may or may not be possible depending on whether you're able to get data back.

### 用同样的三步框架来看自动驾驶汽车的关键组件——输入是车辆前方摄像头拍到的图片，输出是其他车辆的位置；数据收集阶段你需要输入A（前方道路照片）和输出B（在图片上标注其他车的位置用的矩形框）；训练模型时AI工程师第一次尝试通常也不太行，比如软件可能把背景物体误判成车，需要反复迭代才能准确检测出车的位置

> 咱们用这三步来看看另一个项目——构建自动驾驶汽车的关键组件。记住核心三步：收集数据、训练模型、部署模型。假设你在造自动驾驶汽车，其中一个关键组件是机器学习算法，输入是车辆前方拍摄的图片，输出是其他车辆的位置。第一步是收集数据——如果你的目标是让算法输入图片、输出其他车的位置，你需要收集的数据就是图片以及你希望 AI 输出的其他车的位置。比如有张图片作为输入 A，你还需要标注输出 B 是什么——给图片里每一辆你想检测到的车画上一个矩形框。看起来像是手画的，实际上你会用专门的软件来画精准的矩形框。第二步训练模型，AI 工程师开始训练的时候，总会发现一开始不太行——比如说，第一次尝试时软件可能把一个背景物体错当成车。只有通过反复迭代，你才能得到更好的结果，让它准确找到车的位置。第三步部署模型。在自动驾驶领域，安全永远是第一位的，部署和测试模型的时候必须以保障安全为前提。当软件真正上路后，你可能会发现有一些新类型的车辆——比如高尔夫球车——软件检测不出来。那你就需要获取这些高尔夫球车的图片数据回来，用来维护和更新模型，这样你的 AI 软件就能持续变好，最终做到能很准确地从图片中检测出其他车辆。
> Let's look at these three steps and see how they apply on the different projects on building a key component of a self-driving car. So remember the key steps of collect data, train model, and deploy model, since we'll revisit these three steps on the next slide. Let's say you're building a self-driving car. One of the key components of a self-driving car is a machine learning algorithm that takes as input, say a picture of what's in front of your car and tells you where are the other cars. So what's the first step of building this machine learning system? Basically you remember from the last slide that the first step was to collect data. So if your goal is to have a machine learning algorithm that can take as input and image and output the position of other cars, the data you would need to collect would be both images as well as position of other cars that you want the AI system to output. So let's say you start off with a few pictures like this. These are the inputs A to the machine learning algorithm. You need to also tell it what is the output B you would want. And so for each of these pictures, you would draw a rectangle around the cars in the picture that you wanted to detect. And on this slide, I'm hand drawing these rectangles, but in practice you would use some software that lets you draw perfect rectangles rather than these hand drawn ones. And then having created this data set, what was the second step? If you remember that the second step was to train the model. Now invariably when your AI engineers start training a model, they'll find initially that it doesn't work that well. For example, given this picture, maybe the software, the first few tries, thinks that that is a car. And it's only by iterating many times that you hopefully get a better result by figuring out that that is where the car actually is. Finally, what was the third step? It was to deploy the model. Of course, in the self-driving world, it's important to treat safety as number one and deploy a model or to test the model only in ways that can preserve safety. But when you put the software in cars on the road, you may find that there are new types of vehicles, say golf carts, that the software isn't detecting very well. And so you would get data back, say pictures of these golf carts, use the new data to maintain and update the model so that hopefully you can have your AI software continually get better and better to the point where you end up with a software that can do a pretty good job detecting other cars from pictures like these.

### 这节课你学到了机器学习项目的三个关键步骤：收集数据、训练模型、部署模型——下一步我们来看数据科学项目的工作流程有什么不同

> 这节课你学到了机器学习项目的关键步骤：收集数据、训练模型、部署模型。接下来咱们看看数据科学项目的工作流程是什么样的。下个视频见。
> In this video, you learn what are the key steps of a machine learning project, which are to collect data, to train the model, and then to deploy the model. Next, let's take a look at what are the key steps or what is the workflow of a data science project. We'll come to the next video.


---

## 四、数据科学项目工作流程——与机器学习不同，数据科学的输出是一组可操作的洞察，帮你做不同的决策；三步走：收集数据、分析数据、提出假设和行动建议，以销售漏斗优化和咖啡杯生产线优化两个案例详细拆解

### 数据科学项目的输出不是模型，而是一组可操作的洞察——这些洞察可能会让你改变做事的方式；以优化电商销售漏斗为例：用户在你的网站上浏览各种咖啡杯，进入产品页，加入购物车，最后结账，你想让尽可能多的人走完这个流程，数据科学能怎么帮你

> 和机器学习项目不同，数据科学项目的输出通常是一组可操作的洞察，这些洞察可能会让你改变做事的方式。所以数据科学项目的工作流程和机器学习不一样。咱们以优化销售漏斗为例来看看。假设你经营一个卖咖啡杯的电商网站，用户要在你这里买咖啡杯，他们通常会走这样一系列步骤：先浏览网站看看不同款式的咖啡杯，进入产品页面，把杯子加入购物车并进入购物车页面，最后完成结账。如果你想优化这个销售漏斗，让尽可能多的人走完所有步骤，数据科学怎么帮解决这个问题呢？
> Unlike a machine learning project, the output of a data science project is often a set of actionable insights, insights that may cause you to do things differently. So data science projects have a different workflow than machine learning projects. Let's take a look at what are the steps of a data science project. As our running example, let's say you want to optimize a sales funnel. You run an e-commerce or online shopping website that sells coffee mugs. And so for users to buy coffee mug from you, the sequence of steps they'll usually follow. First, they'll visit your website and take a look at the different coffee mugs on offer. Then eventually they'll get to a product page and then they'll have to put it into the shopping card and go to the shopping card page. And then they'll finally have to check out. So if you want to optimize the sales funnel to make sure that as many people as possible get through all of these steps, how can you use data science to help with this problem?

### 数据科学项目的三步：第一步收集数据——比如记录不同用户在网站上的浏览行为和他们来自哪个国家（通过IP地址判断）；第二步分析数据并反复迭代——团队可能提出很多猜想，比如海外客户因为国际运费太高止步于结账页面（提议把运费摊进产品价格里）、节假日期间购物行为会出现波动（更多人因为送礼而购物，或者更少因为不在公司电脑前）、某些国家有午休时段在线购物人数下降（建议减少午休时段的广告投放）

> 数据科学项目的关键步骤：第一步是收集数据。比如在网站上有数据记录不同用户访问了哪些页面，你还能通过 IP 地址判断用户来自哪个国家——当然实际中你能获取的用户数据远不止国家。第二步是分析数据。你的数据科学团队可能会有很多想法：比如海外客户看到国际运费被吓跑了，很多人走到结账页面但就是不结账——如果是真的，你就可以考虑把部分运费摊到产品价格里；或者团队发现节假日期间数据有波动，也许更多人因为送礼而购物，也许更少因为不在公司电脑前；或者有些国家有午休习惯，午休时段在线购物的人会下降，那你就应该减少午休时段的广告投放。一个好的数据科学团队会有很多想法，他们会尝试很多想法——也就是"迭代"很多次——来获取好的洞察。
> The first step is to collect data. So on a website like the one we saw, you may have a data set that stores when different users go to different web pages. In this simple example, I'm assuming that you can figure out the country that the users are coming from. For example, by looking at their computer's address called an IP address and figuring out what is the country from which they're originating. But in practice, you could usually get quite a bit more data about users than just what country they're from. The second step is to then analyze the data. Your data science team may have a lot of ideas about what is affecting the performance of your sales funnel. For example, they may think that overseas customers are scared off by the international shipping cost, which is why a lot of people go to the checkout page, but don't actually check out. And if that's true, then you might think about whether to put part of shipping costs into the actual product costs. Or your data science team may think there are blips in the data whenever there's a holiday. Maybe more people will shop around the holidays because the buying gives. Or maybe fewer people will shop around the holidays because they're staying home rather than sometimes shopping from their work computers. And in some countries, there may be time of day blips where in countries that observe a siesta, so a time of rest, like an afternoon rest, there may be fewer shoppers online and so your sales may go down. And they might then suggest that you should spend fewer advertising dollars during the period of siesta because fewer people will go online to buy at that time. So a good data science team may have many ideas and so they try many ideas or will say iterate many times to get good insights.

### 第三步是提炼洞察、提出假设和行动建议——比如把运费融入产品价格，然后部署这些变更；之后你会获取新的数据反馈，用户行为随之改变，数据科学团队再周期性重新分析新数据，不断提出更好的假设和行动——这就是数据科学的循环

> 第三步，数据科学团队会把这些洞察提炼成较少数量的假设——关于哪些地方做得好、哪些做得不好——以及较少数量的建议行动，比如把运费融入产品成本而不是单独列一项。当你采纳这些建议并部署到网站之后，用户的行为就会因为你的改变而产生新数据——比如你在午休时段调整了广告策略、或者改变了结账政策之后——然后你的数据科学团队可以继续收集数据、周期性重新分析，看看能不能提出更好的假设和更好的行动。所以数据科学项目的关键步骤是：收集数据、分析数据、提出假设和行动建议，然后继续获取数据回传并周期性重新分析。
> Finally, the data science team will distill these insights down to a smaller number of hypotheses about ideas of what could be going well and what could be going poorly, as well as a smaller number of suggested actions, such as incorporating shipping costs into the product costs rather than having it as a separate line item. When you take some of these suggested actions and deploy these changes to your website, you then start to get new data back as users behave differently now that you advertise differently at the time of siesta or have a different checkout policy. And then your data science team can continue to collect data and reanalyze the new data periodically to see if they can come up with even better hypotheses or even better actions over time. So the key steps of a data science project are to collect the data, to analyze the data, and then to suggest hypotheses and actions, and then to continue to get the data back and reanalyze the data periodically.

### 用同一个三步框架来看另一个场景——优化咖啡杯生产线；生产线包括混合黏土、杯体成型、上釉、窑炉烧制、质检五步；目标是提高良品率减少次品浪费；数据分析可能发现每当湿度太低而窑温太高时杯子会开裂，或者下午温度高时需要根据时间来调整湿度和温度参数

> 用同样的框架来看一个新问题——优化生产线。假设你经营一个工厂，生产成千上万个咖啡杯，你想优化生产线。制造咖啡杯的关键步骤是：第一步混合黏土并加适量的水，第二步将黏土成型为杯体，第三步上釉——加上颜色和保护层，第四步窑炉加热烧制，第五步质检——检查杯子有没有凹陷或裂纹才能发货。制造业一个常见问题是优化生产线的良品率，尽可能少出次品，因为次品得扔掉，浪费时间和材料。第一步收集数据：比如不同批次的黏土是谁供应的、搅拌了多久、含水量多少，不同批次的杯子的湿度是多少、窑炉温度是多少、烧了多久。第二步分析数据并迭代：数据科学团队可能会发现，每当湿度太低、窑温太高时杯子会开裂；或者因为下午温度更高，需要根据一天中的不同时间来调整湿度和温度。第三步提出假设和行动建议：基于这些洞察调整生产线的操作方法，部署变更后获取新数据，周期性重新分析，持续优化生产线表现。
> Let's take this framework and apply it to a new problem to optimizing a manufacturing line. Let's say you run a factory that's manufacturing thousands of coffee mugs and models for sale, and you want to optimize the manufacturing line. So these are the key steps to manufacturing coffee mugs. Step one is to mix the clay, so make sure the appropriate amount of water is added. Step two is to take this clay and to shape the mugs. Then you have to add the glaze, so add the coloring, the protective cover. Then you have to heat this mug, and we call that firing the kiln. And finally, you would inspect the mug to make sure there aren't dents in the mug and it doesn't crack before you ship it to customers. So a common problem in manufacturing is to optimize the yield of this manufacturing line to make sure that as few damaged coffee mugs get produced as possible, because those are coffee mugs you have to throw away, resulting in time and material waste. What's the first step of a data science project? I hope you remember from the last slide that the first step is to collect data. So for example, you may save data about the different batches of clay that you've mixed, such as who supplied the clay and how long did you mix it, or maybe how much moisture was in the clay, how much water did you add. You might also collect data about the different batches of mugs you made, so how much humidity was in that batch, what was the temperature in the kiln, and how long did you fire it in the kiln. Given all this data, you would then ask the data science team to analyze the data and they would, as before, iterate many times to get good insights. And so they might find out, for example, that whenever the humidity is too low and the kiln temperature is too hot that it cracks in the mug, or they may find out that because it's warmer in the afternoon that you need to adjust the humidity and temperature depending on the time of day. Based on the insights from your data science team, you get suggestions for hypotheses and actions on how to change the operations of your manufacturing line in order to improve the productivity of the line. And when you deploy the changes, you then get new data back that you can reanalyze periodically so they can keep on optimizing the performance of your manufacturing line.

### 总结：数据科学项目的关键三步是收集数据、分析数据、提出假设和行动建议——机器学习和数据科学几乎在影响每一个工作职能，下个视频就来看看这些想法如何影响你和你同事的工作

> 总结一下，数据科学项目的关键步骤是：收集数据、分析数据、提出假设和行动建议。这个视频和上个视频你看了机器学习项目和数据科学项目的例子。你会发现机器学习和数据科学几乎在影响每一个工作职能。下个视频我想给你看看这些理念是如何影响很多工作职能的——说不定包括你自己的工作，而且肯定包括你很多同事的工作。咱们进入下个视频。
> To summarize, the key steps of a data science project are to collect the data, to analyze the data, and then to suggest hypotheses and actions. In this video in the last video, you saw some examples of machine learning projects and data science projects. It turns out that machine learning and data science are affecting almost every single job function. What I want to do in the next video is show you how these ideas are affecting many job functions, including perhaps yours and certainly that of many of your colleagues. Let's go on to the next video.
