## 五、每个工作职能都被数据改变——从销售、制造、招聘、营销到农业，数据和AI已经渗透到每一个岗位，用具体案例看数据科学和机器学习如何影响你和你同事的工作

### 过去几十年的社会数字化让数据无处不在——纸质调查问卷变成了数字表单，医生手写病历变成了数字档案，每个职能部门因此都有机会用数据科学和机器学习来提升效率；先从销售说起：数据科学可以帮你优化销售漏斗（上节课讲过），而机器学习可以帮你自动对销售线索做优先级排序——比如优先给大公司CEO打电话而不是小公司实习生，这种自动线索排序能让销售更高效

> 数据正在改变很多不同的工作职能。不管你是做招聘的、销售、营销、制造还是农业，数据很可能正在改变你的工作。过去几十年发生的是社会的数字化——纸质调查问卷变成了数字格式，医生还在写手写病历但这些记录越来越多地变成数字档案。每个职能几乎都是如此。数据的可获取性意味着你的工作很有可能被数据科学或机器学习这类工具赋能。销售方面：上节课你看到了数据科学怎么优化销售漏斗。机器学习呢？如果你是销售，你可能有一堆销售线索——你要联系不同的人说服他们买你们公司的东西。机器学习可以帮你对这些线索做优先级排序：你可能想优先给大公司的CEO打电话，而不是小公司的实习生。这种自动线索排序正让销售人员更高效。
> Data is transforming many different job functions. Whether you work in recruiting or sales or marketing or manufacturing or agriculture, data is probably transforming your job function. What's happened in the last few decades is the digitization of our society. So rather than handing out paper surveys like these, surveys are more likely to be done in digital format. Or doctors still write some handwritten notes, but a doctor's handwritten note is increasingly likely to be a digital record. And so too it is in just about every single job function. And this availability of data means that there's a good chance that your job function can be helped with tools like data science or machine learning. Let's start with sales. You've already seen in the last video how data science can be used to optimize a sales funnel. How about machine learning? If you're a salesperson, you may have a set of leads about different people that you could reach out to to convince them to buy something from your company. Machine learning can help you prioritize these leads. So you might want to prioritize calling up the CEO of the large company rather than the intern at a much smaller company. And this type of automated lead sorting is making salespeople more efficient.

### 制造业：数据科学帮你优化生产线（已讲），而机器学习可以做自动视觉质检——现在很多工厂有成百上千人用肉眼检查产品有没有划痕或凹陷，机器学习可以输入产品图片数据集，自动判断咖啡杯是否有瑕疵，减少人力成本的同时提升品控质量；吴恩达自己也在做这个方向，认为自动视觉检测将对制造业产生巨大影响

> 制造业方面：如果你是生产线经理，数据科学已经在帮你优化生产线了。机器学习能做什么？制造流程中有一道工序是最终质检。事实上现在很多工厂有成百上千人用肉眼检查产品——可能是咖啡杯或者其他东西——看有没有划痕或凹陷，这叫"质检"。机器学习可以输入像这样的数据集，自动判断一个咖啡杯是不是有瑕疵。通过自动发现划痕或凹陷，它可以降低人工成本、同时提升工厂品质。这种自动视觉检测是我认为会对制造业产生巨大影响的技术之一，我自己也一直在做这方面的工作。
> Let's say you're a manufacturing line manager. You've already seen how data science can help you optimize a manufacturing line. How about machine learning? One of the steps of this manufacturing process is the final inspection. And in fact, today in many factories, there can be hundreds or thousands of people using the human eye to check over objects, maybe coffee mugs, maybe other things to see if there's scratches or dents, and that's called inspection. So machine learning can take as input a data set like this and learn to automatically figure out if a coffee mug is defective or not. And by automatically finding scratches or dents, it can reduce labor costs and also improve quality in your factory. This type of automated visual inspection is one of the technologies that I think will have a big impact on manufacturing. This is something I've been working on myself as well.

### 招聘：数据科学可以优化招聘漏斗——招聘流程有发邮件、电话初筛、现场面试、发offer、关闭offer等步骤，如果你发现几乎没人从电话初筛走到现场面试，那可能是因为电话初筛把关太严，应该让更多人进入现场面试；机器学习还能做自动简历筛选——但这个方向涉及重要的伦理问题，需要确保AI系统没有偏见、公平对待每个人（第四周会详细讨论公平性和伦理）

> 招聘方面：招一个人进公司会有一系列可预测的步骤——招聘人员发邮件给候选人、打电话聊一轮、邀请来现场面试、发offer、关闭offer。和数据科学优化销售漏斗类似，招聘也可以用数据科学优化招聘漏斗——很多招聘机构现在就在做这件事。比如，如果你发现几乎没人从电话初筛阶段走到现场面试阶段，那可能说明有太多人进入了电话初筛、或者电话初筛的人太严格了，应该让更多人进入现场面试。机器学习方面：招聘中有一道工序是筛大量简历，决定哪些人值得联系。机器学习开始进入自动简历筛选领域——这确实引发了重要的伦理问题，比如确保你的AI软件不会展现出不良的偏见、公平对待每个人。机器学习的希望是在做这些的同时确保系统是合乎伦理和公平的。在这门《AI for Everyone》课程的最后一周，你还会学到更多关于AI公平性和伦理的内容。
> How about recruiting? When recruiting someone to join your company, there may be a pretty predictable sequence of steps where your recruiter or someone else would send an email to candidate, and then you'd have a phone call with them, bring them on site for an interview, and then extend an offer and maybe close the offer. Similar to how data science can be used to optimize a sales funnel, recruiting can also use data science to optimize a recruiting funnel. And in fact, many recruiting organizations are doing so today. For example, if you find that hardly anyone is making it from the phone screen step to the on-site interview step, then you may conclude that maybe too many people are getting to the phone screen stage or maybe the people doing the phone screen are just being too tough and they should let more people get to the on-site interview stage. This type of data science is already having an impact on recruiting. What about machine learning projects? Well, one of the steps of recruiting is to screen a lot of resumes to decide who to reach out to. So you may have to look at one resume and say, yes, let's email them, look at a different one to say, no, let's not move ahead with this candidate. And machine learning is starting to make its way into automated resume screening. This does raise important ethical questions such as making sure that your AI software does not exhibit undesirable forms of bias and treats people fairly, but machine learning is starting to make inroads into this and the hope can do so while making sure that the systems are ethical and fair. In the final week of this AI for Everyone calls, you also learn more about the issues of fairness and ethics in AI.

### 营销：数据科学的经典应用是A/B测试——上线两个版本的网页（比如红色按钮vs绿色按钮），看哪个版本点击更多，数据科学团队帮你从这类数据中获取洞察和建议；机器学习则驱动了个性化产品推荐，比如服装网站发现吴恩达只买蓝衬衫后就会只给他推荐蓝衬衫，这种定制推荐已经占据了大型电商平台销售的很大一部分份额

> 营销方面：优化网站表现的一个常用方法是 A/B 测试——上线两个版本的网页，A 版本有个红色按钮，B 版本有个绿色按钮，测试哪个版本用户点击更多。通过这类数据，数据科学团队帮你获取洞察并提出假设和行动建议来优化网站。机器学习呢？今天很多网站会给你定制化的产品推荐，展示你最可能想买的东西——这实际上显著提升了网站销售额。比如一个服装网站，看了我的购物习惯之后，很快就会只给我推荐蓝衬衫，因为说实话那是我唯一会买的衬衫类型。当然其他用户可能会有更多样、更有趣的推荐。今天这种定制化产品推荐实际上已经驱动了很多大型电商网站销售的很大一部分。
> What if you work in marketing? One of the common ways to optimize the performance of a website is called A-B testing in which you launch two versions of a website. Here, version A has a red button, version B has a green button, and you measure which website causes people to click through more. So with this type of data, a data science team can help you gain insights and suggest hypotheses or actions for optimizing a website. How about machine learning and marketing? Today, a lot of websites will give customized product recommendations to show you the things you are most likely to want to buy. And this actually significantly increases sales on these websites. For example, a clothing website after seeing the way I shop after a while will hopefully just recommend blue shirts to me because that's frankly pretty much the only type of shirt I ever buy. But maybe other customers will have more diverse and more interesting recommendations than mine. But today, these customized product recommendations actually drive a large percentage of sales on many large online e-commerce websites.

### 农业：数据科学用于作物分析——结合土壤条件、天气状况、市场不同作物的价格等数据，数据科学团队可以推荐种什么、什么时候种，在维持土壤条件的同时提升产量；机器学习推动精准农业——用摄像头拍农田照片，机器学习识别出棉花植株和杂草的区别，精准地只往杂草上喷洒除草剂而非大面积喷洒，既帮助农民提高产量又保护了环境

> 最后一个例子来自完全不同的行业——农业。假设你是一个大型工业化农场的农民。数据科学怎么帮你？今天农民已经在用数据科学做作物分析了——结合土壤条件、天气状况、市场上不同作物的价格，数据科学团队可以推荐种什么、什么时候种，从而在维持土壤条件的同时提升产量。这种数据科学正在也将在农业中扮演越来越重要的角色。机器学习方面：我认为农业最令人兴奋的变化之一是精准农业。这是我在农场用手机拍的一张照片——右上角是一株棉花，中间是一棵杂草。通过机器学习，我们开始看到有产品可以到农田里，拍一张这样的照片，然后精准地把除草剂只喷到杂草上，杀死杂草而不是整片喷洒。这种机器学习技术既能帮农民提高产量，同时也能保护环境。
> One last example from a totally different sector. Let's say you work in agriculture. Maybe you're a farmer working on a large industrial farm. How can data science help you? Today, farmers are already using data science for crop analytics, where you can take data on the soil conditions, the weather conditions, the prices of different crops in the market, and have data science teams make recommendations to what to plant, when to plant, so as to improve yields while maintaining the condition of the soil on your farm. This type of data science is and will play a bigger and bigger role in agriculture. Let's also look at the machine learning example. I think one of the most exciting changes to agriculture is precision agriculture. Here's a picture that I took on a farm with my cell phone. On the upper right is a cotton plant, and shown in the middle is a weed. And so with machine learning, we're starting to see products that can go onto the farms, take a picture like this, and spray a weed killer in a very precise way just onto the weed so that it gets through the weed, but without having to spray an excessive amount of weed killers. This type of machine learning technology is both hoping farmers increase crop yields while also hoping to preserve the environment.

### 从销售、招聘、营销、制造到农业，所有这些工作职能都在被数据、数据科学和机器学习改变——看起来AI能做的事很多，但你怎么选一个值得做的项目呢？下个视频讨论这个问题

> 这个视频里你看到了所有这些工作职能——从销售到招聘、营销、制造到农业——所有这些岗位都在被数据、数据科学和机器学习改变。看起来 AI 能做的事很多，但你怎么选一个值得做的项目呢？下个视频咱们聊聊这个。
> In this video, you saw how all of these job functions, everything from sales recruiting, to marketing, to manufacturing, to farming and agriculture, how all of these job functions are being affected by data, by data science and the machine learning. It seems like there's a lot of different things you could do with AI, but how do you actually select a promising project to work on? Let's talk about that in the next video.


---

## 六、如何选择AI项目（上）——选AI项目要找"AI能做"和"业务有价值"的交集；用三个原则做头脑风暴：自动化任务而非工作、围绕业务价值驱动因素、围绕业务痛点，并且不要被"大数据"吓到，小数据集也能起步

### 选AI项目的核心框架：找两个圆的交集——左边是"AI能做的事"，右边是"对你业务有价值的事"，你要选的是落在这两个圆交集里的项目；AI专家最懂左边那个圆，业务领域专家最懂右边那个圆，所以最好把两类人聚在一起做"跨职能团队"头脑风暴

> 如果你想尝试做一个 AI 项目，怎么选一个值得做的项目呢？别指望一个好主意一夜之间冒出来——有时候会，但有时候也需要几天甚至几周才能想出值得追求的点子。这个视频给你一个框架，用来头脑风暴有潜力的 AI 项目。假设你想为你的业务做一个 AI 项目。你已经知道 AI 不是万能的，所以有一组事情是 AI 能做的——用一个圆来表示。还有一组事情是对你业务有价值的——用第二个圆来表示。你要做的就是选落在这两个圆交集里的项目——选那些既可行（AI 能做）又对你业务有价值的项目。AI 专家通常对左边那个圆里面有什么、没什么有很好的判断力，而你业务领域的专家——不管是销售、营销、农业还是别的——最清楚什么对你的业务真正有价值。所以在做头脑风暴的时候，我经常把懂 AI 的人和业务领域的专家聚到一起，让他们共同找出落在两个圆交集里的项目。我们有时也管这叫"跨职能团队"——就是同时包含 AI 专家和领域专家的团队。
> If you want to try your hand at an AI project, how do you select a worthwhile project to work on? Don't expect an idea to next become overnight. Sometimes it happens, but sometimes it also takes a few days or maybe a few weeks to come up with a worthy idea to pursue. In this video, you see a framework for brainstorming potentially exciting AI projects to pursue. Let's say you want to build an AI project for your business. You've already seen that AI can't do everything, and so there's going to be a certain set of things that is what AI can do. So let's let the circle represent the set of things that AI can do. Now there's also going to be a certain set of things that is valuable for your business. So let's let this second circle represent a set of things that are valuable for your business. What you would like to do is try to select projects that are at the intersection of these two sets. So you select projects hopefully that are both feasible, that can be done with AI, and that are also valuable for your business. So AI experts will tend to have a good sense of what is and what isn't in the set on the left, and domain experts in your business, be it sales and marketing or agriculture or something else, will have a best sense of what is actually valuable for your business. So when brainstorming projects that AI can do and are valuable for your business, I will often bring together a team comprising both people knowledgeable about AI as well as experts in your business area to brainstorm together so that together they can try to identify projects at the intersection of both of these two sets. So sometimes we also call these cross functional teams, and that just means a team that includes both AI experts as well as domain experts, meaning experts in your area of business.

### 头脑风暴的三个原则之第一：思考自动化"任务"而不是自动化"工作"——以呼叫中心为例，员工的"工作"包含接电话、回邮件、处理退款等很多任务，你要做的是从中找到某一个任务（比如来电自动路由或邮件路由）特别适合用机器学习自动化；放射科医生也同理，他们的"工作"包含读X光片、继续教育、会诊、带教、直接面对病人等，你从读X光片这一个任务切入，就能找到最有成果的AI项目

> 做头脑风暴的时候，我有一套在很多公司用过的框架，觉得很有用。分享三个原则。第一：虽然媒体大量报道 AI 会消灭工作岗位——这确实是个需要解决的重要社会问题——但在想具体的 AI 项目时，我发现想"自动化任务"比想"自动化工作"有用得多。以呼叫中心为例：呼叫中心里有很多任务——接电话、回邮件、处理具体操作比如根据客户要求退款。在这些员工做的任务里，可能有一个"来电路由"或"邮件路由"任务特别适合机器学习自动化。就是要通过审视员工做的所有任务，从中找出那一个，让你能在短期内做出最有成果的自动化项目。再看放射科医生：很多媒体报道说 AI 会取代放射科医生，但放射科医生实际上要做很多事——他们读 X 光片（这确实很重要），但他们还要继续教育、和其他医生会诊、带教年轻医生、有些还直接和病人沟通。通过看放射科医生做的所有这些任务，你才能找出其中某一个——比如读 X 光片的 AI 辅助或自动化——让你选到最有成果的项目。
> When brainstorming projects, there's a framework that I've used with a lot of companies that are found to be useful. So let me share with you three principles of three ideas for how you can have a team brainstorm projects. First, even though there's been a lot of press coverage about AI automating jobs away, and this is an important societal issue that needs to be addressed, when thinking about concrete AI projects, I find it much more useful to think about automating tasks rather than automating jobs. Take call center operations. There are a lot of tasks that happen in the call center, ranging from people picking up the phone to answering phone calls, to replying to emails, to taking specific actions, such as issuing a refund on the behalf of a customer request. But the bottom of these tasks that employees in the call center do, there may be one call routing or email routing that may be particularly amenable to machine learning automation. And it's by looking at all these tasks that the group of employees do and selecting one that may allow you to select the most fruitful project for automation in the near term. Let's look at another example, the job of radiologists. There's been a lot of press about how AI may automate radiologists' jobs, but radiologists actually do a lot of things. They read x-rays. That's really important. But they also engage in their own continuing education. They consult with other doctors. They may mentor younger doctors. Some of them also consult directly with patients. And so it's by looking at all of these tasks that a radiologist does, that you may identify one of them, let's say AI assistance or AI automation for reading x-rays, that allows you to select the most fruitful projects to work on.

### 头脑风暴三个原则之第二和第三：问"业务价值的主要驱动因素是什么"——找到能用AI或数据科学增强这些驱动因素的方式可以创造巨大价值；再问"业务的主要痛点是什么"——有些痛点可以用AI解决，有些不能，但理解这些痛点是头脑风暴的有用起点

> 我的建议是，看看你的业务，想想人们做的任务，看能不能找出其中一两个可以用机器学习自动化的。和大公司 CEO 开会做 AI 项目头脑风暴时，我常问的问题是：业务价值的主要驱动因素是什么？有时候找到 AI 或数据科学解决方案来增强这些驱动因素会非常有价值。第三个问题是：业务的主要痛点是什么？有些痛点可以用 AI 解决，有些不行，但理解业务的主要痛点也是做 AI 项目头脑风暴的一个有用起点。
> So what I would recommend is if you look at your business, think about the tasks that people do to see if you can identify just one of them or just a couple of them that may be automatable using machine learning. When meeting CEOs of large companies to brainstorm AI projects for the company, a common question I'll also ask is, what are the main drivers of business value? And sometimes finding AI solutions or data science solutions to augment this can be very valuable. Finally, a third question that I've asked is, sometimes let a valuable project idea is, what are the main pain points in your business? Some of them could be solved with AI. Some of them can't be solved with AI. But by understanding the main pain points in the business, that can create a useful starting point for brainstorming AI projects as well.

### 最后一个重要建议：没有大数据也能起步——更多人数据几乎只有好处，大数据也确实让搜索引擎这类业务拥有护城河；但大数据有时候被过度炒作了，即使只有很小的数据集你通常也能取得进展——比如做咖啡杯自动质检，你可能不希望已经生产了一百万个次品，有时候有100张、1000张甚至只有10张图片就能开始做机器学习项目；所需数据量完全取决于具体问题，跟AI工程师聊一下会更清楚，但千万别因为没有大数据就放弃

> 我还有最后一条做 AI 项目头脑风暴的建议：即使没有大数据——没有海量数据——你也可以取得进展。别误会，更多数据几乎从来不会有害，除了可能要多花点钱买磁盘空间或网络带宽来传输和存储数据。更多数据几乎永远只有好处，我也喜欢有大量数据。数据也确实让某些业务——比如网页搜索——具有防御性壁垒。网页搜索是一个"长尾"业务，意味着有大量非常非常罕见的搜索词，而看到人们在这些罕见搜索词上点击了什么，确实帮助领先的搜索引擎提供了更好的搜索体验。所以大数据很棒——当你能拿到的时候。但我认为大数据有时候也被过度炒作了。即使只有很小的数据集，你通常也能取得进展。举个例子：假设你在为咖啡杯建自动视觉检测系统，要自动检测右边的杯子有瑕疵。如果你有一百万张好杯子和次品杯子的图片，那当然很棒。但我不希望你制造了一百万个次品杯子——那代价也太高了。所以有时候只有 100 张图片，或者 1000 张，甚至有时候只有 10 张，你可能就能开始做机器学习项目了。所需数据量完全取决于具体问题，跟 AI 工程师或 AI 专家聊一下会帮你更好地判断。有些问题 1000 张图片不够，确实需要大数据才能表现好。但我的建议是：不要因为一开始没有很多数据就放弃，即使只有小数据集，你往往也能取得进展。
> I have one last piece of advice for brainstorming AI projects, which is that you can make progress even without big data, even without tons of data. Now don't get me wrong, having more data almost never hurts other than maybe needing to pay a bit more for disk space or network bandwidth to transmit and store the data. Having more data almost always is only helpful and I love having lots of data. And it is also true that data makes some businesses like web search defensible. Web search is a long tail business, meaning that there are a lot of very, very rare web queries. And so seeing what people click on when they search on all of these rare web queries does help the leading web search engines have a much better search experience. So big data is great when you can get it. But I think big data is also sometimes overhyped and even over small data set, you can still often make progress. Here's an example. Let's say you're building an automated visual inspection system for the coffee mug. So you want to automatically detect that the coffee mug on the right is defective. Well if you had a million pictures of good coffee mugs and defective coffee mugs, it would be great to have that many examples of pictures of good and bad coffee mugs to feed to your AI system. But I hope that you have not manufactured a million defective coffee mugs because that feels like a very expensive thing to have to throw away. So sometimes with as few as 100 pictures or maybe a thousand or sometimes maybe as few as 10, you may be able to get started on a machine learning project. The amount of data you need is very problem dependent and speaking with an AI engineer on AI expert would help you get a better sense. There are some problems where a thousand images may not be enough, where you do need big data to get good performance. But my advice is don't give up just because you don't have a lot of data to start off with and you can often still make progress even with a small data set.

### 这个视频你学到了头脑风暴框架和筛选标准——找到既AI可行又对业务有价值的项目；那么头脑风暴出一堆项目之后，你怎么从中选出一两个真正投入去做呢？下个视频接着讲

> 这个视频你看到了一个头脑风暴框架和一系列标准，帮你找出既 AI 可行又对业务有价值的项目。头脑风暴完一堆项目之后，你怎么从中选出一两个真正投入去做的呢？下个视频咱们接着说。
> In this video, you saw a brainstorming framework and a set of criteria for trying to come up with projects that hopefully can be doable with AI and that also valuable for your business. Now having brainstormed with this project, how do you select one or select a small handful to actually commit to and work on? Let's talk about that in the next video.


---

## 七、如何选择AI项目（下）——确定项目前要做技术尽调和业务尽调；还要加一道伦理尽调确保对人类社会有益；另外要面对"自建还是采购"的经典选择，记住"别在火车前面跑"

### 在投入一个可能需要好几个月的大项目之前，要做"尽职调查"——技术尽调要问三个问题：AI系统能否达到期望的性能水平（比如语音识别95%准确率或视觉检测99%准确率是否可行）？需要多少数据才能达到这个性能、你有没有办法获取那么多数据？工程时间线——需要多长时间、多少人才能建成？

> 也许你有一堆 AI 项目的想法，但在确定投入之前，你怎么确保这真的是一个值得做的项目？如果是个小项目，几天就能搞定，可能直接跳进去试试看就行了。但有些 AI 项目可能要几个月才能完成。这个视频我想带你走一遍我用来验证一个项目值不值得花好几个月投入的流程。在投入一个大 AI 项目之前，我通常会做尽职调查。法律世界里"尽职调查"有特定含义，但通俗地说就是你花一些时间去确保你希望是真的东西确实是真的。你已经知道最好的 AI 项目是那些既可行（AI 能做）又有价值的——我们要选落在两个圆交集里的项目。所以为了确保项目可行，我会做技术尽调；为了确保项目有价值，我会做业务尽调。技术尽调是确保你希望建的 AI 系统真的能做出来。你可以跟 AI 专家聊聊这个 AI 系统能不能达到你期望的性能水平——比如你想建一个 95% 准确率的语音识别系统，或者一个 99% 准确率的工厂咖啡杯检测系统，这在今天的技术下真的能做到吗？技术尽调的第二个重要问题是：要达到这个性能水平需要多少数据，你有没有办法获取那么多数据？第三个问题是工程时间线——大概需要多长时间、多少人才能建成你想要的系统。
> Maybe you have a lot of ideas for possible AI projects to work on, but before committing to one, how can you make sure that this really is a worthwhile project? It is a quick project that might take you just a few days, maybe just jump in right away and see if it works or not, but some AI projects may take many months to execute. In this video, I want to step you through the process that I use to double check that a project is worth that many months of effort. Let's take a look. Before committing to a big AI project, I will usually conduct due diligence on it. Due diligence has a specific meaning in the legal world, but informally it just means that you want to spend some time to make sure what your hope is true really is true. You've already seen how the best AI projects are ones that are feasible, so it's something that AI can do as well as valuable. We really want to choose projects at the intersection of these two sets. So to make sure a project is feasible, I will usually go through technical diligence and to make sure that the project is valuable, I will usually go through a business diligence process. Technical diligence is the process of making sure that the AI system you hope to build really is doable, really is feasible. So you might talk to AI experts about whether or not the AI system can actually meet the desired level of performance. For example, if you are hoping to build a speech system that is 95% accurate, consulting with AI experts or perhaps reading some of the trade literature can give you a sense of whether this is doable or not. Or if you want a system to inspect coffee mugs in the factory and you need your system to be 99% accurate, again, is this actually doable with today's technology? A second important question for technical diligence is how much data is needed to get to this desired level of performance and do you have a way to get that much data? Third would be engineering timeline to try to figure out how long it will take and how many people it will take to build the system that you would like to have built.

### 业务尽调要仔细想清楚：这个AI系统建出来之后——比如一个95%准确率的语音识别系统或99.9%准确率的视觉检测系统——能不能帮你实现业务目标？无论是改善现有业务还是创造全新业务线；吴恩达通常会建财务模型来定量估算：到底能省多少钱、增加多少收入，把项目的经济效益建模出来再决定是否投入好几个月

> 除了技术尽调，我通常还会做业务尽调，确保你设想的项目真的对业务有价值。很多 AI 项目通过降低成本来创造价值——比如自动化几个任务或挤出系统效率。很多 AI 系统也能增加收入——比如让更多人完成购物车结账。或者你建的 AI 系统是为了推出新产品或新业务线。所以业务尽调就是仔细想清楚：你正在建的 AI 系统——比如一个 95% 准确率的语音识别系统或 99.9% 准确率的视觉检测系统——能不能帮你实现业务目标？不管你的业务目标是改善现有业务还是创造全新业务线。做业务尽调时，我通常会建电子表格财务模型来定量估算价值——比如估算实际能省多少钱，或者合理的收入增长假设是什么，把项目的经济学模型建出来，再决定是否投入好几个月去做。
> In addition to technical diligence, I will often also conduct business diligence to make sure that the project you envision really is valuable for the business. So a lot of AI projects will drive value through lowering costs, for example, by automating a few tasks or by squeezing more efficiency out of the system. A lot of AI systems can also increase revenue, for example, driving more people to check out in your shopping cart. Or you may be building an AI system to help you launch a new product or a new line of business. So business diligence is the process of thinking through carefully for the AI system that you're building, such as a speech recognition system that's 95% accurate or a visual inspection system that's 99.9% accurate, will allow you to achieve your business goals. Whether your business goal is to improve your current business or to even create brand new businesses in your company. When conducting business diligence, I'll often end up building spreadsheet financial models to estimate the value quantitatively, such as estimate how many dollars are actually saved or what do we think is a reasonable assumption in terms of entries revenue and to model out the economics associated with the project before committing to many months of effort on a project.

### 除了技术尽调和业务尽调，还有第三道"伦理尽调"——AI能做很多能赚钱的事，但有些事赚了钱却不能让社会变得更好；在确定项目之前，请确保你做的事对人类社会是有益的（第四周会详细讨论）

> 虽然这张幻灯片上没有明确列出来，但有一件事我希望你也能做：第三类尽调——伦理尽调。我认为 AI 有很多能做的事，有的甚至能赚很多钱，但可能不会让社会变得更好。所以除了技术尽调和业务尽调，我希望你也能做伦理尽调，确保你做的事真正让人类和社会变得更好。我们会在课程最后一周详细讨论这个。
> Although not explicitly listed on this slide, one thing I hope you also consider doing is a third type of diligence, which is ethical diligence. I think there are a lot of things that AI can do that will even make a lot of money, but that may not make society better off. So in addition to technical diligence and business diligence, I hope you also conduct ethical diligence and make sure that whatever you're doing is actually making humanity and making society better off. We'll also talk more about this in the last week of this course as well.

### 自建还是采购？这是IT界的经典问题——机器学习项目可以自己做也可以外包，两种模式都有成功案例，外包能更快获得人才和启动速度，但最终建起自己的内部AI团队更好；数据科学项目则更多在内部做，因为它和你的业务绑定太深，需要对业务有非常深入的日常理解；记住"别在火车前面跑"——如果某样东西正在成为行业标准（可能是一家创业公司、大公司或开源项目在推动），你不如拥抱行业标准而不是自己从头造，因为你的资源有限，应该聚焦在最独特、最能对你公司产生差异化价值的项目上

> 规划 AI 项目时你还要决定：自建还是采购？这是 IT 界的老问题了，AI 领域也面临同样的选择。比如现在几乎没有公司自己造电脑了，大家都买别人的电脑；几乎没有公司自己造 Wi-Fi 路由器了，大家都买商用路由器。机器学习项目可以内部做也可以外包——两种模式我都见过成功的。有时候外包可以更快获得人才、项目启动更快。但如果最终能建起自己的内部 AI 团队来做这些项目，那会更好——下周讲 AI 转型指南时会详细说。和数据科学项目不像机器学习项目，它们更多是在内部做的。不是不可能外包，有时候也能外包。但我的观察是数据科学项目和你的业务绑定太深，需要对你业务非常深入的日常理解才能做出最好的数据科学项目。所以按比例来说，我见到内部做的数据科学项目比机器学习项目更多。最后，每个行业里有些东西会成为行业标准，你应该避免自己去做那些。对于"自建vs采购"的一个常见答案是：建那些对你来说非常专有、完全定制化的，或者能让你建立独特防御壁垒或竞争优势的东西。而那些会成为行业标准的东西，很可能其他公司会做，你去买比自己做更高效。我的一个团队有句话说得很好："别在火车前面跑"。意思是——如果这是铁轨上跑的火车，前面冒着烟——你要做的不是拼命比火车跑得更快。火车就是行业标准解决方案。如果有公司——可能是创业公司、大公司或者开源项目——正在建一个行业标准解决方案，那你可能应该避免拼命跑在火车前面。因为就算你短期内能跑得更快，最终火车会追上来——在火车前面跑的人会被碾过去。所以当有一个行业标准解决方案被大规模推进的时候，你不如拥抱这个标准或拥抱别人的平台，而不是试图所有东西都在内部自己造。我们都活在一个资源有限的世界里——有限的时间、有限的数据、有限的工程资源。所以我希望你能把这些资源聚焦在最独特、能对你公司产生最大差异化影响的项目上。
> As you're planning out your AI project, you also have to decide do you want to build or buy? This is an age old question in the IT world, and we're facing this question in AI as well. For example, hardly any companies build their own computers these days. They buy someone else's computers and hardly any companies build their own Wi-Fi routers just buy a commercial Wi-Fi router. How about machine learning and data science? Machine learning projects can be in-house or outsourced. I've seen both of these models use successfully. Sometimes if you outsource a machine learning project, you can have access much more quickly to talent and get going faster on a project. It is nice if eventually you build your own in-house AI team and can also do these projects in-house. You hear more about this when we talk about the AI Transformation Playbook in greater detail next week. Unlike machine learning projects though, data science projects are more commonly done in-house. They're not impossible to outsource. You can sometimes outsource them. But what I've seen is that data science projects are often so closely tied to your business that it takes very deep day-to-day knowledge about your business to do the best data science projects. And so just as a percentage as a fraction, I see data science projects in-house more than machine learning projects. Finally, in every industry, some things will be industry standard and you should avoid building those. A common answer to the build versus buy question was build the things that are going to be quite specialized to you or completely specialized to you or that allow you to build a unique defense or advantage. But the things that will be industry standard, probably some other company will build and it'll be more efficient for you to just buy it rather than build it in-house. One of my teams that I really poetic phrase, which is don't sprint in front of a train. And what that means is if this is a train running on the railway tracks, and there's the la chimney with the puff of smoke, what you don't want to do is to be the person or the engineer trying to sprint faster and faster ahead of the train. The train is the industry standard solution. And so if there's a company, maybe a startup, maybe a big company, or maybe an open source effort that is building an industry standard solution, then you may want to avoid trying to run faster and faster to keep ahead of the train. Because even though you could sprint faster in the short term, eventually the train will catch up and you know crush someone trying to sprint in front of a train. So when there's a massive force of an industry standard solution that is being built, you might be better off just embracing an industry standard or embracing someone else's platform rather than trying to do everything in-house. We all live in a world of limited resources, limited time, limited data, limited engineering resources. And so I hope you can focus those resources on the projects that are most unique and will make the biggest difference to your company.

### 通过技术尽调和业务尽调，你应该能开始筛选出有潜力、有价值的项目——如果是大项目可能要花好几个月，吴恩达通常会花好几周做尽调才确定投入；找到了有潜力的项目后，怎么跟AI团队合作把它做出来呢？下个视频讲

> 通过技术尽调和业务尽调，我希望你能开始筛选出有潜力、对你的业务来讲有价值的项目。如果项目是重大投入，可能要花好几个月，我通常会花好几周来做这类尽调再确定投入。现在假设你找到了几个有潜力的项目，你怎么跟 AI 团队合作？怎么跟 AI 团队一起把这些项目做出来呢？下个视频咱们聊聊。
> Through the process of technical diligence as well as business diligence, I hope you can start to identify projects that are potentially valuable or that seem promising for your business. If the project is a big commitment, maybe it'll take many months to do, it's not unusual for me to spend even a few weeks conducting this type of diligence before committing to a project. Now, say you've found a few promising projects. How do you engage with an AI team? How do you work with an AI team to try to get these projects done? Let's talk about that in the next video.


---

## 八、与AI团队协作——帮AI团队成功的关键：用统计方式定义验收标准、理解训练集和测试集的分工、不要期望100%准确率；即使没有AI团队，你和工程师朋友上几门在线课程也能起步

### 与AI团队协作的第一步是明确验收标准——由于AI系统性能通常用统计方式定义（比如检测咖啡杯瑕疵达到95%准确率），你需要准备一个"测试集"来度量这个准确率；测试集是一组带有标注的图片（哪些是好杯子、哪些是次品），AI团队要在测试集上验证是否达到了目标性能；技术上有些AI团队还需要两个测试集（开发和验证集），这是合理的

> 假设你找到了一个想做的有意思的项目，你怎么跟 AI 团队合作呢？这个视频你学到的是 AI 团队如何看待数据，以及你如何与 AI 团队互动来帮他们在项目上取得成功。有个前提要说一下：如果你有一个很酷的点子但接触不到 AI 团队、没有任何 AI 工程师——好在今天这个世界，如果你自己、或者你能鼓励你的工程师朋友去上一两门机器学习或深度学习的在线课程，通常就足以让他们有了起步的知识，可以在这些项目上做出合理的尝试了。那咱们聊聊怎么跟 AI 团队合作。首先，如果你能为项目指定验证标准，这对 AI 团队非常有帮助。我用自动视觉检测来举例——假设你的目标是检测咖啡杯瑕疵，准确率达到至少 95%，这就可以是你的验收标准。但 95% 准确率——怎么度量这个准确率？AI 团队需要的是一个数据集来度量准确率。数据集就是一组图片加上标签——期望输出 B：前两个杯子合格，第三个是次品。所以作为验收标准的一部分，你要确保 AI 团队有一个数据集可以用来度量性能，这样他们才知道是否达到了 95% 准确率。这个数据集的正式术语叫"测试集"，测试集不需要太大，比如一千张图片可能就够了。但咨询 AI 专家，他们会更好地告诉你测试集需要多大才能评估是否达到了 95% 准确率。
> First it really helps your AI team if you can specify an acceptance criteria for the project. I've done a lot of work in automated vision inspection, so I'm going to use that as a running example in these few slides. Let's say your goal is to detect defects in coffee mugs with at least 95% accuracy. So that can be your acceptance criteria for this project. But 95% accuracy, how do you measure accuracy? One of the things the AI team would need is a data set on which to measure the accuracy. So a data set is just a set of pictures, like these, together with the labels with the desired output B that the first two coffee mugs are okay and the third one is defective. So as part of your specification for the acceptance criteria, you should make sure that the AI team has a data set on which to measure the performance so that they can know if they've achieved 95% accuracy. The formal term for this data set is called a test set and the test set may not need to be too big. Maybe a thousand pictures would be just fine for this example. But if you consult an AI expert, they can give you a better sense of how big the test set needs to be for them to be able to evaluate whether or not they're getting to 95% accuracy.

### AI系统的一个新颖之处在于性能通常用统计方式来表达——你不要求系统"完美地"做某件事，而是要求它达到某个百分比准确率、或者在平均意义上表现如何；所以定义验收标准时要想清楚要不要用统计方式

> AI 系统一个新颖的地方在于性能通常用统计方式来定义。你不要求一个 AI 系统完美地做某件事，而更多是要求它在某个百分比准确率上表现——就像这里的例子。所以当你指定验收标准的时候，想一想你的验收标准是否需要以统计方式定义——指定它平均表现如何，或者有多大比例的情况下必须得到正确答案。
> One novel part of AI systems is that the performance is usually specified in a statistical way. So rather than asking for an AI system that just does something perfectly, you see very often that we want an AI system that performs at a certain percentage accuracy, like this example here. So when specifying your acceptance criteria, think of whether your acceptance criteria needs to be specified in a statistical way, where you specify on average how well it does or what percent of time it has to get the right answer.

### AI团队把数据分成两个主要集合：训练集（带标注的样本，机器学习算法从中学习A到B的映射——输入是咖啡杯图片，输出是合格还是次品）和测试集（另一组不同图片也带标注，用来评估学习出来的效果）；比如给测试集的3张图片分别输出合格、合格、合格，实际第三张是次品，那准确率就是2/3即66.7%；实践中训练集通常比测试集大非常非常多

> 咱们深入看看训练集和测试集的概念——这就是AI团队看待数据的方式。AI团队把数据分成两个主要数据集：训练集和测试集。训练集是一组图片加上标签，标明每张图片的咖啡杯是合格还是次品。训练集给出了输入 A（咖啡杯图片）和期望输出 B（合格还是次品）的样例。有了训练集，机器学习算法会学习——也就是"计算"或"找出"——从 A 到 B 的某种映射，这样你就有了一个软件，输入一张图就能尝试判断对应的输出 B 是什么。测试集是另一组不同的图片，也都带着标签。AI 团队评估学习算法的方式是把测试集中的图片输入到 AI 软件中，看它输出了什么。比如测试集有三张图，前两张是合格的、第三张是次品，如果 AI 软件输出都是"合格"，那就是对了 2 个、错了 1 个——66.7% 的准确率。这个图里训练集和测试集都只有三张图片，实践中两个数据集都会大得多。你会发现对大多数问题，训练集比测试集大非常非常多。具体需要多少数据，跟 AI 工程师聊一下。最后，出于技术原因，有些 AI 团队需要的不是一个测试集而是两个——如果你听到他们说"开发集"、"验证集"或"dev 集"，那就是第二个测试集。为什么需要两个测试集的原因比较技术化，超出了这门课的范围。但如果 AI 团队找你要两个不同的测试集，这很合理，尽量提供给他们。
> Let's dive more deeply into the concept of a test set. This is how AI teams think about data. AI teams group data into two main data sets. The first called the training set and the second called the test set, which we've already talked a bit about. The training set is just a set of pictures together with labels showing whether each of these pictures is of a coffee mug that is okay or defective. So the training set gives examples of both the input A, the pictures of the coffee mugs, as well as the desired output B, whether it's okay or defective. And so given this training set, what a machine learning algorithm will do is learn, in other words, compute or figure out some mapping from A to B so that you now have a piece of software that can take as input the input A and try to figure out what is the appropriate output B. So the training set is the input to the machine learning software that lets it figure out what is this A to B mapping. The second data set that the AI team will use is the test set. And as you've seen, this is just another set of images that's different from the training set, also with the labels provided. The way an AI team will evaluate their learning algorithm's performance is to give the images in the test set to the AI software and see what the AI software outputs. For example, if on these three test set images, the AI software outputs okay for this, okay for this, and also okay for this, then we would say that it got two out of three examples, right? So that's a 66.7% accuracy. In this figure, the training set and test set are both only three pictures. In practice, both of these data sets would be much bigger, of course. And you find that for most problems, the training set is much, much, much bigger than the test set. But you can talk to AI engineers to find out how much data they need for a given problem. Finally, for technical reasons, some AI teams will need not just one, but two different test sets. If you hear AI teams talk about development or dev or validation sets, that's the second test set that they're using. The reasons why they need two test sets is quite technical and beyond the scope of this course. But if an AI team asks you for two different test sets, it's quite reasonable to try to provide that to them.

### 一个重要陷阱：不要期望AI软件达到100%准确率——原因有三：一是机器学习技术本身仍有局限；二是训练数据不够；三是数据本身是脏的，可能标注错误（比如一个看起来完全没问题的绿色杯子被标成了次品）或模糊不清（比如杯子上有个小划痕，有人觉得算合格有人觉得要算次品）；这些问题有些可以改善——多收集数据、清理错误标注、让专家统一标注标准——但很多AI系统即使达不到100%准确率，依然非常有价值

> 结束这个视频之前，有一个陷阱我想劝你避开：不要期望你的 AI 软件达到 100% 准确率。说说为什么。假设这是你的测试集，我再加几个例子。以下是一些 AI 软件不可能做到 100% 准确的原因。第一，今天的机器学习技术虽然非常强大，但仍有局限，它们不是万能的——你可能恰好碰上一个对今天技术来说非常难的问题。第二，数据不够——如果你没有足够的数据，特别是没有足够的训练数据让 AI 软件来学习，那要拿到很高的准确率可能非常困难。第三，数据是脏的——数据有时候标注错误，比如有个绿色咖啡杯看起来完全没问题，但标签写着它是次品，这个错误标签会伤害 AI 软件的性能。数据还可能是模糊的——比如有个咖啡杯这里有一个很小的划痕，有人可能觉得算合格，但有人可能觉得应该算次品。甚至不同专家之间可能都不确定这个咖啡杯该不该通过质检。这些问题有些是可以改善的——比如数据不够可以尝试多收集数据，更多数据往往有帮助；也可以清理标注错误的数据，或者让工厂专家对模糊标注达成更好的共识。但很多 AI 系统即使达不到 100% 准确率，依然非常有价值。所以我劝你跟 AI 工程师讨论：什么水平的准确率是合理的？然后找到既能通过技术尽调又能通过业务尽调的目标——不一定需要 100% 准确率。
> Before wrapping up this video, one pitfall I want to urge you to avoid is expecting 100% accuracy from your AI software. Here's what I mean. Let's say this is your test set, which you've already seen on the last slide. But let me add a few more examples to this test set. Here are some of the reasons it may not be possible for a piece of AI software to be 100% accurate. First, machine learning technology today, despite being very powerful, still has limitations and they just can't do everything. And so you may be working on a problem that is just very difficult, even for today's machine learning technology. Second, insufficient data. If you don't have enough data, specifically if you don't have enough training data for the AI software to learn from, it may be very difficult to get a very high level of accuracy. Third, data is messy and sometimes data can be mislabeled. For example, this green coffee mug here looks perfectly okay to me. So the label of it being a defect looks like an incorrect label and that would hurt the performance of your AI software. And data can also be ambiguous. For example, it looks like this coffee mug has a small scratch over there and it's a pretty small scratch. So maybe we will think it is still okay, but maybe this should actually have been a defect. Or maybe even different experts won't agree if this particular coffee mug is okay and should pass the inspection step. Some of these problems can be ameliorated. For example, if you don't have enough data, maybe you can try to collect more data and more data will often help. Or you can also try to clean up mislabeled data or try to get your factory's experts to come to better agreement about these ambiguous labels. So there are ways to try to make these things better, but a lot of AI systems are incredibly valuable even without achieving 100% accuracy. So I would urge you to discuss with your AI engineers what is a reasonable level of accuracy to try to accomplish and then try to find something that passes both technical diligence as well as business diligence without necessarily needing 100% accuracy.

### 祝贺你完成了本周所有视频！你现在知道了做AI项目是什么感觉、需要什么条件，希望你能开始头脑风暴和探索一些自己的想法；还有一个可选视频介绍AI团队用的技术工具，下周期待见到你——你会学习AI项目如何在更大的公司背景下发挥作用

> 祝贺你完成了本周所有视频！你现在知道了做 AI 项目是什么感觉、需要什么条件。希望你能开始头脑风暴和探索一些自己的想法。还有一个可选视频描述 AI 团队使用的一些技术工具，你想看的话可以看。不管怎样，期待下周见到你，届时你会学到 AI 项目如何融入更大公司的背景中。期待下周见。
> Congratulations on finishing all the videos for this week. You now know what it feels like and what it takes to build an AI project and I hope you start brainstorming and exploring some ideas. There is one more optional video describing some of the technical tools that AI teams use that you can watch if you wish, but either way, I look forward to seeing you next week where you learn how AI projects fit in the context of a bigger company. Look forward to seeing you next week.


---

## 九、AI团队技术工具（可选）——了解AI工程师常用的开源框架（PyTorch、TensorFlow、HuggingFace等）、论文发布平台Arxiv、代码托管平台GitHub；理解CPU/GPU/TPU的区别；以及云端部署、本地部署和边缘部署三种方案

### AI世界非常开放，团队之间经常分享——常用的开源机器学习框架和工具有PyTorch、TensorFlow、HuggingFace、PaddlePaddle、Scikit-learn和R，这些工具帮AI团队高效地构建系统；你也可以在GitHub上搜到大量可下载的AI软件，比如人脸识别软件，注意使用前要确认许可证

> 当你跟 AI 团队合作时，你可能会听到他们提到自己构建 AI 系统用的工具。这个视频我给你介绍一下最常用的 AI 工具的名字和细节，这样你就能更好地理解这些 AI 工程师在做什么。咱们很幸运，今天的 AI 世界非常开放，很多团队会公开分享想法。有很多很棒的机器学习开源工具和框架，很多团队会用来构建他们的系统。如果你听到这些名字——PyTorch、TensorFlow、HuggingFace、PaddlePaddle、Scikit-learn、R——这些都是开源机器学习工具或框架，帮 AI 团队高效工作。很多 AI 技术突破也免费发布在网上的 Arxiv 网站上。我希望其他学术社区也能这样免费分享研究成果，因为我亲眼看到这多大程度上加速了整个 AI 领域的进步。最后，很多团队也会把代码免费分享到网上，最常用的是 GitHub——这已经成了 AI 和其他领域开源软件的事实标准仓库。通过使用恰当许可的开源软件，很多团队可以比从头造所有东西快得多地起步。比如你在 GitHub 上搜人脸识别软件，会找到这样的网页——往下翻能看到非常清楚的描述，介绍这个网站上的软件可以用来识别人的脸，甚至找到人脸的各个部位。网上真的有大量可下载的软件能做各种事——当然，在产品中使用之前要核查许可证；AI 团队会做这件事。很多软件是开源或者宽松许可的。虽然 GitHub 是为工程师建的技术网站，但如果你想的话也可以随意逛逛，看看人们在线发布了什么类型的 AI 软件。
> When you work with AI teams, you may hear them refer to the tools that they're using to build these AI systems. In this video, I want to share with you some details and names of the most commonly used AI tools so that you'll be able to better understand what these AI engineers are doing. We're fortunate that the AI world today is very open and many teams will openly share ideas with each other. There are great machine learning open source tools and frameworks that many teams will use to build their systems. So if you hear any of these, PyTorch, TensorFlow, HuggingFace, PaddlePaddle, Scikit-learn, or R, all of these are open source machine learning tools or frameworks that help AI teams be much more effective. A lot of AI technology breakthroughs are also published freely on the internet on this website called Arxiv. I hope that other academic communities also freely share their research since I've seen firsthand how much this accelerates progress in the whole field of AI. Finally, many teams will also share their code freely on the internet, most commonly on the website called GitHub. This has become the de facto repository for open source software in AI and in other sectors in AI. And by using appropriately licensed open source software, many teams can get going much faster than if they had to build everything from scratch. So for example, if I search online for face recognition software on GitHub, you might find a webpage like this. And if you scroll down, this actually has a pretty good, very readable description of software that is made available on this website for recognizing people's faces and even finding parts of people's faces. There's just a ton of software that is really downloadable for doing all sorts of things on the internet. And just double check the license or AI team would double check the license before using it in a product of course. A lot of the software is open source or is otherwise very permissively licensed for anyone to use. Although GitHub is a technical website built for engineers, if you want, you should feel free to play around GitHub and see what other types of AI software people have released online as well.

### CPU和GPU的区别：CPU是电脑里的中央处理器（Intel和AMD制造），而GPU（图形处理单元）原本是用来画游戏画面的，但几年前人们发现GPU的硬件架构非常适合训练超大规模神经网络——AI社区对算力有无尽的渴求，GPU恰好完美匹配了训练大神经网络所需的计算类型，这也是GPU在深度学习崛起中扮演重要角色的原因；NVIDIA卖了很多GPU，但高通、谷歌（TPU）等公司也在越来越多地制造专用硬件来驱动超大规模神经网络

> 除了这些开源技术工具，你也会经常听到 AI 工程师提到 CPU 和 GPU。这些词的意思：CPU 是你电脑里的计算机处理器——不管是台式机、笔记本还是云端的服务器。CPU 全称是"中央处理单元"，由 Intel、AMD 等公司制造，负责你计算机里大量计算任务。GPU 全称是"图形处理单元"。历史上 GPU 是用来处理图片的——玩视频游戏时，负责绘制精美画面的就是 GPU。但几年前我们发现，原本为图形处理设计的硬件，对于构建非常大的神经网络、非常大的深度学习算法来说也非常非常强大。由于需要训练非常大的深度学习或神经网络系统，AI 社区对更多计算能力有无尽的渴求来训练更大的神经网络。GPU 恰好完美匹配了训练超大神经网络所需的这种计算类型——这就是 GPU 在深度学习崛起中扮演重要角色的原因。NVIDIA 这家公司卖了很多 GPU，但其他公司——包括高通，以及谷歌做自己的 TPU——也越来越多地在制造专用硬件来驱动这些超大规模神经网络。
> In addition to these open source technical tools, you often also hear AI engineers talk about CPUs and GPUs. Here's what these terms mean. A CPU is the computer processor in your computer, whether it's your desktop, your laptop, or a computer server off in the cloud. CPU stands for central processing unit and CPUs are made by Intel and AMD and a few other companies. This does a lot of the computation in your computer. GPU stands for graphics processing unit. Historically, the GPU was made to process pictures. So if you play a video game, it's probably a GPU that is drawing the fancy graphics. But what we found several years ago was that the hardware that was originally built for processing graphics turns out to be very, very powerful for building very large neural networks, very large deep learning algorithms. Given the need to build very large deep learning or very large neural network systems, the AI community has had this insatiable hunger for more and more computational power to train big, grand, bigger neural networks. And GPUs have proved to be a fantastic fit to this type of computation that we need to have done to train very large neural networks. So that's why GPUs are playing a big role in the rise of deep learning. NVIDIA is a company that has been selling many GPUs, but other companies, including Qualcomm, as well as Google, making their own TPUs are increasingly making specialized hardware for powering these very large neural networks.

### 三种部署方案：云端部署——租用亚马逊AWS、微软Azure或谷歌GCP等第三方计算服务器；本地部署——自己购买服务器放在公司内部运行；边缘部署——把处理器直接放在数据采集的地方（比如自动驾驶汽车里的电脑、或者你家智能音箱里的语音识别处理器），优点是响应时间更快、减少需要传输到网络的数据量；每种方案都有各自的优缺点

> 最后你可能会听到"云端"和"本地部署"这两个词。云端部署指你租用计算服务器——比如从亚马逊 AWS、微软 Azure 或谷歌 GCP——用别人的服务器做你的计算。本地部署则是购买自己的计算服务器，在公司内部本地运行。详细探讨这两种方案的利弊超出了这个视频的范围。世界上的趋势是越来越多的走向云端部署，但如果你在线搜索，会找到很多文章讨论云端和本地部署的优缺点。还有最后一个你可能听到的词——边缘部署。如果你在造自动驾驶汽车，没有时间把数据从车上传到云端服务器来判断是否应该停车、再把指令传回车上——计算必须在车内的计算机上完成。这就是边缘部署——把处理器放在数据采集的位置，这样你可以快速处理数据做出决策，无需通过互联网传输数据到别处处理。看看你家的一些智能音箱，这也是边缘部署——有些语音识别任务是由内置在智能音箱里的处理器完成的。边缘部署的主要优势是可以提高系统的响应速度，同时减少需要通过互联网传输的数据量。但边缘、云端、本地部署之间也有很多利弊，你可以在线搜索了解更多。
> Finally, you might hear about cloud versus on-premises or, for short, on-prem deployments. Cloud deployments refer to if you rent compute servers, such as from Amazon's AWS or Microsoft's Azure or Google's GCP, in order to use someone else's servers to do your computation. Whereas an on-prem deployment means buying your own compute servers and running the servers locally in your own company. A detailed exploration of the pros and cons of these two options is beyond the scope of this video. A lot of the world is moving to cloud deployments. But if you search online, you find many articles talking about the pros and cons of cloud versus on-prem deployments. There is one last term you might hear about, which is edge deployments. If you are building a self-driving car, there's not enough time to send data from a self-driving car to a cloud server to decide if you should stop the car or not, and then send that message back to a self-driving car. So the computation has to happen usually in a computer right there inside the car. That's called an edge deployment, where you put a processor right where the data is collected so that you can process the data and make a decision very quickly without needing to transmit the data over the internet to be processed somewhere else. If you look at some of the smart speakers in your home as well, this too is an edge deployment where some, not all, but some of the speech recognition tasks is done by a processor that is built in right there into the smart speaker that is inside your home. The main advantage of edge deployments is it can increase response time of the system and also reduce the amount of data you need to send over the network. But there are many pros and cons as well about edge versus cloud versus on-prem deployments that you can also search online to read more about.

### 感谢你看了这个可选视频——了解这些AI工程师常用的技术工具之后，再跟他们交流时你就能更好地理解他们在说什么了；期待下周见到你

> 感谢你看了这个关于 AI 工程师技术工具的可选视频。希望当他们提到这些工具时，你对它们的含义有了更好的理解。期待下周见到你。
> Thanks for finishing this optional video on the technical tools that AI engineers use. Hopefully when you hear them refer to some of these tools, you start to have a better sense of what they mean. I look forward to seeing you next week.

### 前两周你学了AI是什么以及怎么建AI项目，这周我们从单个项目放大到公司层面——不管是营利企业、非营利组织还是政府机构都适用；虽然有些内容听起来像CEO级别的讨论，但别被吓到，每个人都需要了解这些，因为你的公司要花两三年才能真正擅长AI，不是只做一个AI项目，而是做一系列有价值项目的序列

This week, we'll look at the projects we talked about already and talk about how projects fit in the context of a company, whether it's a for-profit, non-profit, or even within a government entity. For the sake of concreteness, I'll talk about building AI for companies but equally apply no matter what type of organization you're in. Now, in case some of what you hear this week sounds like CEO level talk, don't be intimidated. This is actually useful for everyone to know that once the hope, your company will help your organization improve using AI. And it does take maybe two or three years for a company to become good at AI, to embrace not just one AI project, but a sequence of valuable AI projects and become much more effective. But what I hope to do this week is help you paint a vision for where an organization can go over a longer period of time like that, but also end the week with very concrete short steps you can take right away. So let's get started.

### 这周的四个核心主题：第一，复杂AI产品的案例研究——上周期你看到的是单个机器学习或数据科学模块，这周你会看到多个模块如何组合在一起构建更复杂的AI产品，比如智能音箱和自动驾驶汽车；第二，AI团队的主要角色——如果你的公司将来要建一个几十甚至上百人的大AI团队，这些人分别做什么；第三，AI转型指南——怎么让你的公司不只是做一两个有价值的项目，而是整个公司都擅长AI，从而变得更有价值更高效；第四，如何立刻迈出在公司的第一步

> 具体来说，这周你会看到这些主题。第一，复杂 AI 产品的案例研究——上周期你看到的是单个机器学习或数据科学模块，这周你会看到多个模块怎么组合在一起构建更复杂的 AI 产品，比如智能音箱和自动驾驶汽车。第二，你会学到 AI 团队的主要角色——如果将来你的公司要建一个大的 AI 团队，可能有几十甚至几百人，这些人分别做什么？我们开始描绘组建 AI 团队的路线图。第三，你还会学到 AI 转型指南——怎么帮助你的公司擅长 AI，不只是做一两个有价值的项目，而是让整个公司都擅长 AI，从而变得更有价值、更高效。第四，虽然有些步骤可能需要几年才能完成，这周视频结尾会给你非常具体的建议——怎么立刻迈出在你的公司做 AI 的第一步。除了这些主要主题，最后还有几个可选视频，带你概览主要的 AI 应用领域和技术。
