## 一、主要AI应用领域概览——从图像/视频、语言、语音、生成式AI到机器人，AI已成功应用于多个领域；监督学习不仅在非结构化数据（图片、语音、文本）上大放异彩，在结构化数据（表格数据）上创造的经济价值至少同等巨大，只是后者不如图片识别那样"看得见"所以媒体报道少很多

### 计算机视觉的几种核心任务：图像分类/物体识别是输入图片输出"这是什么"（比如猫、特定品种的花或食物），其中人脸识别是重要分支——用户先注册几张面部照片，系统就能判断新照片是不是同一个人从而决定是否开门/解锁手机/电脑；物体检测不是识别整张图而是找出图中物体的位置和类型——比如自动驾驶中不光判断有没有车和行人，还要说出它们分别在图片的哪个位置；图像分割更精细——不是画方框而是为每个像素标注属于哪个物体，能画出精确轮廓，比如医学影像中精确分割出肝脏、心脏、骨骼的位置

> AI今天已成功应用于图像和视频数据、语言数据、语音数据等多个领域。这个视频来概览一下这些不同应用领域，希望能启发你把这些技术用在自己的项目上。先说计算机视觉的几大任务。图像分类/物体识别——输入一张图片，告诉我们里面是什么，比如这是一只猫。不仅能识别猫，还能识别特定品种的花、特定类型的食物，这个能力已经应用在各种场景中。人脸识别是图像分类的一个重要分支——用户先注册一两张面部照片让AI知道你长什么样，有了新照片后系统判断这是不是你，然后决定是否开门、解锁手机或电脑。当然，人脸识别应该只在尊重个人隐私的前提下使用。物体检测是另一种任务：不光是识别物体，而是检测物体在不在、在哪个位置。自动驾驶中我们看到AI系统输入一张图片，不只告诉我们有没有车、有没有行人，还要告诉我们这些车和行人在图中的具体位置。物体检测既可以输出"这里有一辆车、一个行人"，也可以输出"这张图里什么都没检测到"。图像分割更进一步——输入一张图，不仅告诉你车和行人在哪，还为每个像素标注它属于车还是行人。所以它不是画方框，而是画出精确的轮廓。比如在医学影像中，图像分割算法可以看X光片，仔细分割出肝脏在哪、心脏在哪、骨骼在哪。

> AI today is being successfully applied to image and video data, to language data, to speech data, to many other areas. In this video, you see a survey of AI applied to these different application areas. And I hope that this may spark off some ideas of how you might be able to use these techniques someday for your own projects as well. Let's take a look. One of the major successes of deep learning has been computer vision. Let's take a look at some examples of computer vision applications. Image classification and object recognition refer to taking as input a picture like that and telling us what is in this picture, in this case, if you're a cat. Rather than just recognizing cats, I've seen AI algorithms able to recognize specific types of flowers, AI able to recognize specific types of food, and the ability to take as input a picture and classify it into what type of object it is, is being used in all sorts of applications. One specific type of image classification that has had a lot of traction is face recognition. This is how face recognition systems today work. A user might register one or more pictures of their face to show the AI what they look like. Given a new image, the AI system can then say, is this the same person? Is this you? Or is this a different person so that they can decide if they should unlock the door or unlock the cell phone, unlock the laptop, or something else based on the identity of the person. Of course, I hope face recognition will only be used in ways that respect individuals' We'll talk more about AI and society next week as well. A different type of computer vision algorithm is called object detection. So rather than just trying to classify or recognize an object, you're trying to detect if the object appears at all. For example, in building a self-driving car, we've seen how an AI system can take as input a picture like this and not just tell us yes or no is there a car, yes or no is there a pedestrian. But actually tell us the position of the cause as well as the positions of the pedestrians in this image. An object detection algorithm can also take as input a picture like that and just say, no, I'm not finding any cause or any pedestrians in that image. So rather than taking a picture and labeling the whole image, which is image classification, instead an object detection algorithm will take us and put an image and tell us where in the picture different objects are, as well as what are the types of those objects. Image segmentation takes us one step further. Given an image like this, an image segmentation algorithm may output that, where it tells us not just where the cause and pedestrians, but tells us for every single pixel, is this pixel part of this car or is this pixel part of a pedestrian? So it doesn't just draw rectangles around the objects it detects, instead it draws very precise boundaries around the objects that it finds. So in reading x-rays, for example, it would be an image segmentation algorithm that could look at an x-ray scan or some other image of a human body and carefully segment out where is the liver or where is the heart or where is the bone in this image.

### 计算机视觉还能处理视频——比如"跟踪"技术不仅检测视频中运动员的位置，还追踪他们随时间移动的轨迹（红框下面的小尾巴就是运动轨迹）；语言/NLP领域的核心任务包括：文本分类（如垃圾邮件过滤、根据商品描述自动归类到"手机"或"服装"）、情感识别（输入"食物不错"判断是四星好评，输入"服务烂透了"判断是一星差评）、信息检索（最典型的例子就是网页搜索）、命名实体识别（在一句话中自动提取人名如"Queen Elizabeth II""Sir Paul McCartney"、地名、公司名、电话号码、国家名等）、以及机器翻译（输入日语->输出英语）

> 计算机视觉还可以处理视频。一个应用是"跟踪"——不只检测视频中的跑步者，而是跨时间段追踪他们的运动轨迹，红框下面的小尾巴显示了算法如何追踪不同人跑过几秒。如果你用摄像机追踪野生动物比如鸟群，跟踪算法也能帮你追踪单只鸟在视频帧间的飞行路径。接下来讲自然语言处理（NLP）——AI理解人类日常交流的语言。NLP的几个核心任务：文本分类——输入一段文字（比如邮件），判断它的类别，比如是不是垃圾邮件。有些网站的文本分类会自动判断商品属于什么品类：你写"卖二手手机"它自动归到"手机/电子产品"；你写"卖新T恤"它自动归到"服装"。情感识别是文本分类的一种——输入一条餐厅评论"food was good"自动判断大概四星好评；输入"service was horrible"判断大概一星差评。信息检索——搜索是最典型的信息检索例子：你输入文字查询，AI帮你找到相关文档；很多公司内部也有信息检索系统帮员工搜索公司内部文档。命名实体识别——比如句子"Queen Elizabeth II knighted Sir Paul McCartney for the service of music at the Buckingham Palace"，命名实体识别系统能提取出两个人物名，也能提取所有地名、公司名、电话号码、国家名等。机器翻译——比如输入日语"AI wa denki desu"，翻译成"AI is the new electricity"。

> Computer vision can also deal with video and one application of that is tracking. In this example, rather than just detecting the runners in this video, it is also tracking in a video where the runners are moving over time. So those little tails below the red boxes show how the algorithm is tracking different people running across several seconds in a video. So the ability to track people and cause and maybe other moving objects in the video helps the computer figure out where things are going. If you're using a video camera to track wildlife, for example, say birds flying around, a tracking algorithm would also be able to help track individual birds flying across the frames of your video. These are some of the major areas of computer vision and perhaps some of them will be useful for your projects. AI and deep learning specifically is also making a lot of progress in natural language processing. Natural language processing or NLP refers to AI understanding natural language, meaning the language that you and I might use to communicate with each other. One example is text classification, where the job of the AI is to input a piece of text such as an email and tell us what is the class or what is the category of this email, such as is this spam or non-spam email. There are also websites that would input a product description. For example, you might write, I have a second hand cell phone for sale and automatically figure out what is the product category in which to list this product. So that would go under cell phones or electronics. Or if you write, I have a new t-shirt to sell, then it would list it automatically under clothing. One type of text classification that has had a lot of attention is sentiment recognition. For example, a sentiment recognition algorithm can take as input a review like this of a restaurant, the food was good, and automatically try to tell us how many stars this review might get. The food was good, it's a pretty good review, maybe that's a four out of five star review. Whereas if someone writes service was horrible, then the sentiment recognition algorithm should be able to tell us that this corresponds maybe to a one star review. A second type of NLP or natural language processing is information retrieval. Search is perhaps the best known example of information retrieval where you type in the text query and you want the AI to help you find relevant documents. Many corporations will also have internal information retrieval systems where you might have an interface to help you search just within your company's center documents for something relevant to a query that you might enter. Name entity recognition is another natural language processing technology. As illustrated with an example, say you have this sentence and you want to find all the people names in this sentence. So Queen Elizabeth II is a person, Sir Paul McCartney is a person. So in the sentence, Queen Elizabeth II knight Sir Paul McCartney for the service of music at the Buckingham Palace, it would be a name entity recognition system to define all the people's names in a sentence like this. If you want to find all the location names, all the place names in a sentence like that, a name entity recognition system can also do so. Name entity recognition systems can also automatically extract names of companies, phone numbers, names of countries. And so if you have a large document collection and you want to find automatically all the company names or all the company names that occur together, all the people's names, then a name entity recognition system would be the tool you could use to do that. Another major AI application area is machine translation. So for example, if you see this sentence in Japanese, AI war, or that don't know, then hopefully machine translation system can input that and output the translation. AI is the new electricity. The four items on this slide, text classification, information retrieval, name entity recognition, and machine translation are four major categories of useful NLP applications.

### 语音/音频AI应用：语音识别（语音转文字）近年来的巨大进步主要靠深度学习驱动，通过分析音频波形图（横轴时间、纵轴空气压力变化）来推断说的是哪些词；触发词检测是语音识别的一个特例（如Alexa、Hey Google）；说话人识别验证说话人身份；语音合成（文字转语音/TTS）把文本句子转成越来越像真人说话的音频——比如输入"the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog"（这个包含全部26个英文字母的句子）就能生成自然语音

> 现代AI尤其是深度学习完全改变了软件处理音频/语音的方式。语音在计算机里怎么表示？这是一段音频波形图——横轴是时间，竖轴是麦克风记录到的空气压力的微小且快速变化。这个图显示了有人说出"machine learning"时，气压是怎么随时间迅速变化的。语音识别（语音转文字）的任务就是输入这样的波形图，推断出说的是哪些词——近年来的巨大进步主要靠深度学习驱动。触发词/唤醒词检测是语音识别的一种特例，你在前面视频里看到了AI系统检测"Alexa""Hey Google"这类触发词。说话人识别是另一个专门的语音问题——听一个人说话判断说话人的身份，就像人脸识别通过照片验证身份，说话人识别通过声音验证身份。最后，语音合成也叫文字转语音（TTS），输入一句文字把它变成音频文件。有趣的是，文字转语音常简写TTS，但语音转文字却很少被简写成STT。举个例子：输入句子"the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog"——这是NLP人喜欢用的一句话，因为它包含了从A到Z全部26个字母——TTS系统会输出一段自然语音。现代TTS系统越来越像真人说话了。

> Modern AI, specifically deep learning, has also completely transformed how software processes audio data, such as speech. How is speech represented in a computer? This is an audio waveform of one of my friends saying the phrase machine learning. The X axis here is time, and the vertical axis is what a microphone is recording. What a microphone is recording is little variations, very rapid variations in air pressure, which your ear and your brain then interpret as sound. And this plot shows as a function of time, the horizontal axis, how the air pressure is changing very, very rapidly in response to someone saying the word machine learning. The problem of speech recognition, also known as speech to text, is the problem of taking as input a plot like this and figuring out what were the words that someone said. A lot of speech recognition's recent progress has been due to deep learning. One particular type of speech recognition is trigger word detection or wake word detection. And you saw this in the earlier video with having an AI system detect a trigger word or wake word, such as Alexa or hey Google or hey device. Speaker ID is a specialized speech problem where the task is to listen to someone speak and figure out the identity of the speaker. Just as face recognition helps verify your identity by taking a picture, speaker ID can also help verify your identity by listening to you speak. Finally, speech synthesis, also called text to speech or TTS, is also getting a lot of traction. Text to speech is a problem of inputting a sentence written in text and turning that into an audio file. Interestingly, whereas text to speech is often abbreviated TTS, I don't often see speech to text abbreviated STT. One quick example, let's take the sentence, the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog. This is a fun sentence that you often see NLP people use because this sentence contains every single letter from A to Z. So that's A, B, C, all the way up to X, Y and Z. You can check all 26 letters appear in this sentence. Some letters appear more than once. If you pass the sentence into a TTS system, then you might get an audio output like this. The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog. Modern TTS systems are increasingly sounding more and more natural and human like.

### 生成式AI能生成文本、图像和音频三类高质量内容：大语言模型能做文字写作、摘要、编辑和聊天——输入一段提示词（prompt）如"为巧克力冰淇淋产品线起一些搞笑创意名字"就能生成创意内容，写好prompt正成为许多岗位的有用技能，几乎所有知识工作者都能通过在工作流程中使用大语言模型获得至少一些生产力提升；图像生成方面，Midjourney/DALL-E/Adobe Firefly/Stable Diffusion等工具通过从互联网上学习数百万张图片学会了根据文字描述生成全新图片；音频生成方面，Stable Audio和Meta AudioCraft等能通过文字提示生成音乐和音效

> 生成式AI是一系列能生成高质量内容的AI系统，具体包括文字、图像和音频。先说文本生成：大语言模型擅长文字生成，包括从零写作、写摘要、文字编辑（改进语法和清晰度等）以及聊天。你输入一段提示词比如"suggest funny creative names for a line of chocolate ice cream"，模型就能生成各种创意名字。这输入文本叫"prompt"，写好prompt来获得你想要的输出正成为许多工作中很有用的技能。吴恩达说把大语言模型当作头脑风暴伙伴让他的生产力提高了，如果你能有效写prompt，工作中和个人生活中都会很有用。实际上他觉得大语言模型已经到了一个阶段——几乎所有知识工作者都能通过在日常工作中使用大模型获得至少一些生产力提升。图像生成方面，Midjourney、DALL-E、Adobe Firefly、Stable Diffusion等工具通过从互联网上学习数百万张图片，学会了根据文字描述生成图片。你输入"一个紫色的友好的机器人在吃冰淇淋"，它就能生成匹配的高质量图片。音频生成方面，Stable Audio和Meta AudioCraft可以根据文字提示生成音乐和音效——比如输入"140 BPM的鼓独奏"就能生成一段对应的音轨。

> You've heard me mention generative AI a few times so far in this course. Generative AI is a collection of AI systems that can produce high quality media, specifically text images or audio. Let's take a look at these applications of generative AI in more detail. Large language models are great at text generation tasks, including writing content from scratch, writing summaries, copy editing, that is editing text improve grammar, clarity and so on, and chatting. For example, you can give one of these models an instruction like suggest the funny creative names for a line of chocolate ice cream and the model will generate some creative sounding names like these. Note that this input text here is known as a prompt and writing prompts to generate the output you want is becoming a useful skill for many jobs. I find that having a large language model as a brainstorming partner makes me more productive. If you're able to write prompts effectively, perhaps you find it a useful tool at work or in your personal life as well. In fact, I think large language models are now at a point where almost all knowledge workers can get at least a bit of a productivity boost by learning and using them in their day-to-day workflow. Generative AI can also create new images from scratch. Software like Maggiorni, Dali, Adobe Firefly and Stable Diffusion have learned how to generate images from text descriptions by learning from millions of images on the Internet. So with one of these image generation models, you can input an example prompt like a purple friendly robot eating ice cream and the model would generate a high quality image for you that matches your prompt. That would be a cute robot. Lastly, Gen2 of AI is also capable of generating audio. Previously, we saw how speech synthesis models can convert text to speech audio. Software also exists like Stable AI's Stable Audio or Meta's AudioCraft. They can generate music and sound effects from a text prompt. So by writing a prompt like drum solo 140 bpm or beats per minute, you can use a music generation model to create an audio track like this one. So, Gen2 of AI is capable of creating several types of content. This is affecting many industry sectors and we'll learn more about the impact of AI, including Gen2 of AI on jobs next week.

### 机器人AI三大任务：感知（perception）——通过摄像头/雷达/LiDAR理解周围世界；运动规划（motion planning）——为机器人找到行驶路径和速度；控制（control）——把路径转成电机的具体指令如方向盘角度、油门和刹车力度；最后强调：监督学习在结构化数据（表格数据）上创造的经济价值至少和在非结构化数据上一样大——但因为"识别一只猫"的图像远比表格数据更直观、更好理解、更有故事性，媒体报道严重偏向非结构化数据的AI应用，而忽略了同样创造巨大价值的结构化数据AI

> AI也在机器人领域广泛应用。自动驾驶车就是一个例子。机器人学中，"感知"指根据传感器（摄像头、雷达、LiDAR）弄清楚周围世界是什么样的——右边图上显示的是自动驾驶车的3D激光扫描以及检测到的周围其他车辆。"运动规划"指为机器人找一条要跟随的路径——比如车要左转，运动规划器就规划出转弯的路径和速度。"控制"指把路径翻译成发送给电机的指令——方向盘电机、油门和刹车电机——让车平稳地沿规划路径行驶。这里着重讲的是软件/AI层面的机器人学，当然硬件的构建也有大量重要工作在进行。除了以上主要应用领域，机器学习还有一个非常广泛的应用——结构化数据。视频里的例子大多是非结构化数据（图像、音频、文字），但机器学习在结构化数据（也就是表格数据）上的应用至少同等广泛。只不过因为非结构化数据如图片对人类来说直观易懂——大家一看"AI识别了一只猫"就很有共鸣——所以大众媒体大量报道AI处理非结构化数据的进展，而对结构化数据上的AI报道少很多。结构化数据也往往更"公司专属"、更难被外人理解和描述。但不管非结构化还是结构化数据上的AI，都在创造巨大的经济价值。希望这个应用领域概览让你看到了AI今天成功应用的广阔范围，甚至激发你想出一些怎么把这些技术用在自己项目上的点子。下个视频讲不同AI技术——监督学习之外还有其他什么技术？无监督学习是什么？强化学习又是什么？咱们继续。

> AI is also applied to many applications in robotics and you've already seen one example in the self-driving car. In robotics, the term perception means figuring out what's in the world around you based on the senses you have, be it cameras or radar or LiDAR. Shown on the right is the 3D laser scan or the LiDAR scan of a self-driving car as well as the vehicles that this self-driving car in the middle has detected in the vicinity of your car. Motion planning refers to finding a path for your robot to follow. So if your car wants to make a left turn, the motion planner might plan a path as well as a speed for the car to make a left turn that way. And finally, control refers to sending commands to the motors, such as your steering wheel motor as well as your gas pedal and brake motors, in order to make the car smoothly follow the path that you want. On this slide, I focus on the software and the AI aspects of robotics. Of course, there's also a lot of important work being done to build hardware for robotics as well. A lot of the work of AI on perception, motion planning and control has focused on the software rather than the hardware of robotics. In addition to these major application areas, machine learning is also very broadly used. The examples you've seen in this video may need to unstructured data, such as images, audio and text. Machine learning is applied at least as much to structured data, and that means these tables of data, some of which you saw in the earlier videos. But because unstructured data, such as images, is so easy for humans to understand, there's something very universal, very easy for any person to understand and empathize with when we talk about an AI system that recognizes a cat. And so the popular press tends to cover AI progress on unstructured data much more than it does AI on structured data. Structured data also tends to be more specific to a single company, and so it's harder for people to write about or understand. But AI on structured data or machine learning on structured data is creating tremendous economic value today, as well as AI on unstructured data. I hope this survey of AI application areas gives you a sense of the wide range of data that AI is successfully applied to today, and maybe this will even inspire you to think of how some of these application areas may be useful for your own projects. Now so far, the one AI technique we're spending most time talking about is supervised learning. That means learning input output or A to B mappings from labeled data, where you give the AI system both A and B. But that's not the only AI technique out there. In fact, the term supervised learning almost invites a question of what is unsupervised learning, or you might also have heard from media articles from the news about reinforcement learning. So what are all these other techniques? In the next video, the final optional video for this week, we'll do a survey of AI techniques, and I hope that through that, maybe you'll see if some of these other AI techniques and supervised learning could be useful for your projects as well. Let's go on to the final optional video for the week.


---

## 一、主要AI技术概览——除监督学习外，还有无监督学习、迁移学习、强化学习、GANs和知识图谱等技术；监督学习虽然创造了最大经济价值，但其他技术各有独特优势，知道它们的存在能帮你更好地跟AI工程师交流

### 无监督学习（unsupervised learning）最典型的例子是聚类——比如开一家薯片专卖店，根据每个顾客每次买多少包薯片以及每包的均价，聚类算法能自动把顾客分成两组：一群是买便宜薯片但买很多的（如大学城附近的大学生），另一群是买得少但舍得买贵薯片的（如职场专业人士）；这能帮你在不同市场细分做差异化营销；无监督学习和监督学习的根本区别在于：监督学习需要给出"B"这个正确答案，而无监督学习只给AI一堆数据让它自己发现有意义的结构，不需要预先知道市场细分是什么；吴恩达做过的著名例子是"Google Cat"——让无监督学习算法观看大量YouTube视频自己发现规律，结果它在没被预先告知"猫"这个概念的情况下，仅通过看大量YouTube视频就自己学会了认猫

> 今天AI和机器学习技术很多，虽然监督学习（学A到B的映射）在经济上是最有价值的，但还有很多其他技术值得了解。先说无监督学习——最经典的例子是聚类。假设你开一家薯片专卖店，你收集顾客数据，记录每人每次买多少包薯片以及每包均价。你有些便宜的薯片和高端贵价的薯片。聚类算法看到数据后会"说"：你数据里有两个群体——一群顾客倾向于买便宜薯片但一次买很多包（比如店铺开在大学附近，大学生买便宜薯片但量大）；另一群顾客买得少但买的都是贵的。聚类算法自动把数据分成两个簇甚至更多簇，这常用于市场细分，帮你发现你有一个大学生群体和一个职场精英群体，从而做不同的营销策略。为什么叫"无监督"？因为监督学习是告诉AI"这是A的正确输出B"，而无监督学习不告诉AI你想要什么输出——只给AI一堆数据，让它自己去找到数据中有趣、有意义的东西。聚类算法事先不知道存在"大学生群体"和"职场精英群体"，它只是自己去发现不同市场细分。吴恩达自己做过一个稍微有点"出名"的无监督学习例子叫"Google Cat"——他让团队在一个非常庞大的YouTube视频集上跑无监督学习算法，问算法"你在YouTube视频里发现了什么？"结果算法发现的诸多事物中就有"猫"——因为典型的YouTube上确实有大量猫的视频。令人惊叹的是，算法并没有被预先告知"去发现猫"，但通过观看大量YouTube视频，它自己就学会了"猫"这个概念，还能生成右边图中那种对"猫"概念的内部表示（可视化）。

> There are a lot of AI and machine learning techniques today. And while supervised learning, that is learning A to B mappings, is the most valuable one, at least economically today, there are many other techniques that we're of knowing about. Let's take a look. The best known example of unsupervised learning is clustering. Here's an example. Let's say you run a grocery store that specializes in selling potato chips and you collect data on different customers and keep track of how many different packets of potato chips a single customer buys as well as what's the average price per packet that person paid for their potato chips. So you sell some low end cheaper potato chips as well as some high end more expensive packets of potato chips and different people may buy different numbers of potato chip packets in a typical trip to your grocery store. Given data like this, a clustering algorithm will say that it looks like you have two clusters in your data. Some of your customers tend to buy relatively inexpensive potato chips, but by a lot of packets. If your grocery store is near a college campus, for example, you may find a lot of college students that are buying cheaper potato chip packets, but they should buy a lot of them. And there's a second cluster in this data of a different group of shoppers that buy fewer packets of potato chips, but buy more expensive packets. A clustering algorithm looks at data like this and automatically groups the data into two clusters or more clusters. And it's commonly used for market segmentation and will help you discover things like that if you have a college student audience that buys a certain type of potato chips and a working professional audience that buys few potato chips, but is willing to pay more. And this could help you market differently to these market segments. The reason this is called unsupervised learning is the following. Whereas supervised learning algorithms learn an A to B mapping, and you have to tell the algorithm what is the output B that you want. A unsupervised learning algorithm doesn't tell the AI system exactly what it wants. Instead, it gives the AI system a bunch of data, such as this customer data, and it tells the AI to find something interesting in the data, find something meaningful in the data. And in this case, a clustering algorithm doesn't know in advance that there's a college student demographic and a working professional demographic. Instead, it just tries to find what are the different market segments without being told in advance what they are. So unsupervised learning algorithms, given data without any specific desired output labels, without a target label B, can automatically find something interesting about the data. One example of unsupervised learning that I have worked on was the slightly infamous Google Cat. In this project, I had my team run a unsupervised learning algorithm on a very large set of YouTube videos. And we asked the algorithm, tell us what you find in YouTube videos. And one of the many things it found in YouTube videos was cats, because somewhat stereotypically, YouTube apparently has a lot of cat videos. But it was a remarkable result that without telling it in advance that it should find cats, the AI system, the unsupervised learning algorithm was able to discover the concept of a cat all by itself, just by watching a lot of YouTube videos and discovering that, boy, there are a lot of cats in YouTube videos. It's hard to visualize exactly what an AI algorithm is thinking sometimes, but this picture on the right is a visualization of the concept of a cat that the system had learned.

### 监督学习的核心痛点是太依赖大量标注数据——要训练系统认咖啡杯可能需要一万张咖啡杯图片，但世界上没有哪个父母会给孩子指一万个不同的咖啡杯来教"什么是杯子"；所以AI研究者对无监督学习寄予厚望，希望未来AI能用更接近人类和生物的方式、用更少的标注数据来高效学习——但今天的生物大脑工作机制我们几乎完全不知道，要实现这个愿景还需要重大的技术突破；目前无监督学习在自然语言处理等特定领域已有实际价值（如提升网页搜索质量），但整体经济价值仍远小于监督学习

> 监督学习虽然极其强大，但有一个痛点：它需要海量的标注数据。比如你要用监督学习训练系统识别咖啡杯，可能需要给它一千张甚至一万张咖啡杯的图片。真的太多了！当父母的都知道，世界上没有任何父母——再爱孩子再用心——会指着10,000个不同的咖啡杯来教自己孩子"什么是咖啡杯"。今天的AI系统需要比人类小孩（或大多数动物）多得多的标注数据才能学习。所以AI研究者对无监督学习抱有很大期望——希望未来AI能通过更接近人类、更接近生物的方式，用更少的标注数据来高效学习。不过说实话，我们对生物大脑的工作原理几乎一无所知，所以实现这个愿景需要AI领域的重大突破——这些突破我们现在还不知道怎么做。但很多研究者对无监督学习的未来很有信心。即便说这些，无监督学习今天已经有价值了，在自然语言处理的某些具体应用中（比如提升网页搜索质量），无监督学习帮助很大。但整体经济价值仍比监督学习小不少。

> Even though supervised learning is an incredibly valuable and powerful technique, one of the criticisms of supervised learning is that it just needs so much labeled data. For example, if you're trying to use supervised learning to get an AI system to recognize coffee mugs, then you may give it a thousand pictures of coffee mugs or 10,000 pictures of coffee mugs. And that's just a lot of pictures of coffee mugs we would be giving our AI systems. For those of you that are parents, I can almost guarantee you that no parent on this planet, no matter how loving and caring, has ever pointed out 10,000 unique coffee mugs to their children, to try to teach their children what is a coffee mug. So AI systems today require much more labeled data to learn than with a human child or than with most animals, which is why AI researchers hold a lot of hope out for unsupervised learning as a way maybe in the future for AI to learn much more effectively in a more human-like way, a more biological-like way, for much less labeled data. Now, we have pretty much no idea how the biological brain works, and so to realize this vision, we'll take major breakthroughs in AI that none of us know yet today how to realize that many of us hold a lot of hope for the future of unsupervised learning. Having said that unsupervised learning is valuable today, there are some specific applications in natural language processing, for example, where unsupervised learning actually helps improve the quality of web search quite a bit, for example, but the value today of unsupervised learning is still a lot smaller than the value created through supervised learning.

### 迁移学习（transfer learning）是非常实用但被媒体低估的技术——它让你用一个任务（如车辆检测，有10万张图片）学到的知识来帮助另一个数据少得多的任务（如在只有少量图片的新城市中检测高尔夫球车），因为从任务A学到的关于"车轮长什么样""车辆怎么移动"等知识也对任务B有用；今天很多计算机视觉系统都是基于迁移学习构建的，这对性能提升非常关键

> 接下来讲迁移学习。举个例子：你训练了一个自动驾驶系统检测普通车辆（用10万张图片），然后把车部署到一个新城市，那里满街都是高尔夫球车，你需要也检测高尔夫球车。但你在这个新城市才刚开始运营，只有很少量的高尔夫球车图片。迁移学习就是让你从任务A（车辆检测，大数据集）学到的知识，帮助你在任务B（高尔夫球车检测，小数据集）上也做得不错——因为从第一个任务中学到的"车辆长什么样、轮子长什么样、车怎么动"等知识对检测高尔夫球车也有用。迁移学习没有得到太多媒体报道，但它是今天最有价值的技术之一。很多计算机视觉系统都是基于迁移学习构建的，对性能提升贡献巨大。

> Another important AI technique is transfer learning. Let's look at an example. Let's say you built a self-driving car and you have trained your AI system to detect cars, but you then deploy your vehicle to a new city and somehow this new city has a lot of golf cars driving around, and so you need to also build a golf cart detection system. You may have trained your car detection system with a lot of images, say 100,000 images, but in this new city where you just started operating, you may have a much smaller number of images of golf carts. Transfer learning is the technology that lets you learn from a task A, such as car detection, and use the knowledge to help you on a different task B, such as golf car detection. Where transfer learning really shines is if having learned from a very large data set of car detection, task A, you can now do pretty well on golf car detection even though you have a much smaller golf cart data set, because some of the knowledge it has learned from the first task of what the vehicles look like, what the wheels look like, how the vehicles move, maybe that will be useful also for golf cart detection. Transfer learning doesn't get a lot of press, but it is one of the very valuable techniques in AI today. And for example, many computer vision systems are built using transfer learning, and this makes a big difference to their performance.

### 强化学习（reinforcement learning）就像训练宠物狗——让AI自由探索，做对了就奖励（"good dog"给正数）、做错了就惩罚（"bad dog"给负数），AI自动学习怎么最大化奖励；吴恩达用这种方法训练斯坦福的自动驾驶直升机——在模拟器里让它自由飞，飞得好给正奖励，坠毁给负奖励，最终学会了包括倒飞在内的各种飞行动作（他用"good helicopter / bad helicopter"来比喻）；强化学习在游戏领域也有很多成功应用（如AlphaGo下围棋、各种视频游戏），但弱点是需要海量数据——玩游戏可以通过不断自我对弈产生无限数据、飞直升机有模拟器可以无限试飞；在缺乏精确模拟器的场景下，强化学习很难获取足够数据；尽管媒体关注度极高，但今天强化学习创造的经济价值远小于监督学习

> 强化学习你可能听说过。怎么理解呢？以斯坦福的自动驾驶直升机为例——直升机装了GPS、加速度计和指南针始终知道自己的位置。你要写一个程序让它自己飞行，用监督学习的A到B映射很难，因为你很难精确描述"在某个特定位置，飞直升机的最优方式是什么"。强化学习提供了一种不同方案——吴恩达把它比作训练宠物狗：让狗自由行动，表现好就表扬"good dog!"，做坏事就说"bad dog!"，长期下来狗学会多做"good dog"的事、避开"bad dog"的事。强化学习把同样原则用到直升机：在模拟器里让它自由飞行（坠毁也不会伤人），飞得好就表扬"good helicopter!"，坠毁就说"bad helicopter!"，AI的任务就是学会怎么飞能获得更多"good helicopter"奖励、更少"bad helicopter"惩罚。技术上，强化学习算法用一个奖励信号告诉AI什么时候做得好（给一个大的正数）、什么时候做得差（给一个负数），AI自动学会采取能最大化累积奖励的行为。吴恩达展示了一段视频——斯坦福自动驾驶直升机在强化学习控制下飞行，甚至能倒飞——他们给了一个奖励信号奖励直升机倒着飞！这是当时世界上最先进的自动驾驶直升机之一。除了机器人控制，强化学习也在游戏领域大放异彩：奥赛罗棋、西洋跳棋、国际象棋、围棋（AlphaGo），以及各种视频游戏。但强化学习的一个弱点是需要海量数据——玩视频游戏时AI可以无限地自己跟自己玩生成数据，飞直升机时有模拟器可以长时间试飞，但如果是在没有精确模拟器的真实场景中就很难获取足够数据了。所以尽管强化学习在媒体上获得了大量关注，但至少在今天，它创造的经济价值远小于监督学习。不过未来可能会有突破改变这一点。

> You may also have heard of a technique called reinforcement learning. So what is reinforcement learning? Let me illustrate with another example. This is a picture of the Stanford autonomous helicopter. So it's instrumented with GPS accelerometers and a compass, so it always knows where it is. And let's say you want to write a program to make it fly by itself. It's hard to use supervised learning, input to output, A to B mappings, because it's very difficult to specify what is the optimal way, what is the best way to fly the helicopter when it's in a certain given position. Reinforcement learning offers a different solution. I think of reinforcement learning as similar to how you might train a pet dog to behave. My family, when I was growing up, had a pet dog. So how do you train a pet dog to behave yourself? Well, we let the dog do whatever it wanted to do. And then whenever it behaved well, we would praise it. You go, Oh, good dog. And whenever it does something bad, you would go bad dog. And over time, it learns to do more of the Oh, good dog things and fear of the bad dog things. Reinforcement learning takes the same principle and applies it to a helicopter or to other things. So we would have the helicopter fly around in a simulator so it could crash without hurting anyone. But we would let the AI fly the helicopter however once. And whenever it flew the helicopter well, we would go, Oh, good helicopter. And when if it crashed, we go bad helicopter. And then it was the AI's job to learn how to fly the helicopter to get more of the good helicopter rewards and fewer of the bad helicopter negative rewards. More formally, a reinforcement learning algorithm uses a reward signal to tell the AI when it's doing well or poorly. This means that whenever it's doing well, you give it a large positive number to give it a large positive reward. And whenever it's doing really bad job, you send it a negative number to give it a negative reward. And is the AI's job to then automatically learn to behave so as to maximize the rewards. So good dog corresponds to giving you a positive number and bad dog or bad helicopter corresponds to you giving a negative number. And then the AI will learn to get more of the behaviors that result in large positive numbers or in large positive rewards. Let me show you a video of the Stanford autonomous helicopter after we did this. This is a video of the helicopter flying under reinforcement learning control. I was the cameraman that day. And when you zoom out the camera, you see the trees planted in the sky. So we actually gave it a reward signal, which rewarded the helicopter flying upside down. And using reinforcement learning, we built one of the most capable autonomous helicopters in the world. In addition to robotic control, reinforcement learning has also had a lot of traction playing games, games such as Othello or checkers or chess or go. You might have heard of Alpha Go, which did a very good job playing Go using reinforcement learning. And reinforcement learning has also been very effective at playing video games. One of the weaknesses of reinforcement learning algorithms is that they can require a huge amount of data. So if you are playing a video game, a reinforcement learning algorithm can play essentially an infinite number of video games because it's just your computer playing computer game and get a huge amount of data to learn how to behave better. Or for playing games like checkers or other games, it can play a lot of games against itself and for free get a huge amount of data to feed into the reinforcement learning algorithm. In the case of the autonomous helicopter, we had a simulator for the helicopter so it could fly in simulation for a long time to figure out what works and what doesn't work for flying a helicopter. There's still a lot of exciting research work being done to make reinforcement learning work, even for settings where you may not have an accurate simulator, whereas harder to get this huge amount of data. Despite the huge amounts of media attention on reinforcement learning, at least today it is creating significantly less economic value than supervised learning, but there may be breakthroughs in the future that could change that. And AI is advancing so rapidly that all of us certainly hope that there will be breakthroughs in all of these areas that we're talking about.

### GANs（生成对抗网络）由吴恩达的前学生Ian Goodfellow创造，擅长从零生成全新图片——NVIDIA团队用GANs合成了根本不存在的"名人"照片，这些技术正被应用于娱乐行业，包括计算机图形、游戏、媒体等；知识图谱（knowledge graph）是吴恩达认为最被低估的技术——就是在数据库中列出实体及其关键信息（如人物有生日、去世日期、简介等），Google搜索达芬奇或Ada Lovelace时右边出现的信息面板就来自知识图谱——今天各种公司已建立关于人物、电影、酒店、机场、景点等大规模知识图谱；尽管知识图谱为多家大公司创造了大量经济价值，但在学术界研究热度与它的实际经济影响不成比例

> 最后讲两个技术。GANs（生成对抗网络）是由吴恩达的前学生Ian Goodfellow创造的。GANs擅长从零合成新图片——NVIDIA的一个团队用GANs合成名人照片，图上这些人全都不真实存在，是模型从名人图像数据库中学习后凭空生成的。现在有很多团队在做令人兴奋的工作，把GANs应用到娱乐行业——计算机图形、电脑游戏、媒体、以及生成全新的创意内容。最后，知识图谱是另一个吴恩达认为非常被低估的重要AI技术。比如你在Google搜索"Leonardo da Vinci"，右边会弹出一个信息面板显示达芬奇的详细信息——这来自一个知识图谱，本质上是一个数据库，列出人物及其关键信息（出生日期、去世日期、生平简介等）。今天不同公司已经建立了关于电影、名人、酒店、机场、景点等多种类型的知识图谱。例如酒店知识图谱就是一个大数据库，存着各个酒店的关键信息，这样你在看地图时就能快速找到正确信息。知识图谱这个概念最初由Google推广，现在已经遍布很多公司。有趣的是，尽管知识图谱为多家大公司创造着大量经济价值，这个主题在学术研究中却很少被关注——相关研究论文的数量跟其实际经济影响相比少得不成比例。但根据你所处的行业，构建知识图谱的技术也许对你在公司中建立某个重要信息数据库非常有用。

> GANs or Generative Adversarial Networks are another exciting new AI technique they were created by my former student Ian Goodfellow. GANs are very good at synthesizing new images from scratch. Let me show you a video generated by a team from NVIDIA that used GANs to synthesize pictures of celebrities. And these are all pictures of people that had never existed before, but by learning what celebrities look like from a database of celebrity images is able to synthesize all these brand new pictures. There's exciting work by different teams right now on applying GANs to the entertainment industry, everything ranging from computer graphics to computer games to media, and to just making up new content like this from scratch. Finally, the knowledge draft is another important AI technique that I think is very underrated. If you do a search on Google for Leonardo da Vinci, you might find this set of results with this panel on the right of information about da Vinci. If you do a search on Ada Lovelace, you also similarly find a panel of additional information on the right. This information is drawn from a knowledge draft, which basically means a database that lists people and key information about these people, such as the birthday, when they pass away, their bio, and other properties of these individuals. Today, different companies have built knowledge drafts of many different types of things, not just people, but also they built these databases of movies, of celebrities, of hotels, of airports, of scenic attractions, and on and on and on. For example, a knowledge draft with hotel information may have a big database of hotels as well as key information about these hotels, so that if you look them up on the map, you can find the right information relatively quickly. The term knowledge draft was initially popularized by Google, but this concept has spread to many other companies. Interestingly, even though knowledge drafts are creating a lot of economic value for multiple large companies at this point, this is one subject that is relatively little studied in academia. The number of research papers you see on knowledge drafts seems to be disproportionately small relative to their actual economic impact today. But depending on what industry vertical you work in, perhaps some of the techniques for building knowledge drafts will also be useful for building a big database of information about something relevant to your company.

### 总结回顾：今天学到了无监督学习（聚类发现隐藏结构）、迁移学习（借用大任务知识解决小数据任务）、强化学习（通过奖励信号自学最优行为）、GANs（生成全新内容）、知识图谱（结构化实体信息数据库）；知道这些技术能让你跟AI工程师进行更富有成效的交流；下周讲AI对社会的影响——偏见、发展中经济体、就业等议题

> 这个视频你学到了无监督学习、迁移学习、强化学习、GANs和知识图谱，内容不少吧？希望其中一些想法对你的项目有用，也让你知道这些算法是干什么的，能帮你跟AI工程师更顺畅地交流。这周我们讲了很多AI怎么影响公司、你怎么用AI影响公司。AI对社会也在产生巨大影响——怎么理解AI对社会的影响？怎么确保我们做有道德的事、只用AI帮助人们让大家变得更好？下周我们讲AI与社会话题。感谢你一路学到这里，期待在下周最后一期视频见到你。

> In this video, you learned about unsurpassed learning, transfer learning, reinforcement learning, GANs, and knowledge drafts. It seems like a lot, doesn't it? I hope that some of these ideas will also be useful to your projects and that knowing what these algorithms are will make it easier for you to have fruitful discussions with AI engineers. In this week, we've talked a lot about how AI can affect companies, maybe how you could use AI to affect your company. AI is also having a huge impact on society. So how can we understand the impact that AI is having on society, as well as make sure that we do ethical things and that we use AI only to help people and make people better off? In the next week, we'll talk about AI and society. Thanks for sticking with me after this point. I look forward to seeing you in the final week of videos for this course.


---

## 一、第4周课程介绍——AI是一种让小团队也能影响亿万人生活的"超能力"，最后一周将讨论AI的社会影响：偏见、对抗性攻击、发展中经济体和就业

### 最后一周课程聚焦AI与社会的关系——AI虽然强大，但我们都需要对AI的能力和局限建立真实认知，不能过分乐观也不能过分悲观；除了第一周讲的技术局限，AI还有其他重要局限，比如算法偏见、对抗性攻击等问题

> 欢迎回来。你现在进入《AI for everyone》的第四周，也是最后一周，离完成这门课就差最后一步了。AI 是一种超能力，它能让一个小团队去影响数量庞大的人群。所以不管你是在做 AI 的人，还是 AI 的使用者，或者你只是关心 AI 对社会的影响，你都有必要了解这些趋势，这样才能确保你的工作让社会变得更好。AI 正在改变世界，但围绕它也有很多不必要的炒作。对普通公民、企业领袖和政府领导者来说，要想在 AI 的崛起中找准方向，我们大家都必须对 AI 有一个真实的认知。第一周你已经开始了解 AI 在技术上的一些局限了，但 AI 还有其他方面的局限。
> Welcome back. You're now in the fourth and final week of AI for everyone, and so close to finishing this course. AI is a superpower that enables a small team to affect a huge number of people's lives. So whether you're a builder or a user of AI, or whether you're just someone that cares about AI's impact on society, it's important that you learn about these trends so that you can make sure the work you do leaves society better off. Let's take a look. AI is changing the world, but there's also been a lot of unnecessary hype about it. For citizens and business leaders and government leaders to navigate the rise of AI, it's important that we all have a realistic view of AI. Now in week one, you already started to learn about some of the technical limitations of AI. AI has other limitations as well.

### AI系统可能带有偏见、不公平地歧视少数群体或其他人群；AI也容易遭受对抗性攻击——比如垃圾邮件发送者会攻击垃圾邮件过滤器，而新技术也会面临新的攻击方式；AI对发展中经济体和全球就业格局同样会产生深远影响

> 比如，AI 可能会带有偏见，不公平地歧视少数群体或其他人群。那我们该怎么应对？AI 技术也容易遭受对抗性攻击——举个例子，我们都很喜欢垃圾邮件过滤器，它能帮邮件系统持续运转，但总有人在尝试攻击这些过滤器。而且，随着我们开发出新的 AI 技术，如果有人故意想欺骗 AI，这些新技术也可能面临新的攻击方式。像美国、中国这样的发达经济体已经在大量使用 AI 了，但 AI 也会对发展中经济体和全球就业格局产生很大影响。这些问题中有很多都牵涉到 AI 与伦理的关系。
> For example, AI can be biased and discriminate unfairly against minorities or against other groups. So how do we address this? AI technology is also susceptible to adversarial attacks, meaning for example, we love our spam filters, it helps the email system keep on functioning, but there are spammers that are trying to attack spam filters. And even as we develop new AI technologies, some of the new technologies may be susceptible to new types of attacks as well if people are deliberately trying to fool the AI. Developed economies such as the US and China are already using AI extensively, but they'll also have a big impact on developing economies and on the global jobs landscape. Many of these issues are implicated in AI and ethics.

### AI与伦理这个主题非常复杂，没有简单的答案——吴恩达认为这个主题本身就值得一门四周甚至更长的课程；最后一周只能触及一些最主要的问题，让你在构建或使用AI时至少能意识到伴随AI崛起的这些重大议题

> 所以我们要确保我们在 AI 领域做的工作是符合伦理的。这个话题非常复杂，没有简单的答案。说实话，我觉得 AI 与伦理这个主题本身就值得专门开一门四周的课，甚至更长。但这一周，我希望至少能触及一些最主要的问题，这样即使你正在构建或使用 AI，你也能了解伴随 AI 崛起的那些重大议题。最后，这一周结束时，我们会做一个课程总结。我期待在最后这几个视频里跟你一起度过。我们先来深入聊聊怎么建立一个关于 AI 的真实视角——这个视角远远超出了 AI 的技术和性能局限。咱们进入下一个视频。
> So make sure that the work we do in AI is ethical. This is a topic that is complex and there aren't simple answers. In fact, I think the topic of AI and ethics deserves its own four week or much longer course. But I hope this week to at least touch on some of the major issues so that even as you build or use AI, that you understand some of the major issues associated with the rise of AI. Finally, at the end of this week, we will wrap up AI for everyone. I look forward to spending these last few videos of you. Let's get started by talking more about having a realistic view of AI and the way that goes beyond just the technical and performance limitations of AI. Let's go to the next video.
