## 一、AI的"金凤花规则"——对AI既不能过分乐观，也不能过分悲观，取中间的务实态度刚刚好；把通用人工智能（AGI）或超级智能当作"近在眼前"是导致过度乐观和过度恐惧的共同根源

### 我们需要对AI保持一种类似"金凤花与三只熊"故事里的中庸态度——既不能过分乐观地认为AI会解决人类所有问题、带来全球乌托邦，也不能过分悲观地担心AI会有意识、成为超越人类的物种并消灭人类；后一种恐惧属于科幻小说范畴，因为人类有丰富经验控制比任何个体都强大的东西，比如公司和国家

> AI 对社会和亿万人的生活正产生巨大影响。为了让我们所有人都能做出好的决策，我们必须对 AI 抱有一个真实的认识——既不能太乐观，也不能太悲观。我说的是什么意思呢？你小时候可能读过《金凤花与三只熊》的故事——故事里说一碗粥不能太烫也不能太凉，一张床不能太硬也不能太软。我觉得我们对 AI 也需要类似的"金凤花规则"：对 AI 技术能做什么、不能做什么，我们既不应该过分乐观，也不应该过分悲观。比如，我们不应该对 AI 过分乐观。AI 是非常强大的技术，但我不指望它能解决人类所有问题，也不太可能仅凭它自己就把人类带进一个全球乌托邦。有些过度乐观源自于大家认为 AI 的"意识"、通用人工智能甚至超级智能已经近在咫尺，等到了那一天，AI 会迅速在医疗健康领域取得巨大突破，让我们活得更久更健康，还会给每个人创造难以想象的财富——我希望这能这么简单就好了。反过来，一些最悲观恐惧也跟认为超级智能近在咫尺有关——怕 AI 会产生意识，然后决定统治所有人类。在我看来这极不可能。AI 确实有风险，比如它可能产生偏见、给出不公平或不准确的输出，但"失去对 AI 的控制"到那种 AI 变成超越人来的高级物种然后消灭我们的地步，这真的属于科幻小说范畴，不是一个现实的场景。人类有丰富的经验来控制比任何个体都更强大的东西，比如公司和民族国家。AI 的输出有时不可预测，但我并不担心我们所谓的"失去对 AI 的控制"或者它变成人类的竞争对手。
> AI is having a huge impact on society and on so many people's lives. So for all of us to make good decisions, it's important that we have a realistic view of AI and be neither too optimistic nor too pessimistic. Here's what I mean. Did you ever read the story of Goldilocks and the Three Bears maybe when you were a kid? The story was that a bowl of porridge should be neither too hot nor too cold and a bed should be neither too firm nor too soft. I think we need a similar Goldilocks rule for AI where I think it's important that we be neither too optimistic nor too pessimistic about what AI technology can or cannot do. For example, we should not be too optimistic about AI technologies. AI is a very powerful technology, but I wouldn't expect it to solve all of humanity's problems and by itself usher in some sort of global utopia for humanity. Some of the over-optimism stems from people thinking that AI sentience, artificial general intelligence or even superintelligence might be right around the corner and that when we get there, AI will rapidly invent huge breakthroughs in healthcare and give us off long and healthy lives and also create an insane amount of wealth for everyone. I wish it were that easy. On the flip side, some of the most pessimistic fears about AI also relate to thinking superintelligence might be right around the corner and that AI might become sentient and decide to, I don't know, co-op all humans. This seems extremely unlikely to me. Even though AI has risks, for example, it can give bias, unfair or inaccurate outputs. Our losing control of it to the point of AI becoming akin to a superior species that then wipes us out is really in the realm of science fiction rather than a realistic scenario. Humanity has lots of experience controlling things more powerful than any individual, like corporations and nation states. And while AI's output is sometimes unpredictable, I'm not worried about us so-called losing control of AI or becoming a competitive species to us.

### 对AI的真实视角应该是：AI是非常非常强大的工具，但也有很多事情AI做不了；它有偏见、不公平、输出不准确等潜在危害，但这些是可以减轻的；它已经在创造巨大经济价值，而且在多个行业继续创造更多价值的路径出奇地清晰——AI将在可预见的未来持续帮助越来越多的人

> 我觉得对"意识"、超级智能和通用人工智能的那些不必要的恐惧和不切实际的期待，正在分散人们关注真正问题的注意力，也在社会的某些角落制造对 AI 的不必要恐慌。反过来，我认为一种更真实的 AI 视角是这样的：AI 是一个极其强大的工具，但同时也有很多 AI 做不了的事。它有一些潜在的危害，比如偏见、不公平、输出不准确——但这些都是可以缓解的。而且它已经在创造巨大的经济价值。更进一步说，我们看到了一条出奇清晰的路径，让它继续在多个行业里创造更多价值。所以我非常有信心——很多其他 AI 系统构建者也同样有信心——AI 会继续成长，在可预见的未来继续帮助越来越多的人。总结一下：与其太乐观或太悲观，"金凤花"的启示是——取中间的务实态度，刚刚好。当你和朋友聊到 AI 的时候，我希望你也能告诉他们这个 AI 的"金凤花规则"，让他们也对 AI 有一个更真实的认识。
> I think unnecessary fears and overly optimistic hopes about sentience, superintelligence, artificial general intelligence are distracting people from the real issues and is also causing unnecessary fears about AI in parts of society. In contrast, I think a more realistic view of AI is that it is a very, very powerful tool, but that there are also many things that AI cannot do. It has some potential harms like bias, unfairness and inaccurate outputs that we can mitigate, and it is already creating tremendous economic value. Further, we see a surprisingly clear path for it to continue to create even more value in multiple industries. So I'm confident and so are many other AI system builders that AI will continue to grow and continue to help more and more people for the foreseeable future. To summarize, rather than being too optimistic or too pessimistic, the lesson of Goldilocks is that taking a realistic in-between view is just right. When you speak with friends about AI, I hope you can also tell them about this Goldilocks rule for AI so that they too can have a more realistic view of AI.

### 可解释性是AI的一大局限——很多高性能AI系统是"黑箱"，它做得很好但解释不了为什么会这样做；比如一个AI系统通过X光片诊断出病人右侧气胸（右肺塌陷），虽然它通过热力图指出自己是在看右肺的某些关键特征来做判断，给了我们更多信心，但整体上AI的自我解释能力仍然很差；有意思的是，人类自己解释决策的能力其实也很差，只是因为AI是新生事物，缺乏可解释性成了它被接受的一个障碍

> AI 有很多局限。你已经看到了它在性能上的一些局限，但 AI 还有别的局限。其中一个局限是可解释性很难实现：很多高性能的 AI 系统都是"黑箱"——它运行得很好，但 AI 自己也不知道怎么解释它为什么这么做。举个例子。假设你有一个 AI 系统，让它看这张 X 光片来诊断病人有没有问题。在这个真实案例中，AI 系统说它认为病人有"右侧气胸"，也就是右肺塌陷了。但我们怎么知道 AI 说得对不对？怎么知道该不该相信 AI 的诊断？有很多工作在研究怎么让 AI 系统能够自我解释。在这个例子里，热力图是 AI 在告诉我们——它到底在看图像的哪一部分来做出这个诊断。因为它明确地基于右肺、而且事实上是基于右肺的一些关键特征来做判断，这张图会让我们更有信心，觉得 AI 的诊断是合理的。当然，公平地讲，人类自己其实也很不擅长解释我们是怎么做决策的。比如，你之前几周的课里就见过这个咖啡杯——你怎么知道这是一个咖啡杯？人类是怎么看着它就说出"这是咖啡杯"的？有些特征你可以指出来，比如有个装液体的空间、有个把手——但我们人类真的不擅长解释我们到底是怎么看着它就做出判断的。但正因为 AI 是一种相对新生的事物，缺乏可解释性就成了它被接受的一个障碍。而且，有时候如果一个 AI 系统出问题了，如果它能自我解释，也会帮我们搞清楚该怎么去改进这个系统。
> There are many limitations of AI. You have already seen earlier some of the performance limitations, but AI has other limitations as well. One of the limitations of AI is that explainability is hard, and many high-performing AI systems are black boxes, meaning that it works very well, but the AI doesn't know how to explain why it does what it does. Here's an example. Let's say you have an AI system, look at this x-ray image to diagnose if anything is wrong with the patient. In this example, which is a real example, the AI system says that it thinks the patient has right-sided pneumothorax, which means that the right lung is collapsed. But how do we know if the AI is right, and how do you know if you should trust the AI system's diagnosis or not? There's been a lot of work on making AI systems explain themselves. In this example, the heat map is the AI telling us what part of the image it is looking at in order to make this diagnosis. Because it is clearly basing this diagnosis on the right lung, and in fact on some key features of the right lung, seeing this image may give us more confidence that the AI is making a reasonable diagnosis. Now, to be fair, humans are also not very good at explaining how we make decisions ourselves. For example, you've already seen this coffee mug in the last week's videos, but how do you know it's a coffee mug? How does a human look at this and say that's a coffee mug? There are some things you can point to, like there's a room for liquid and there's a handle, but we humans are not very good at explaining how we can look at this and decide what it is. But because AI is a relatively new thing, the lack of explainability is sometimes a barrier to its acceptance. And also, sometimes if an AI system isn't working, then its ability to explain itself would also help us figure how to go in and make the AI system work better.

### 可解释性是重要的开放研究领域，实践中AI团队通常能给出"够用"的解释来让系统部署；此外AI还有偏见歧视和对抗性攻击等严重局限——如果训练数据不能反映公平价值观，AI就会学会歧视，AI社区在这些问题上取得了良好进展但还有大量工作要做

> 所以，可解释性是很多研究者正在努力攻克的重大开放研究方向之一。我在实践中看到的是，当一个 AI 团队想部署一个系统时，他们通常能找到一个"够用"的解释，让系统能够运行和部署。也就是说，可解释性虽然很难，但往往并非不可能——不过我们确实需要更好的工具来帮 AI 系统自我解释。AI 还有其他一些严重的局限。作为一个社会，我们不希望基于性别或族裔来歧视任何人，我们希望人人被公平对待。但当 AI 系统被喂进那些没能反映这些价值观的数据时，AI 就可能变得有偏见，学会歧视某些人。AI 社区正在努力攻关，也在这些问题上取得了不错的进展，但我们的路才走了一半，还有很多工作要做。你会在下个视频深入学习有偏见的 AI，也会了解一些确保你使用的 AI 系统偏见更少的方法。最后，很多 AI 系统在做经济上很重要的决策，而其中一些系统可能面临对抗性攻击——如果有人故意要来欺骗你的 AI 的话。所以，根据你的应用场景，确保你的 AI 不会暴露在对抗性攻击之下，可能非常重要。AI 与歧视、AI 与偏见，以及 AI 的对抗性攻击问题——不管对潜在的 AI 构建者和使用者，还是对整个社会，都非常重要。下个视频，我们来深入探讨 AI 与偏见的问题。
> So, explainability is one of the major open research areas that a lot of researchers are working on. What I see in practice is that when an AI team wants to deploy something, the AI team is often able to come up with an explanation that is good enough to enable the system to work and be deployed. So, explainability is hard, but is often not impossible, but we do need much better tools to help AI systems explain themselves. AI has some other serious limitations. As a society, we do not want to discriminate against individuals based on their gender, based on their ethnicity, and we want people to be treated fairly. But when AI systems are fed data that doesn't reflect these values, then an AI can become biased or can learn to discriminate against certain people. The AI community is working hard and is making good progress on these issues, but we're far from done and there's still a lot of work to do. You learn more about biased AI in the next video, and some ideas how to make sure that AI systems you work with are less biased. Finally, many AI systems are making economically important decisions, and some AI systems are open to adversarial attacks if someone else is deliberately out to fool your AI system. So, depending on your application, it may be important to make sure that you are not open to these types of attacks on your AI systems. The issues of AI and discrimination or AI and bias, as well as the issue of adversarial attacks on AI, are important both to you as a potential builder and user of AI, as well as to society. In the next video, let's dive more deeply into the issue of AI and bias.


---

## 一、AI偏见与歧视——AI从互联网文本中学习，会把人类语言中的不健康刻板印象也学进去：它认为"男人之于程序员，正如女人之于家庭主妇"；好消息是我们现在对减少AI偏见的思路比减少人类偏见更清晰

### 微软研究团队发现：让AI阅读互联网文本后，AI会学到不健康的刻板印象——当你问AI类比题："男人之于女人，正如国王之于___"，它会回答"王后"（这挺合理）；但当你问"男人之于计算机程序员，正如女人之于___"，它却回答"家庭主妇"——这个偏见答案非常遗憾；我们希望AI理解的是男女都可以平等地做程序员，也都可以平等地做家庭主妇

> AI 系统怎么会变得有偏见、从而歧视某些人？我们该怎么尝试减少甚至消除 AI 系统中的这种影响？我们从一个例子讲起。微软的一个研究团队发现了一个引人注目的结果：当 AI 从互联网上的文本学习时，它会学到不健康的刻板印象。值得称赞的是，他们也提出了减少这类 AI 系统中偏见程度的技术方案。他们发现了什么？让 AI 阅读互联网上的文本，AI 就能学到词汇，然后你可以让它做类比推理。你可以"考"这个 AI 系统。好了，你已经读完了互联网上所有文本——在"男人之于女人，正如父亲之于___"这个类比中，AI 会输出"母亲"这个词，这反映了这些词在互联网上的典型用法。如果你问"男人之于女人，正如国王之于___"，同一个 AI 系统会回答——"国王之于王后"，这在互联网用语的语境下似乎也挺合理。但研究还发现了下面这个结果：如果你问它"男人之于计算机程序员，正如女人之于___"，同一个 AI 系统给出的答案是——"女人之于家庭主妇"。我觉得这个答案真的非常遗憾。要是由我们来修正偏见，答案应该是"女人之于计算机程序员"。如果我们希望 AI 系统理解男人和女人可以平等地做计算机程序员，就像男人和女人可以平等地做家庭主妇一样，那么它应该输出"男人之于计算机程序员，正如女人之于计算机程序员"，同时也应该输出"男人之于家庭主妇，正如女人之于家庭主妇"。
> How does an AI system become biased and therefore discriminate against some people? And how can we try to reduce or eliminate this effect in our AI systems? Let's start with an example. A group at Microsoft found this remarkable result that when AI learns from text found on the internet, it can learn unhealthy stereotypes. To the credit, they also proposed technical solutions for reducing the amount of bias in this type of AI system. Here's what they found. By having an AI read text on the internet, it can learn about words and you can ask it to reason about analogies. So you can quiz the AI system. Now that you've read all this text on the internet, in the analogy man is the woman, as father is the what. So the AI will output the word mother, which reflects the way these words are typically used on the internet. If you ask it, man is the woman, as king is the what, then the same AI system will say as king is the queen, which again seems reasonable relative to the way these words are used on the internet. The research has also found the following result, which is that if you ask it, man is the computer programmer, as woman is the what, that the same AI system would output the answer woman is to homemaker. And I think this answer is really unfortunate. Let's buy as answer would be a word to say, woman is to computer programmer. If we want our AI system to understand that men and women can equally be computer programmers, just as men and women can equally be homemakers, then we would like it to output man is the computer programmer, as women is computer programmer, and also man is the homemaker, as women is the homemaker.

### AI偏见的技术原理：AI用一组数字（向量）来存储每个词，数字来自该词在互联网上的使用统计——比如"男人"表示为(1,1)，"计算机程序员"表示为(3,2)，"女人"表示为(2,3)；做类比推理时，AI会构建一个平行四边形，从"男人"到"程序员"是右移2步上移1步，从"女人"也右移2步上移1步就落在(4,4)，而"家庭主妇"恰好被放在了(4,4)的位置——这就是偏见产生的数学机制

> AI 系统怎么就从数据中学到了这样的偏见呢？我们稍微深入一点看技术细节。AI 系统存储词汇的方式是用一组数字。比如，"男人"这个词被存储——有时也说被"表示"——为两个数字 1 和 1。AI 系统怎么得到这些数字的呢？是通过统计"男人"这个词在互联网上怎么被使用。这些数字的具体计算过程相当复杂，这里就不展开了。但这些数字代表了这些词的典型用法。实践中，AI 可能用成百上千个数字来存储一个词，但这里只用两个数字来简化例子。我把这个数字画在图表上——所以"男人"我就画在右边这张图的(1,1)位置。通过观察"计算机程序员"这个词组在互联网上的使用统计，AI 会得到另一对数字，比如(3,2)，来存储或者说表示"计算机程序员"。同理，通过观察"女人"这个词怎么被使用，它会得到另一对数字，比如(2,3)，来存储或表示"女人"。当你让 AI 系统去计算上面的类比——男人之于计算机程序员，正如女人之于___——AI 系统会构建一个这样的平行四边形。它会问：哪个词关联到了(4,4)这个位置？因为 AI 认为这就是这个类比的答案。从数学角度理解：AI 认为男人和计算机程序员之间的关系是——从"男人"出发，往右走2步，往上走1步。所以它从"女人"出发，也向右2步、向上1步来找到对应词。不幸的是，当这些数字来自互联网文本时，AI 系统发现"家庭主妇"在互联网中的用法导致它被放在了(4,4)的位置——这就是 AI 系统得出这个偏见类比的数学原因。
> How does an AI system learn to become biased like this from data? Let's dive a bit more into the technical details. The way an AI system stores words is using a set of numbers. So let's say the word man is stored, or we sometimes say represented as the two numbers one one. The way an AI system comes up with these numbers is through statistics of how the word man is used on the internet. The specific process for how these numbers are computed is quite complex, and I won't go into that here. But these numbers represent the typical usage of these words. In practice, an AI might have hundreds or thousands of numbers to store a word, but I'm just going to use two numbers here to keep the examples simpler. Let me take this number and plot it on a chart. So the word man, I'm going to plot at the position one one on the figure on the right. By looking at the statistics of how the words or how the phrase computer programmer is used on the internet, the AI will have a different pair of numbers, say three two, to store or to represent the phrase computer programmer. And similarly, by looking at how the word women is used, it'll come up with a different pair of numbers, say two three, to store or to represent the word women. When you ask the AI system to compute the analogy above, man is the computer programmer as women is the what, then what the AI system will do is construct a parallelogram that looks like this. And it will ask what is the word associated with the position four four, because they will think that is the answer to this analogy. One way to think about this mathematically is that the AI thinks the relationship of man to computer programmer is that you start from the word man, go two steps to the right and one step up. And so to find the same answer for women as to what you would also go two steps to the right and one step up. Unfortunately, when these numbers are derived from text on the internet and AI system finds that the way the word homemaker is used on the internet causes it to be placed at the position four four, which is why the AI system comes up with this bias analogy.

### AI偏见在真实世界中有严重后果——某公司用AI做招聘，发现他们的招聘工具歧视女性，不得不关停；有些面部识别系统对浅肤色的人比对深肤色的人准确得多，如果用于刑事调查会带来严重不公；还有AI贷款审批系统对某些少数族裔给出更高利率；一个8岁女孩在搜索引擎搜CEO却只看到男性照片，这可能打消她未来追求高管职位的念头

> AI 系统今天已经在做重要决策了，未来还会做更多，所以偏见问题至关重要。举例来说，有一家公司用 AI 来做招聘，结果发现他们的招聘工具歧视女性——这显然不公平，于是这家公司关停了这个工具。另外，有些面部识别系统对浅肤色和深肤色人群的识别准确率不一样——如果 AI 系统训练数据主要是浅肤色人群，那它对这类人就会更准确。而当这些系统用于比如刑事侦查时，就可能对深肤色人群产生极具偏见和不公的影响。所以很多面部识别团队正在努力确保他们的系统不存在这种偏见。还有，有些 AI 或统计贷款审批系统最终对某些少数族裔群体给出了歧视性的更高利率。银行也在努力减少甚至消除审批系统中的这类偏见。最后，我觉得很重要的一点是：AI 系统不应该助长"强化不健康刻板印象"的有毒效应。比如，假设一个 8 岁的小女孩在图片搜索引擎里搜索"首席执行官"——如果她看到的全都只有男性照片，或者看不到任何和她性别或族裔相似的人，我们可不希望她被打击到，就此放弃将来某天成为大公司 CEO 的职业梦想。
> AI systems are already making important decisions today and will continue to do so in the future as well. So bias matters. For example, there's a company that was using AI for hiring and found that they're hiring to discriminated against women. This is clearly unfair. And so this company shut down there too. Second, there are also some facial recognition systems that seem to work more accurately for light skinned and dark skinned individuals. If an AI system is trained primarily on data of lighter skinned individuals, then it will be more accurate for that category of individuals. To the extent that these systems are used in, for example, criminal investigations, this can create a very bias and unfair effect for dark skinned individuals. So many face recognition teams today are working hard to ensure that the systems do not exhibit this type of bias. There have also been AI or statistical loan approval systems that wound up discriminating against some minority ethnic groups and quoted them a higher interest rate. Banks have also been working to make sure to diminish or eliminate this type of bias in their approval systems. Finally, I think it's important that AI systems do not contribute to the toxic effect of reinforcing unhealthy serotypes. For example, if an eight-year-old girl goes through an image search engine and searches for chief executive officer, if they see only pictures of men, or if they see no one that looks like themselves, either by gender or ethnicity, we don't want them to be discouraged from pursuing a career that might lead her to someday be a chief executive officer of a large company.

### 减少AI偏见的四种方案——第一，技术方案：当AI学到大量表示词汇的数字时，其中有少数数字对应着偏见，把那些数字"归零"就能显著降低偏见；第二，使用偏见更少、更具包容性的数据，比如做人脸识别需要包含多种族裔和性别；第三，透明度和系统性审计，比如人脸识别团队系统地检查系统在不同人群子集上的准确率，尽早发现问题并修复；第四，多元化的团队，不同视角的人更容易发现不同的问题，从源头让数据更多元、更包容

> 正因为这些问题，AI 社区在对抗偏见方面投入了大量精力。比如，我们开始在减少 AI 偏见的领域获得越来越好的技术方案。在本视频开头那个 AI 输出偏见类比的例子里——稍微简化一下——研究者发现，当 AI 学到很多用来表示词汇的数字时，其中有一小部分数字对应于偏见，如果你把那些数字"归零"、直接设为 0，偏见就会显著降低。第二个方案是尝试使用偏见更少、更包容的数据。比如，如果你在做一个面部识别系统，确保训练数据包含了多个族裔和所有性别，那么系统就会偏见更少、包容性更强。第三，很多 AI 团队正在让他们的系统接受更好的透明度和/或审计流程，这样我们可以持续检查这些 AI 系统到底有没有偏见——是哪种类型的偏见——这样至少能在大问题出现时及时发现，然后采取行动去解决。比如，很多面部识别团队正在系统性地检查他们的系统在不同人群子集上的准确性——例如深肤色和浅肤色人群的准确率差异。有了透明的系统加系统性的审计流程，就能提高我们尽早发现问题、然后进行修复的概率。最后，我认为拥有一支多元化的团队也有助于减少偏见。如果你的团队是多元化的，团队成员就更有可能发现不同类型的问题，更有可能从最开始就让你的数据更多元、更具包容性。通过在构建 AI 系统时拥有更多独特的视角，我觉得这将帮助所有人创建偏见更少的应用。
> Because of these issues, the AI community has put a lot of effort into combating bias. For example, we're starting to better and better technical solutions for reducing bias in AI systems. In the example you saw at the start of this video of the AI outputting bias analogies, simplifying the description a little bit, researchers have found that when an AI system learns a lot of different numbers with which to store words, there are a few numbers that correspond to the bias, and if you zero out those numbers, just set them to zero, then the bias diminishes significantly. A second solution is to try to use less bias and or more inclusive data. For example, if you are building a face recognition system and make sure to include data from multiple ethnicities and all genders, then your system will be less biased and more inclusive. Second, many AI teams are subjecting their systems to better transparency and or auditing processes so that we can constantly check what types of bias, if any, these AI systems are exhibiting so that we can at least recognize the problem if it exists and then take steps to address it. For example, many face recognition teams are systematically checking how accurate their system is on different subsets of the population to check whether it is more or less accurate on dark skin versus light skin individuals, for example. Having transparent systems as well as systematic auditing processes increases the odds that will at least quickly spot the problem in case there is one so we can fix it. Finally, I think having a diverse workforce will also help reduce bias. If you have a diverse workforce, then the individuals at your workforce are more likely to be able to spot different problems and maybe to help make your data more diverse and more inclusive in the first place. By having more unique points of view as you're building AI systems, I think this will help all of us create less bias applications.

### 值得乐观的是：我们今天减少AI偏见的思路，实际上比减少人类偏见的思路更清晰；虽然在任何偏见被彻底消除之前我们都不该满足，而且这需要大量工作，但如果能通过技术手段让AI系统——它们从人类那里学到了与人类同等级的偏见——在此基础上大幅降低偏见，那社会就能通过AI让决策迅速变得比纯靠人类更公平、偏见更少；接下来将讨论AI的另一大局限：对抗性攻击

> AI 系统今天在做真正重要的决策，所以它们的偏见——或者说它们的偏见风险——是我们必须关注并努力降低的。让我对此保持乐观的一点是：我们现在对减少 AI 偏见的思考，实际上比减少人类偏见的思考要更清晰。所以——虽然在任何 AI 偏见彻底消除之前我们都不该满足，而且这需要大量工作——但我也很乐观：如果我们能让 AI 系统从跟人类相近的偏见水平起步（因为它们的偏见本来就是跟人类学的），然后通过技术方案或其他手段把偏见大幅降低，那么作为一个社会，我们就有望让自己用人类或 AI 做出来的决策，迅速变得越来越公平、偏见越来越少。除了偏见问题，AI 的另一个局限是它可能暴露在对抗性攻击之下。下个视频你将会了解什么是对抗性攻击，以及你能做哪些事来防范它们。咱们进入下一个视频。
> AI systems are making really important decisions today and so their bias or potential for bias is something we must pay attention to and work to diminish. One thing that makes me optimistic about this is that we actually have better ideas today for reducing bias in AI than reducing bias in humans. So while we should never be satisfied until all AI bias is gone and it will take us quite a bit of work to get there, I'm also optimistic if we could take AI systems that started off with a level similar to humans because they learn from humans and we can cut down the bias from there through technical solutions or other means so that as a society, we can hopefully make the decisions we're making through humans or through AI rapidly become more fair and less biased. In addition to the problem of bias, one of the other limitations of AI is that it can be open to adversarial attacks. In the next video, you'll learn what are adversarial attacks as well as some of the things you could do to guard against them. Let's go on to the next video.


---

## 一、对抗性攻击——人类肉眼几乎看不出任何变化的微小像素扰动，就能让AI把蜂鸟认成锤子、把野兔认成桌子、把香蕉认成烤面包机，甚至贴上贴纸就能让自动驾驶汽车看不见停车标志

### 现代AI系统有时会被"骗"——给一张鸟的图片，AI正确识别为蜂鸟；但在图片上做一个肉眼几乎察觉不到的微小像素改变后，同一个AI系统却说这是一把锤子；这就是对抗性攻击，即有人故意去欺骗AI系统，让它做出本不该做的错误判断；AI"看"世界的方式跟人类完全不同，所以它对人类察觉不到的改动却可能极其敏感

> 尽管现代 AI 极其强大，但现代 AI 技术——尤其是深度学习——的一个局限是：有时它会"被骗"。具体来说，如果有人故意要欺骗你的 AI 系统，现代 AI 有时会容易遭受"对抗性攻击"。我们来看一下。假设你给 AI 系统这张鸟的图片，让它做分类。AI 系统输出说这是一只蜂鸟。但是，我们对这张图片做一个微小的扰动——我说的"微小扰动"意思是把像素值改动一点点，大多数人都察觉不到这种变化。同样的 AI 系统却说——这是一把锤子。你可能会说，这怎么可能？右边的图看起来跟左边几乎一模一样。事实上这些变化对人眼来说几乎是察觉不到的。但 AI 系统"看"世界的方式跟你我非常不同。因此，如果一个攻击者对图片做出了你我看不出、却能让 AI 误以为图片内容完全不同的改动，AI 就很容易上当。我们把这叫做对 AI 系统的"对抗性攻击"。在计算机安全领域，"攻击安全系统"的意思是试图让系统做它本不该做的事。同理，对 AI 系统的对抗性攻击，就是试图让它做它本不该做的事——比如骗它输出错误的分类结果。还有一个例子：这是一张野兔的图片——只需对像素值做微小改动——AI 却说这是一张桌子。计算机看图片的方式和人类不同，这既有好处也有坏处。比如，计算机系统读条形码和二维码比你我强得多。但深度学习系统的工作方式，也让它们对这些特定形式的攻击打开大门——而这样的攻击没有人类会上当。
> Even though modern AI is incredibly powerful, one of the limitations of modern AI technologies, especially deep learning, is that sometimes it can be fooled. In particular, modern AI systems are sometimes susceptible to adversarial attacks if someone else sets out deliberately to fool your AI system. Let's take a look. Let's say you give an AI system this picture of a bird and ask it to classify it. The AI system outputs that this is a hummingbird. But let's make a minor perturbation to this image. By minor perturbation, I mean to change the pixel values just a little bit, an almost imperceptible change to most people. The same AI system then says this is instead a hammer. Now to a person, you might say, how is this even possible? The picture on the right looks almost identical to the picture on the left. And in fact, the changes are almost imperceptible to the human eye. But an AI system sees the world very differently than you and I do. And so it is susceptible to, if an adversary makes changes to a picture that could be imperceptible to you and me, but their results in fooling the AI into thinking this picture is something totally different. We call this an adversarial attack on an AI system. In computer security, an attack against a secure system means an attempt to make it do something other than what it was intended to do. In the same way, an adversarial attack on an AI system is an attempt to make it do something other than what it was intended to do, such as trying to fool it into outputting incorrect classifications. Here's another example. Here's a picture of a hare with just a minor perturbation or a small change to the pixel values. AI instead says this is a desk. The fact that computers see pictures differently than humans do gives it advantages and disadvantages. For example, computer systems are much better than you and me at reading barcodes and QR codes. But the way that deep learning systems work also opens it up to these particular forms of attack, which no human would be fooled by.

### 攻击不仅能发生在数字图片上，还能延伸到物理世界——卡内基梅隆大学研究者设计了一副古怪的眼镜，戴上后能骗过AI系统让它以为这个男人是女演员米拉·乔沃维奇；UC伯克利和密歇根大学等研究者展示了在停车标志上贴贴纸，能让AI完全"看不见"停车标志——人类依然轻松看到这是个停车标志，但如果用在自动驾驶汽车上就非常危险

> 今天，AI 被用来过滤垃圾邮件、过滤仇恨言论——而像这样的攻击会削弱这些过滤器的效果。目前这页幻灯片里的攻击需要直接修改图像的能力——比如垃圾邮件发送者在把图片上传到网站或放进邮件之前直接修改它。还有一些攻击则通过改变物理世界来实现。例如，卡内基梅隆大学的一个团队设计了一副像这样很"搞怪"的眼镜——当这位男士戴上这副眼镜后，他能骗过 AI 系统让 AI 以为他是女演员米拉·乔沃维奇。我觉得这真的很惊人——只要戴这么一副眼镜，就能骗 AI 以为这个男人是一位著名女演员。另一组来自 UC 伯克利、密歇根大学等高校的研究者展示：如果你把像这样的贴纸贴在停车标志上，可以让 AI 系统完全"看不见"这个停车标志。这个例子特别有意思的一点是——它看起来就像有人在停车标志上涂了个鸦，大多数人类依然能很轻松地认出这是停车标志。但如果你的自动驾驶汽车里内置了计算机视觉系统，而因为贴了这些贴纸，车就不认识停车标志了——那就非常糟糕了。
> Today, AI is being used to filter out spam, to try to filter out hate speech, and attacks like these will diminish the effectiveness of such filters. Now the attacks on this slide require the ability to modify an image directly. For example, a spammer might directly modify an image before they try to upload it to a website or send it in an email. There are some attacks that work by changing the physical world as well. For example, a group at Carnegie Mellon University was able to design a funky pair of glasses like this so that when this man wears this pair of glasses, he can fool an AI system into thinking that he is actress Mila Jovovich. I think it's remarkable that just wearing a pair of glasses like this can fool an AI system into thinking that this man is a well-known actress. A different group of researchers from UC Berkeley, University of Michigan, and other universities showed that if you affix stickers like these onto a stop sign, you can fool an AI system into not seeing the stop sign at all. One interesting thing about this example is that it looks like the stop sign just had some graffiti applied on top of it and most humans would still see this as a stop sign quite easily. But if you have a computer vision system built into a self-driving car, for example, it would be really unfortunate if the car doesn't see the stop sign anymore because of these stickers applied on top of it.

### Google研究者展示了另一个惊人案例：给AI看一张香蕉图片，AI正常识别为香蕉；但当在场景中放入一张特殊设计的贴纸后，AI几乎百分之百确信这张图片里是一个烤面包机——研究人员甚至把贴纸图案公开发表在论文中，意味着理论上任何人都能下载论文、打印贴纸，去欺骗AI系统

> 最后一个例子来自 Google 的一组研究者：如果你给 AI 系统看这张图，它会说这是香蕉。但研究者设计了一种贴纸——把它放进场景中——就能让 AI 把香蕉错分。我给你看看研究者做的视频。左边是分类器的输入，右边是分类器的输出——它认为很可能是香蕉，还有很小概率是一只蛞蝓。看起来还行。来看看放入贴纸后会发生什么。当这个贴纸被放入场景后，AI 系统几乎百分之百确信这张图是烤面包机。这项研究有一个很有意思的地方：幻灯片底部引用的这篇论文的作者，竟然在论文里公开发表了他们贴纸的图片——这意味着理论上海任何人都可以下载论文、把贴纸打印出来，贴到某个地方来骗 AI 系统以为那里有个烤面包机。当然，我绝对不支持任何人去攻击 AI 系统骗它们以为某处有烤面包机。但这恰恰说明了——不幸的是——攻击这些 AI 系统可能有多么容易。
> One last example, this one from a group of researchers at Google, is if you show an AI system this picture, it will say this is a banana. But what the researchers did was design a sticker so that if you place it into the scene, it misclassifies this banana. Let me show you the video that the researchers made. Shown on the left is the classifier input and shown on the right is the classifier output where it thinks it's very likely a banana and maybe this is a small chance is a slug. Looks okay. Let's see what happens when you put a sticker or put a small patch into the scene. When the sticker is placed in the scene, the AI system now is all but certain that this picture is a picture of a toaster. One interesting aspect of this work is that the authors of this paper cited at the bottom of the slide actually published in the paper a picture of their sticker so that anyone in the world could hypothetically download their paper, print out the sticker and stick it somewhere if they want to fool an AI system into thinking there's a toaster where there isn't. Now, I do not support anyone attacking AI systems to fool them into thinking there's toasters where they're none. But this shows unfortunately perhaps the ease with which one could hypothetically attack these AI systems.

### 防御手段存在但需要付出代价——修改神经网络可以让AI更难被攻击，但系统可能因此运行变慢；对于很多AI应用来说根本没人有动机去攻击（比如工厂里检测咖啡杯有没有划痕的系统），但对于垃圾邮件过滤、反欺诈等应用，攻击者会持续尝试突破防线，这就像一场"军备竞赛"——吴恩达分享了自己带领反欺诈团队时与欺诈者"全面开战"的经历，对方发动攻击后团队必须在几小时内做出反应来防御

> 那我们该怎么防御这些对抗性攻击呢？幸运的是，AI 界正在研发让它们更难被攻击的新技术。这些防御手段往往非常技术性，但有办法修改神经网络和其他 AI 系统来让它们更难被攻击。一个缺点是这些防御确实会带来一些代价——比如 AI 系统可能会运行得稍微慢一点。但这个领域的研究还在进行中，我们离拥有对所有重要应用都足够好的对抗性防御还很远。对很多 AI 系统来说，可能压根没人有动机去攻击。比如，你在工厂里运行一个自动视觉检测系统来检查咖啡杯有没有划痕——可能没什么人会有动机去骗你的系统让它以为一个有划痕的咖啡杯没有划痕。但一定会有一些 AI 应用场景，攻击是会出现的。对那些场景来说，我觉得——就像垃圾邮件与反垃圾邮件一样：垃圾邮件发送者想把垃圾邮件发进去，垃圾邮件过滤器在努力拦截它们——我认为将会出现这样的应用场景：我们 AI 社区一边在搭建防御，另一边有攻击者社区在尝试突破防御，双方进入"军备竞赛"。在我构建 AI 系统的经历中，少数让我感觉自己在跟别人"全面开战"的时刻，就是在带领反欺诈团队对抗欺诈者的时候。互联网上不幸存在一定量的欺诈行为——有人试图在支付系统里偷钱，或创建虚假账户。那几次做反欺诈系统的经历，是我少数真的感觉像在玩"零和游戏"的时刻：对手架起防御，他们立刻反应；他们发动攻击，我的团队有时必须在几个小时内做出反应来防御。所以我认为，在未来几年里，即使 AI 技术不断演进，也会存在一些像垃圾邮件、欺诈之类的垂直领域，团队会在对抗攻击者时感觉像在跟对手打"零和战争"。话虽这么说，我也不想过度夸大对抗性 AI 的潜在危害。对某些应用它确实极其重要，但也有很多 AI 应用场景不太可能遭受对抗性攻击。
> What can we do to defend against these adversarial attacks? Fortunately, the AI world has been working on new technologies to make them harder to attack. The defenses tend to be very technical, but there are ways of modifying new networks and other AI systems to make them somewhat harder to attack. One downside is that these defenses do incur some costs. For example, the AI system may run a little bit slower. But this is an area of ongoing research and we're far from having adversarial defenses that seem good enough for all the important applications that we want to apply AI to. For many AI systems, there may be no incentive for anyone to try to attack it. For example, if you're running a automatic visual inspection system to check if coffee mugs have scratches in your factory, maybe not that many people have any incentive to try to fool your system into thinking a scratch coffee mug doesn't have a scratch on it. But there are going to be AI applications where there will be attacks as well. For those applications, I think that similar to spam versus anti-spam, where spam is trying to get the spam email through and spam filters are trying to stop them. I think there will be applications where we will be in an arms race as the AI community is building up defenses and there is a community of attackers trying to fool our defenses. In my experience building AI systems, one of the few times I felt I was at total war with someone else was when I was leading anti-fraud teams fighting against fraudsters. There is unfortunately some amounts of fraud on the internet where people are trying to steal money and payment systems or create fraudulent accounts. The times I worked on anti-fraud systems was one of the few times that it really felt like a zero sum game had an adversary would put up a defense and they would react. They would launch an attack, my teams would have to react sometimes within hours to defend ourselves. So I think over the next few years, even as AI technologies evolve, there will be verticals like that, like spam, like fraud, where teams will be at war in what might feel like a zero sum game against adversaries. Having said that, I also don't want to overhype the potential of damage for adversarial AI systems. It is really important for some applications, but there are also plenty of AI applications which are less likely to be subject to adversarial attacks. Now in addition to adversarial attacks, AI unfortunately can also be used for some adverse or some negative use cases. Let's take a look at some of them in the next video as well as what we should do to address them. Let's go on to the next video.


---

## 一、AI的恶意应用——Deepfake换脸造假、AI生成假评论、压迫性监控……AI给恶意使用者也提供了新武器；但就像反垃圾邮件一方资源远超垃圾邮件发送者一样，"善的一方"整体资源占优，最终会占上风

### AI技术被用来制造Deepfake——即合成一个人做了他从未做过的视频；Buzzfeed曾制作了前总统奥巴马说一些他从未说过的话的视频，因为透明地标注了"这是假的"所以没问题；但如果这种技术被用来针对个人、让人以为他们说了或做了从未发生的事，受害者就会被伤害，并且不得不面对"假的视频证据"为自己辩护

> AI 极其强大，绝大多数 AI 使用者都在让个人、公司、国家和社会变得更好。但 AI 也存在少数恶意使用者。我们来看看其中一些例子，并讨论一下我们可以做些什么。AI 技术已经被用来制造 Deepfake——也就是合成人们做了他们从未真正做过的事的视频。Buzzfeed 网站曾经制作了一个美国前总统巴拉克·奥巴马说一些他从未说过的话的视频。Buzzfeed 对此是透明的——他们在发布时明确告诉大家"这是假视频"，所以非常明显。但如果这种技术被用来针对某个人，让别人以为他们说了或做了他们从未做过的事，那这些个人就可能受到伤害，并且不得不去为自己从未做过的事、去辩护一段假的视频证据。就像垃圾邮件与反垃圾邮件的对抗一样，今天也有 AI 技术可以检测一段视频是不是 Deepfake。但在今天这个社交媒体时代——假内容可能比真相比传得还快——很多人担心 Deepfake 伤害个人的潜力。
> AI is incredibly powerful and the vast majority of users of AI are making people, companies, countries, society better off. But there are a few adverse users of AI as well. Let's take a look at some of them and discuss what we can do about them. AI technology has been used to create deep and that means to synthesize video of people doing things that they never actually did. The website Buzzfeed created a video of former US President Barack Obama saying things that he never did. Buzzfeed was transparent about it and when they published the video it was really obvious because they told everyone that this is a fake video. But if this type of technology is used to target an individual and make others think they said or did things they never actually did then these individuals could be harmed and left to defend themselves against fake video evidence of something they never actually did. Similar to the wall of spam versus anti-spam there is AI technology today for detecting if a video is a deep fake. But in today's world of social media where a fake could spread around the world faster than the truth can catch up many people are concerned about the potential of deep fakes to harm individuals.

### AI也可能被用来破坏民主和隐私——一些压迫性政权可能会用AI对其公民进行压迫性监控；政府确实有正当需求去提高公共安全和减少犯罪，但有些使用AI的方式让人感觉是被压迫而不是被赋能；此外，AI能生成虚假评论——不管是商业上的虚假产品评论，还是政治讨论中的虚假政治评论，AI比人工写手高效得多；因此检测和清除虚假评论，是维护网络评论可信度的关键技术

> 还有 AI 技术被用来破坏民主和隐私的风险。比如，全世界很多政府都在努力改善公民的生活——我非常尊敬那些在改善公民福祉的政府领导者。但也存在一些压迫性政权，他们对公民做的事是不对的，可能会试图利用这类技术对自己公民实施压迫性监控。虽然政府有正当需求去提高公共安全和减少犯罪，但有些使用 AI 的方式，让人感觉更像是对自己公民的压迫而非赋能。与此密切相关的，是 AI 可以生成虚假评论的兴起。利用 AI 技术现在已经可以生成虚假评论了——在商业方面，生成虚假的产品评论；在政治讨论中，生成虚假的政治评论——而且效率远比纯靠人去写要高得多。因此，检测这些虚假评论并清除它们，是维护我们在线评论可信度的一项重要技术。就像垃圾邮件与反垃圾邮件、欺诈与反欺诈的战役一样，我认为在所有这些议题上，双方的竞争将在相当长的时间内持续下去。
> There's also a risk of AI technology being used to undermine democracy and privacy. For example many governments around the world are trying to improve their citizens lives and I have a lot of respect for the government leaders that are uplifting their citizens. But there are also some oppressive regimes that are not doing the right things by their citizens that may seek to use this type of technology to carry out oppressive surveillance of their citizens. While governments have a legitimate need to improve public safety and reduce crime there are also ways of using AI that feel more oppressive than uplifting of its own citizens. Closely related to this is the rise of fake comments that AI can generate. Using AI technology is now possible to generate fake comments either on the commercial side fake comments of products or in political discourse fake comments about political matters in public discourse and to generate fake comments much more efficiently than if you only had humans writing them. So detecting such fake comments and weeding them out is an important technology for maintaining trust in comments that we might read online as well. Similar to the battles of spam versus anti-spam and fraud versus anti-fraud I think that for all of these issues there may be a competition on both sides for quite some time to come.

### 吴恩达对这些"善恶之战"持乐观态度——以垃圾邮件过滤器为例，希望反垃圾邮件有效运转的人远比少数试图发垃圾邮件的攻击者多得多，因此反垃圾邮件一方的资源远多于垃圾邮件发送者一方；社会在反垃圾、反欺诈获胜的情况下运转得更好，因此"善的一方"资源占优，最终会胜出——但这仍需要AI社区在未来很多年里付出大量努力

> 就垃圾邮件与反垃圾邮件、欺诈与反欺诈的战役，我对这些战役最终会怎样发展持乐观态度。因为——以垃圾邮件过滤器为例：希望确保垃圾邮件过滤器有效运转、反垃圾邮件系统发挥作用的人，远比少数试图把垃圾邮件发到你收件箱里的垃圾邮件发送者要多得多。正因为如此，反垃圾邮件一方的资源远远多过垃圾邮件发送者一方——因为社会在反垃圾和反欺诈系统运转良好的情况下，实际上运转得更好。因此，尽管 AI 社区在防御这些恶意用例方面还有大量工作要做，但因为社会在"只有 AI 的好用途"这种状态下才真正更好——我对"资源之间的平衡意味着善良一方会胜出"是乐观的。但这仍然需要 AI 社区在未来很多年里付出大量努力。接下来，AI 也在对发展中经济体产生重大影响——我们在下个视频来看看。
> Similar to the battles of spam versus anti-spam fraud versus anti-fraud I'm optimistic about how these battles will play out because if you take spam filters as an example there are a lot more people that are motivated to make sure spam filters do work the anti-spam does work then there are the smaller number of spammers trying to get their spam in to your inbox. Because of this there's a lot more resources on the side of anti-spam than on the side of spam because society actually functions better if anti-spam and anti-fraud works out well. Because of this even though the AI community still has a lot of work to do to defend against these adverse use cases because society is genuinely better off if we could have only good uses of AI I am optimistic that the balance of resources means that the side of good will prevail but it will still take a lot of work from the AI community over many years to come. Next AI is also having a big impact on developing economies let's take a look at that in the next video.


---

## 一、AI与发展中经济体——发展中经济体过去靠"经济阶梯"一步步往上爬（从农业到纺织到制造业到电子到汽车），但AI自动化可能把底下几级台阶敲掉；而AI也可以成为"弹跳床"，让一些国家跳过台阶直接跳到更高的位置

### 发展中经济体有一个可预测的"经济阶梯"：很多国家从低端农产品出口起步，然后转向低端纺织业（服装制造），随着人民财富和健康状况改善，再转向低端零部件制造、低端电子产品制造、高端电子产品制造，最后进入汽车制造等——这是一步步让公民获取技能、爬到更高财富水平的发展路径；但AI带来的问题是：阶梯底层很多环节恰恰最容易被AI自动化取代

> 每次发生像 AI 这样的重大技术颠覆，都会给我们一个重塑世界的机会。AI 是一项非常先进的技术，但它同时影响着发达经济体和发展中经济体。那么，我们怎么确保——即便 AI 创造出巨大的财富——它能让所有国家都受益呢？我们来看看。有一条相当可预测的路线图，几乎像一架梯子——很多发展中经济体都成功地爬上去了——来帮他们的公民掌握技能、爬到更高的财富水平。很多国家是从低端农产品出口开始的——出口农作物，然后转向低端纺织业，也就是服装制造。随着人民开始积累一些财富、变得更加健康，再转向低端零部件制造——比如做比较便宜的塑料零件，然后进入低端电子产品制造，再到高端电子产品制造，也许最终进入汽车制造等等。就是这样一个逐步递进的过程，发展中经济体借以帮助公民获取技能、成为发达经济体。AI 可能造成的一个问题是——这架阶梯上很多较低的梯级，恰恰特别容易被 AI 自动化所取代。比如，随着工厂变得越来越自动化，或者农业变得越来越自动化，一些发展中经济体的大量人口可能就不再那么需要、也就不再有那么多机会去踏上这架经济阶梯的底层梯级，然后从那里一步步往上爬了。
> Every time there's a major technological disruption, such as with AI, it gives us a chance to remake the world. AI is a very advanced technology, yet it's affecting both developed economies and developing economies. So how can we make sure that even as AI creates tremendous wealth, that it uplifts all nations? Let's take a look. There's been a fairly predictable roadmap, almost a ladder that many developing economies have successfully executed in order to help the citizens gain skills and climb to higher levels of wealth. A lot of nations started off with lower-end agricultural products, exporting crops, and then moved to low-end textile manufacturing, such as clothing manufacturing. And then as the population starts to gain a bit more wealth, become a bit more healthy, move on to low-end components manufacturing, such as making less expensive plastic pots, to then moving on to low-end electronics manufacturing, to higher-end electronics manufacturing, maybe to automotive manufacturing, and so on. And there's been this step-by-step progression by which developing economies can help their citizens gain skill and become developed economies. One of the problems that AI could cause is that a lot of the lower-end rungs on the ladder are particularly susceptible to automation through AI. For example, as factories become more automated, or as agriculture becomes more automated, there may be less need and therefore less opportunities for large members of the population of some of these developing economies to get onto the lower rungs of this economic ladder from which they would then climb up.

### 如果AI把经济阶梯的低层梯级敲掉了，那我们就有责任看看AI能不能也制造一个"弹跳床"——帮发展中经济体跳上去、甚至更快地到达更高梯级；历史上很多经济体已经展现了"跳代发展"：比如印度、中国等发展中经济体跳过了大面积铺设固定电话的阶段，直接跳到移动手机；类似地，很多发达经济体因为成熟的信用卡体系反而拖慢了移动支付的普及，而发展中经济体因为没有既有利益格局，移动支付推得更快

> 所以，如果 AI 正在通过"打了类固醇的自动化"把一些低层梯级敲掉，那我们就有责任去看看——AI 能不能也制造一个"弹跳床"，帮一些发展中经济体跳到弹跳床上，然后弹得更高、甚至更快地到达更高梯级。在更早期技术的崛起中，很多经济体已经展示了他们能够"跳代"发达经济体、直接跳到更先进的技术上。比如，在美国，我们大多数人以前都有座机——电话需要用电线连到墙上。正因为我们这么多人有座机，其实花了不少时间才过渡到无线移动手机。相比之下，包括印度、中国在内的很多发展中经济体，没有去费功夫铺设那么多的座机线路，而是直接跳到了移动手机。这就是一种"跳代"——发展中经济体直接越过更早代的技术，不去铺设那么多物理电缆给每户人家打电话，而是直接跳到移动手机。我们在移动支付领域也看到类似的情况：很多发达经济体有成熟的信用卡系统，这实际上反而拖慢了他们对移动手机支付的接受速度——而一些发展中经济体在信用卡行业本就没有顽固的既有势力。我还看到在线教育在发展中经济体的快速普及——那些国家还没有建完他们需要的所有实体学校和大学，而很多教育领导者和政府正在想办法更迅速地拥抱在线教育，相比之下一些发达经济体则因为已经建成了庞大的面对面教育物理基础设施而转变较慢。
> So if we're knocking out some of the lower rungs of the ladder through AI, through AI providing automation on steroids, then it is incumbent on us to see if AI can also create a trampoline to hope some developing economies jump onto the trampoline and bounce maybe even more quickly to the higher rungs of this ladder. With the rise of earlier ways of technology, many economies have shown that they can leap frog developed economies and jump straight to a more advanced technology. For example, here in the United States, most of us had landlines, phones that were connected via wire to the wall. And because so many of us had landline phones that actually took a while to transition to wireless mobile phones. In contrast, many developing economies, including India and China, but many others as well, didn't bother to build nearly as many landlines but skipped straight to mobile phones. So this was a leap frog where developing economies jumped straight over the earlier generation of technology and didn't bother to lay so many physical cables that every person tells and instead jumped straight to mobile phones. We're seeing a similar thing with mobile payments where many developed economies have a mature credit card system. And that actually is slowing down their adoption of mobile or cell phone payments compared to some developing economies which do not already have entrenched incumbents in the credit card industry. I'm also seeing rapid adoption of online education in developing economies in countries that have not yet built all the many, many physical schools and universities that they need. Many educational leaders and governments are seeking ways to more quickly embrace online education compared to some of the developed economies that have this built up physical infrastructure for in-person education.

### 全球AI格局：美国在生成式AI领域领先，中国紧随其后——英国、加拿大、印度、韩国、拉美等很多地方也有活跃的AI社区；但AI仍处于早期发展阶段，每个国家都还有很多机会和增长空间；吴恩达的建议是：与其每个国家都去和美国、中国在通用AI上竞争，不如聚焦AI来强化本国的垂直优势产业——比如一个国家如果在咖啡豆制造上很强，它就特别有资格去做"咖啡制造的AI"，这会进一步巩固它本来就擅长的东西

> 虽然发达经济体也在快速拥抱所有这一切技术，但发展中经济体的一个优势在于——没有被牢固的既有系统绑住手脚，也许有些领域可以有更快的发展。美国在生成式 AI 方面处于领先地位。紧随其后的是中国，拥有极其丰富的 AI 人才；英国、加拿大、印度、韩国、拉美很多地方以及其他很多国家，也有活跃的 AI 社区。但 AI 仍处于早期发展阶段，所以我认为每个国家都仍有很多机会、很大的增长空间。我对发展中经济体的建议是：聚焦 AI 来强化一个国家的垂直产业。比如，我认为今天大多数国家不应该尝试去自建一个网页搜索引擎——早就有很好的网页搜索引擎了，那是上个十年的比赛了。反过来，如果一个国家在某个垂直产业上特别强——比如说咖啡豆制造——那么这个国家就特别有资格去做"咖啡制造的 AI"，而构建咖啡制造的 AI 会进一步强化这个国家本就擅长的东西。所以，与其让每个国家都要在通用 AI 上跟美国和中国竞争，我更建议大多数国家用 AI 来强化自己擅长的、以及自己想在未来做的事。
> While developed economies are also rapidly embracing all of these technologies, one of the advantages of developing economies is that without an entrenched incumbent system, perhaps there are areas that they could build even faster. The United States, particularly when it comes to generative AI, is in the lead. Close behind is China which also has tremendous AI talent and the UK, Canada, India, Korea, many places in Latin America and many other countries also have vibrant AI communities. But we're still in the early development of AI so I think every country still has a lot of opportunities and a lot of growth ahead. My advice to developing economies is to focus on AI to strengthen a country's vertical industries. For example, I think most countries today should not try to build their own web search engine. They're already great web search engines and that was last decade's competition. Instead, if a country has a very strong vertical industry in say coffee bean manufacturing, then that country is actually uniquely qualified to do work in AI for coffee manufacturing and building AI for coffee manufacturing would even further strengthen what that country is already good at. So rather than needing for every country to compete with the US and China on AI in general, I would advise most countries to use AI to strengthen what that country is good at and what that country wants to do in the future.

### 公私合作（政府与企业联手）能有效加速垂直产业的AI发展——在医疗、交通（自动驾驶）、金融等高监管行业，政府如果能制定恰当法规来保护公民、同时又能让AI解决方案落地，就能推动更快的本地经济增长和技术发展；发展中经济体还应投资教育，因为AI还远未成熟，每个国家都仍有空间学习AI、培养自己的AI人才队伍，在这个AI赋能的世界中扮演重要角色；在技术颠覆的时代，领导力至关重要——无论是政府、企业还是教育层面的领导力

> 最后，公私合作——即政府和企业携起手来——能真正帮助加速一个垂直产业的 AI 发展。在从医疗到交通（比如自动驾驶汽车）再到金融的高监管行业中，我们想要某些结果、不想要某些结果——政府如果能深思熟虑地起草正确的法规来保护公民，同时又能让 AI 解决方案在这些行业落地，那就会在本国看到更快的本地经济增长和更快的技术发展。最后，发展中经济体应该投资教育，因为 AI 仍然非常不成熟。每个国家都仍然有很大空间去学习更多 AI 知识，甚至可能培养自己的 AI 人才队伍，在我们正在构建的这个 AI 驱动的世界中，以一个有意义的方式参与其中。在技术颠覆的时刻，领导力至关重要。在美国，我们曾经信任我们的政府能把人送上月球——而且成功了。随着 AI 的崛起，它在某些国家创造出了领导力的空间和需求——无论是在政府层面、企业层面还是教育层面——来带领国家进入 AI 时代，拥抱和采纳 AI，持续改善公民的生活，甚至也许还能持续改善全世界其他地方人们的生活。这个视频里我们简要地触及了 AI 与就业的问题。这是一个在各国被广泛讨论的重要话题。下一课来深入探究 AI 与就业。
> Finally, public private partnerships, meaning governments and corporations working together can really help accelerate a vertical industry's AI developments. In highly regulated sectors ranging from healthcare to transportation like self-driving cars to finance, there are certain outcomes that we want and certain outcomes that we don't want and governments that are thoughtful about drafting the right regulations to protect citizens while at the same time enabling the adoption of AI solutions to these industries will see faster local economic growth as well as faster technology development within their country. Finally, developing economies should invest in education because AI is still so immatural. There's still plenty of room for every nation to learn more about AI, maybe even build up its own AI workforce and participate in a significant way in this AI powered world that we're building. In moments of technological disruption, leadership matters. Here in the United States, we once trusted our government to put a man on the moon and it worked. With the rise of AI, it creates a space and in some countries a need for leadership, whether it's the government levels or in companies or in education to help the country enter the AI era and embrace and adopt AI to keep on lifting up its citizens and perhaps even keep on lifting up other people worldwide. In this video, we've touched briefly on the issue of AI and jobs. This is an important topic that is widely discussed in many countries right now. Let's go on to the next video to take a deeper look at AI and jobs.


---

## 一、AI与就业——麦肯锡估计到2030年AI自动化将替代4到8亿个工作岗位，但AI新创造的岗位数量可能更多；生成式AI的出现让以前"不太怕自动化"的写作沟通类工作也面临AI替代风险

### 麦肯锡全球研究院估计到2030年将有4至8亿个岗位被AI自动化替代——这是一个极其庞大的数字；但同一份报告也估计AI新创造的岗位数可能更大；高盛则给出了更保守的估计，认为到2033年会失去3亿个岗位——不同研究差异很大，说明不确定性仍然很高，但大多数专家都认为影响将是重大的；令人鼓舞的是很多研究都预计创造的新岗位数量很可能超过被替代的岗位

> AI 是"打了类固醇的自动化"。在现代 AI 崛起之前，自动化已经对大量岗位产生了巨大影响。随着 AI 的崛起，我们现在能自动化的东西突然比以前多了很多——而这也在加速对就业的影响。到底有多少岗位会被替代？有多少新岗位会被创造出来？我觉得目前还没有人能给出确切答案，但我们来看看一些研究，试着理解前方可能面临什么。麦肯锡全球研究院估计，到 2030 年将会有 4 到 8 亿个岗位被 AI 自动化替代——这是非常大的数字。但反过来，同一份报告也估计，AI 创造的岗位数量可能还会更大。其他研究给出的数字也确实有所差异——比如高盛的一项研究估计到 2033 年会损失更低的 3 亿个岗位，而非麦肯锡估计的 4 到 8 亿。所以关于 AI 对就业的影响还有相当多的不确定性，不过大多数人都同意影响将是重大的。但我觉得令人鼓舞的是——很多研究都估计，很有可能新创造的岗位会比被替代的更多。所以，未来人类仍有大量工作要做——即便很不幸地，某些行业的工人技能重塑需求可能非常大。而且我认为，未来的很多工作岗位，我们现在甚至都还没有名字——比如无人机交通优化师、3D 打印服装设计师，或者医疗健康领域的定制 DNA 药物设计师等等。所以尽管人们对 AI 替代就业感到担忧，但对很多新工作——甚至可能是比被替代的更多的新工作——被创造出来，我们还是可以抱有希望的。
> AI is automation on steroids. Before the rise of modern AI, automation had already had a huge impact on a lot of jobs. With the rise of AI, the set of things we can now automate is suddenly much bigger than before, and so this is also having an accelerating impact on jobs. How many jobs will be displaced? How many new jobs will be created? I don't think anyone has a firm answer to these questions yet, but let's take a look at some studies to try to understand what may be ahead. McKinsey Global Institute estimated that 400-800 million jobs will be displaced by AI automation by 2030. These are very large numbers. On the flip side though, the same report also estimates that the number of jobs created by AI may be even larger. Other studies do give numbers that can be a bit different. For example, a Goldman Sachs study estimates a lower 300 million jobs lost by 2033 rather than McKinsey's estimate of 400-800 million. So there's still quite a lot of uncertainty about the impact of AI on jobs, though most agree it will be significant. But I find it encouraging that many studies estimate quite possibly even more jobs being created and displaced. So there will be plenty of work for people to do in the future, even if unfortunately the need to reskill workers could be significant in some sectors. And I think many of the jobs of the future we may not even have names for yet, be it Drone Traffic Optimizer or 3D-printed clothing designer, or perhaps in healthcare we'll have custom DNA-based drug designers. So even though there is concern about AI-displacing jobs, there's also hope of many new jobs, maybe even more new jobs being created in the future.

### 估算岗位替代率的典型方法——把一个岗位拆解为所有任务，然后逐项评估每项任务被AI自动化的"可自动化程度"；如果一个岗位主要由高度可自动化的任务组成，那么这个岗位被替代的风险就更高；大多数AI工程师认为，比起"岗位"，用"任务"为单位来思考AI的应用更有意义

> 那你怎么估计有多少岗位可能被替代呢？这些研究通常采用的方法是：取一个岗位，分析组成这个岗位的各项任务。比如，你会分析一位放射科医生要做的所有任务，或者一位出租车司机要做的所有任务。然后，对每一项任务，估计它被 AI 自动化的难易程度。如果一个岗位主要由高度可自动化的任务组成，那么这个岗位被替代的风险就更高。大多数 AI 工程师觉得，以"任务"而不是以"人的岗位"为粒度来思考 AI 的应用更有意义。但这个框架让我们能利用 AI 自动化任务的能力，来估计有多少岗位可能被替代。
> Now you might wonder how do we estimate how many jobs are likely to be displaced. One typical way that these studies are carried out would be to take a job and think of the tasks that make up their job. For example, you might look at the tasks that a radiologist does, or look at all the tasks that a taxi driver does. And then for each of the tasks, estimate how amenable it is to automation through AI. And if a job comprises mainly tasks that are highly automatable, then the risk of the job being displaced will be higher. Most AI engineers find it more useful to think of AI being applied to tasks rather than to people's jobs. But this framework allows us to use AI's ability to automate tasks to estimate how many jobs are likely to be displaced.

### 麦肯锡研究了AI（包括生成式AI工具）对广泛岗位的影响，列出了到2033年AI自动化潜力最高的前10大职业群——包括办公室工作、机械安装维修、商业与法律专业工作、客户服务与销售等；值得注意的是，生成式AI极大地扩展了可被自动化的工作类型：它能生成听起来像人类写的文字、写共情邮件、与人类聊天，使得以前不太容易被自动化替代的写作沟通类工作现在也暴露在AI自动化之下——例如"办公室支持"和"法律工作"如果不考虑生成式AI，其自动化百分比会明显降低；前10大职业群雇佣了全球超过70%的劳动力——数量极其庞大

> 那么，哪些岗位最有可能或最不可能被 AI 和自动化替代呢？麦肯锡研究了 AI（包括生成式 AI 工具）对广泛岗位的影响。这张图改编自他们研究中的数据，展示了到 2033 年 AI 自动化潜力最高的前 10 个岗位。每个柱状条右边紧跟的数字——比如 87 或 82——是估计的这个岗位中能够被自动化的任务百分比。这个列表涵盖了麦肯锡称为"职业群"的广泛范围——包括办公室工作、机械安装与维修、商业与法律专业工作，以及客户服务与销售。这里特别值得注意的一点是：生成式 AI 对现在能够被 AI 自动化的岗位类型产生了巨大影响。这些工具生成人类般自然文字、书写共情邮件、与人类聊天的能力，把一些以前不太容易受自动化影响的岗位类别，新暴露在了 AI 自动化之下。事实上，如果不依赖生成式 AI 工具来看这些岗位的自动化潜力，你会发现涉及写作和沟通任务的职业——比如办公室支持和法律工作——其 AI 自动化潜力在加入生成式 AI 后有了显著提升。右边这一栏数字显示的是全球劳动力在各个职业群中的占比——加起来，这前 10 大职业雇佣了全球超过 70% 的劳动力，数量极其庞大。
> So which are the jobs that are most likely or least likely to be displaced through AI and automation? McKinsey looked at the impact of AI, including generative AI tools on a wide range of jobs. This figure, which is adapted from data presented in their study, presents the 10 jobs that have the most potential for AI automation by 2033. The number immediately to the right of each bar, like 87 or 82 here, is an estimate of the percentage of work tasks that may be automated for that job. This list includes a wide range of what McKinsey calls occupation groups, including office work, mechanical installation and repair, business and legal professional work, and customer services sales. One thing worth noting here is that generative AI has had a big impact on the types of jobs that can now be automated by AI. The ability of these tools to generate human sounding texts, to write empathetic emails, and to chat with humans has exposed new categories of jobs to AI automation that were previously less susceptible to automation. In fact, if you look at the automation potential of these jobs without generative AI tools, you see some significant increases in the potential for AI automation in the occupations that involve writing and communication tasks, like office support and legal work. The numbers in the right column here show how much of the global workforce is employed in each occupation group. Together, these top 10 occupations employ more than 70% of workers around the world, a huge number of people.

### 应对AI对就业冲击的三大方案——第一，"有条件基本收入"：相比"无条件基本收入"（政府无附带条件直接发现金），有条件基本收入在提供安全保障的同时，激励失业者不断学习、投资自我发展，通过结构化支持帮助他们重返工作岗位，既贡献于自己和家庭，也贡献于支付这一切的税收体系；第二，构建"终身学习型社会"——老模式是"大学四年，剩下四十年吃老本"，在当今快速变化的世界已经行不通了，即使大学毕业后也应该终身持续学习；第三，政治层面的方案——从激励新岗位创造、到立法确保公平待遇，社会需要找到正确的政策组合

> 那我们怎么帮助公民和国家来应对 AI 即将对就业产生的影响呢？这里有几个可能的方案。第一，有条件基本收入。你可能听说过"全民基本收入"——政府无任何附加条件直接给公民发钱。我认为人们确实应该有一个安全网，而对于有能力学习但失业的个人来说，我认为更有效的版本是"有条件基本收入"——我们确实给你安全网，但同时激励你不断学习、不断为自己的发展投资。通过提供一种结构来帮助那些能够学习的人真正做到持续学习，会增加这些人重返工作岗位的机会，让他们为自己、为家庭、为社会做贡献，也向支付这一切的税收体系做出贡献。第二，构建终身学习型社会。你现在正在上这门课这件事本身，就说明你可能已经是这个终身学习型社会的一员了。旧的受教育模式——去大学读四年，剩下四十年吃老本——在今天这个快速变化的世界已经行不通了。通过政府、公司和个人都意识到我们所有人都需要不断学习，会提高每个人更好应对的机会——即使有些岗位在消失，也能抓住正在被创造出来的新岗位。在未来，即使大学毕业后，我认为绝大多数人都应该终生持续不断学习。最后，政治上也在探索各种方案——从激励或帮助新岗位创造，到立法确保人得到公平对待。我希望社会能找到正确的政治方案，来应对 AI 即将对就业产生的冲击。
> How do we help citizens and nations navigate the coming impacts of AI on jobs? Here are some possible solutions. First, conditional basic income. You may have heard of universal basic income in which a government would pay citizens with no strings attached. I think people do deserve a safety net, and for individuals that are unemployed but are able to learn, I think a more effective version may be conditional basic income in which we do provide the safety net, but incentivize them to keep on learning and keep on investing in their own development. By providing a structure to help people that can learn do so, this will increase the odds that those individuals can reenter the workforce, contribute to themselves, their families, and to society, as well as to the tax wage that is paying for all this. Second, building a lifelong learning society. By virtue of your taking this course right now, you may already be part of this lifelong learning society. The old model of education where you go to college for four years and then coast for the remaining 40, that just doesn't work anymore in today's rapidly changing world. And through governments, companies, and individuals realizing that all of us need to keep on learning, this increases the odds that everyone will be better positioned, even as jobs may go away, to take advantage of the new jobs being created as well. In the future, even after completing college, I think most individuals should keep on learning throughout their whole lives. Finally, there are political solutions being explored as well. Everything from incentivizing or helping with new job creation to legislation to make sure that people are treated fairly. I hope that society will figure out the right political solutions to navigate the coming impact of AI on jobs as well.

### 如果有人想转行进入AI领域——一位刚入行的放射科住院医生问吴恩达："AI对放射科冲击这么大，我该不该辞职从头学AI？"吴恩达的回答是：你可以这样做，确实很多人成功转了；但更好的选择是"AI + 放射科"——把你在自己领域的专业知识和AI结合起来，你会在"放射科与AI的交叉地带"比绝大多数纯AI背景的人更有独特优势；总结就是：在你自己擅长的领域之上叠加AI，你才最有不可替代的价值

> 有一个我常被问到的问题是——"如果我想做 AI 方面的工作，我该怎么做？"最近有一位放射科住院医生——职业生涯刚开始的放射科医生——跑来问我："Andrew，我听到了很多关于 AI 对放射科冲击的讨论，我该不该退出这个职业，干脆去学 AI、转行做 AI？"我对他的回答是：不改行当然也可以。你当然可以辞职，从头开始学 AI——这完全是可以做到的，很多人也做到了。但有另外一种选择你也可以考虑——我对这位放射科住院医生说：考虑去做"AI 加放射科"的工作吧。因为你拥有放射科的知识，再补充学习 AI，你会在"放射科与 AI 的交叉领域"比绝大多数其他人都有更好的优势和条件。所以，如果你想做更多 AI 相关工作，在今天的世界完全可以通过在线课程和其他资源从零开始学 AI。但如果你把已有的专业能力利用起来，学一点 AI，做"你的领域加 AI"，那么你可能会有更独特的资格，去把 AI 应用到你早就擅长的领域，做出非常有价值的工作。希望这集内容能帮你应对 AI 即将对就业产生的冲击。咱们进入这门课的下一个视频，也是最后一个视频。
> One question of sometimes asked is, what should you do if you want to work in AI? Recently, a radiologist resident, a radiologist near the start of his career, he actually asked me, he said, hey Andrew, I'm hearing a lot about the coming impacts of AI on radiology. And he said, should I quit my profession and just learn AI and do AI instead? And my answer to him was no, you could do that. You can actually quit whatever you're doing and pick up AI from scratch. It is entirely possible to do that. Many people have done that. There's one other alternative that you could consider though, which is, I said to this radiology resident, consider doing work in AI plus radiology, because with your knowledge of radiology, if in addition you learn something about AI, you'd be better positioned to do work at the intersection of radiology and AI than most other people. So if you want to do more work in AI, it is possible in today's world to learn AI from scratch through online courses and other resources. But if you take whatever you are already knowledgeable in and learn some AI and do your area plus AI, then you might be more uniquely qualified to do very valuable work by applying AI to whatever area you are already an expert in. So I hope this video helps you navigate the coming impacts of AI and jobs. Let's go on to the next and final video of this course.


---

## 一、课程总结——四周时间，你从AI技术原理、AI项目构建到公司AI转型、AI社会影响，系统地完成了从"AI小白"到比大多数大公司CEO更懂AI的蜕变

### 第一周回顾：学习了AI技术基础——AI和机器学习是什么、监督学习（输入到输出的A-B映射）、数据科学以及数据如何驱动所有这些技术；更重要的是你看到了AI能做什么和不能做什么的实例，建立了对AI能力的真实认知

> 恭喜你来到这门课的最后一个视频。AI 是一种超能力——理解它，你就能做到这个星球上很少有人能做到的事。咱们来总结一下你在这门课中学到的东西。第一周，你学习了 AI 技术：什么是 AI、什么是机器学习？什么是监督学习——也就是学习"输入到输出"或"A 到 B"的映射。还学了什么是数据科学，以及数据如何为所有这些技术提供燃料。更重要的是，你看到了 AI 能做什么、不能做什么的实例。
> Congratulations on coming to the last video of this course. AI is a superpower and understanding it allows you to do things that very few people on the planet can. Let's summarize what you've seen in this course. In the first week, you learned about AI technology. What is AI and what is machine learning? What supervised learning? That is learning input to output or A to B mappings. As well as what is data science and how data feeds into all of these technologies. Importantly, you also saw examples of what AI can and cannot do.

### 第二周回顾：学习了构建AI项目的实际体验——包括机器学习项目的工作流程（收集数据、构建系统、部署上线）和数据科学项目的工作流程；还学会了做技术尽职调查来确保项目技术可行，以及做商业尽职调查来确保项目有价值——在真正投入一个AI项目之前必须两样都过关

> 第二周，你学到了做 AI 项目是一种什么感觉。你看到了机器学习项目的工作流程——收集数据、构建系统、部署上线——也看到了数据科学项目的工作流程。你还学会了怎么去做技术尽职调查来确保项目可行，以及做商业尽职调查来确保项目有价值——以此来决定是否应该投入到一个具体的 AI 项目中去。
> In the second week, you learned what it feels like to build an AI project. You saw the workflow of machine learning projects, of collecting data, building a system and deploying it, as well as the workflow of data science projects. And you also learned about carrying out technical diligence to make sure a project is feasible, together with business diligence to make sure that the project is valuable before you commit to taking on a specific AI project.

### 第三周回顾：学习了AI项目如何融入公司整体环境——包括智能音箱、自动驾驶汽车等复杂AI产品的案例，大型AI团队的各类角色和职责，以及最重要的《AI转型指南》——帮助公司成为"优秀AI公司"的五步路线图

> 第三周，你学了这些 AI 项目如何在你的公司环境里找到自己的位置。你看到了复杂 AI 产品的案例——比如智能音箱、自动驾驶汽车；了解了大型 AI 团队中的各类角色和职责。你还看到了《AI 转型指南》——帮助公司成为"优秀 AI 公司"的五步路线图。我希望这前三周的内容能帮你去构思 AI 项目，或者去思考怎么在你的公司或组织里用上 AI。
> In the third week, you learned how such AI projects could fit in the context of your company. You saw examples of complex AI products, such as a smart speaker or self-driving car, the roles and responsibilities of large AI teams. And you also saw the AI Transformation Playbook, the five-step playbook for helping a company become a great AI company. I hope these materials throughout these first three weeks can help you brainstorm AI projects or think about how you might be able to use AI in your company or in your organization.

### 第四周回顾：学习了AI与社会——AI在技术局限之外的更多局限（可解释性、偏见歧视、对抗性攻击），以及AI如何影响发展中经济体和全球就业；完成这四周的内容后，你对AI的理解和规划AI未来的能力已经超过了大多数大公司CEO，希望你也能为那些正在寻找方向的人提供指引

> 第四周，你学习了 AI 与社会。你看到了 AI 在纯技术局限之外的其他局限，也学习了 AI 如何影响发展中经济体和全球的就业。在这四周里你学到了很多。但 AI 是一个复杂的主题，所以我希望你继续学下去——不管是上更多的在线课程、通过 Coursera 或 deeplearning.ai、还是读书、看博客、或者仅仅是跟朋友交流。如果你哪天想亲自上手构建 AI 技术，现在学编程和学会怎么使用这些资源来实现 AI 技术，比以往任何时候都容易了。
> In this week, week four, you learned about AI and society. You saw some of the limitations of AI beyond just technical limitations and also learned about how AI is affecting developing economies and jobs worldwide. You've learned a lot in these four weeks, but AI is a complex topic. So I hope you will keep on learning, whether through additional online courses, through Coursera or deeplearning.ai or books or blogs or just by talking to friends. If you ever want to try your hand at building AI technology, it's not easier than ever to learn to code and learn how to implement AI technology through these resources.

### 如果想持续获取AI资讯，可以在deeplearning.ai网站上注册邮件列表，吴恩达会不定期发送有用的AI信息；最后，吴恩达向你表达了感谢——他知道大家都有学业或工作、朋友和家庭要忙，感谢你花这么多时间跟他一起学习这些关于AI技术和影响的重要课题

> 如果你想继续获取 AI 方面的信息，你也可以注册 deeplearning.ai 的邮件列表——去 deeplearning.ai 网站注册就可以。我会通过这个邮件列表不定期发一些有用的 AI 信息给你。恭喜你完成这门课程。你现在对 AI 的理解、以及规划 AI 崛起的能力，已经远超大多数大公司的 CEO。所以我希望你也能够为其他正在努力寻找方向的人提供指引。最后，我想对你说：非常感谢你来上这门课。我知道你有自己的学业或工作要忙，有朋友和家庭要陪——我很感激你花了这么多时间跟我在一起，在这门课里学习这些关于 AI 技术和 AI 影响的复杂议题。所以，非常感谢你在这门课里付出的时间和努力。
> If you'd like to keep on receiving information about AI, you can also sign up for the deeplearning.ai mailing list by going to the deeplearning.ai website and signing up there. I'll occasionally send you useful information about AI through that mailing list. Congratulations on finishing this course. You're now significantly ahead of many large company CEOs in your understanding of AI and your ability to plan for the rise of AI. So I hope that you'll provide leadership to others as well that are trying to navigate these issues. Lastly, I want to say to you, thank you very much for taking this course. I know that you're busy with your own work or school and friends and family, and I'm grateful that you spend so much time with me and in this course learning these complex issues relating to both the technology and the impact of AI. So thank you very much for both the time and the effort you put into this course.
